Bifurcations in Lagrangian systems and geodesics I 0 0 footnotetext: 2020 Mathematics Subject Classification . Primary 37J20, 34C23, 53C22. Key words and phrases. Bifurcation, Lagrangian system, Euler-Lagrange curves, focal point.
Guangcun Lu

Abstract




Abstract
This paper is Part I of a two-part series. We investigate bifurcation phenomena in Lagrangian systems with
various boundary conditions and constraints, focusing on the interplay between Morse theory and the existence of multiple solutions
through three principal configurations: Lagrangian trajectories connecting two submanifolds or with endpoints related by an isometry,
and brake orbits in Lagrangian systems. For each configuration, we establish necessary and sufficient conditions
for bifurcation using Morse index and nullity techniques, including classification of Rabinowitz-type alternative bifurcation scenarios.
For Euler-Lagrange curves emanating perpendicularly from a submanifold, we develop a unified Morse-theoretic framework that rigorously connects
geometric focal structure (e.g., conjugate points) and analytic bifurcation behavior (e.g., solution branching patterns).

1
Introduction and overview of the series

1.1
Background and motivation

The study of bifurcation phenomena in variational problems lies at the heart of nonlinear analysis and differential geometry. A fundamental question is to understand how families of solutions to Euler-Lagrange equations change their qualitative behavior as a parameter varies. Classical results, such as the Lyapunov-Schmidt reduction and the celebrated work of Rabinowitz on potential operators, provide abstract frameworks for analyzing such phenomena. In the geometric context, the Morse index theorem elegantly relates the index of a geodesic to its conjugate points, offering a powerful tool for understanding the stability and multiplicity of geodesics in Riemannian geometry.

However, extending this elegant theory to more general Lagrangian systems with constraints and various boundary conditions presents significant challenges. While substantial progress has been made for specific cases–such as periodic orbits, geodesics between fixed points, or brake orbits–a
unified framework
that systematically handles configurations like trajectories connecting two submanifolds, endpoints constrained by an isometry, or generalized periodic solutions has remained less developed. This paper aims to fill this gap by establishing a comprehensive Morse-theoretic approach to bifurcation theory for a broad class of Lagrangian boundary value problems.

Our work is motivated by the desire to create a common foundation that not only recovers known results in differential geometry (e.g., bifurcation of geodesics) as special cases but also applies to non-geometric, time-dependent Lagrangian systems. The key insight is to leverage the interplay between the analytic data (Morse index and nullity) and the geometric structure of the problem to derive sharp necessary and sufficient conditions for bifurcation.

1.2
Main objectives and structure of the series

This two-part series has the following primary objectives:

1.
Part I (Current Paper):
To develop a unified Morse-theoretic framework for analyzing bifurcations in Lagrangian systems under three general configurations:

•

Trajectories connecting two submanifolds.

•

Trajectories with endpoints related by an isometry (including generalized periodic solutions).

•

Brake orbits (symmetric periodic trajectories).

For each configuration, we establish necessary and sufficient conditions for bifurcation, prove existence theorems, and classify the possible bifurcation scenarios following the tradition of Rabinowitz’s alternatives.

2.
Part II (companion paper):
To apply the framework developed in Part I to autonomous Lagrangian systems and delve deeper into bifurcation phenomena for geodesics in both Finsler and Riemannian settings, extending the results to broader geometric variational problems.

The core technical questions we address in this Part I are precisely formulated in Section 1.4 (see Questions (1)-(3)). The answers, summarized in Theorems 1.4–1.24, provide a complete characterization of bifurcation points in terms of changes in the Morse index and nullity along the trivial branch of solutions.

1.3
Relation to existing literature

Our work builds upon and extends several key strands of research. The abstract bifurcation theory for potential operators was pioneered by Rabinowitz
[
30
]
. In the context of geodesics, the connection between conjugate points and the Morse index is classical
[
27
,
25
]
. More recent contributions by authors such as Duistermaat
[
6
]
(whose index theorem we rely upon in Theorem
3.14
), and Piccione et al.
[
29
]
on the Maslov index and bifurcation, have significantly advanced the field.

The novelty of our approach lies in its systematicity and generality. While previous works often focused on specific settings, we provide a unified treatment that encompasses a wide range of boundary conditions. The techniques employed here combine the abstract critical point theory from

[
19
,
20
,
22
,
21
]
with careful geometric reductions to Euclidean spaces, allowing us to handle the technical complexities arising from the manifold setting and constraints.

1.4
Basic assumptions, conventions, and
statement of the research problems

Throughout,
\mathbb{N}
,
\mathbb{Z}
, and
\mathbb{R}
denote, respectively, the natural numbers, integers, and real numbers. The symbol
\tau
is a fixed positive number, and
\Lambda
denotes the parameter space (typically a real interval or a topological space).

Assumption 1.0

(Basic assumptions and conventions).

Let
M
be a
n
-dimensional, connected
C^{7}
submanifold of
{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
.
Its tangent bundle
TM
is a
C^{6}
-smooth manifold of dimension
2n
,
whose points are denoted by
(x,v)
, with
x\in M
and
v\in T_{x}M
.
The bundle projection
\pi:TM\to M,\,(x,v)\mapsto x
is
C^{6}
.
Let
g
be a
C^{6}
Riemannian metric and
\mathbb{I}_{g}
a
C^{7}
isometry on
(M,g)
,
i.e.,
\mathbb{I}_{g}:M\to M
is
C^{7}
and satisfies
g((\mathbb{I}_{g})_{\ast}(u),(\mathbb{I}_{g})_{\ast}(v))=g(u,v)

for all
u,v\in TM
.
(Thus the Christoffel symbols
\Gamma^{i}_{jk}
and the exponential map
\exp:TM\to M
are
C^{5}
.)

Let
{\bf N}
be a submanifold of
M\times M
(specifically, either a product of two submanifolds of
M
or the graph of a Riemannian isometry of
(M,g)
), and let
L:\Lambda\times[0,\tau]\times TM\to\mathbb{R}
be as in Assumption
1.1
.
The current manuscript focuses on bifurcations research for the following
Lagrangian boundary value problem:

\left.\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{d}{dt}\big(\partial_{v}L_{\lambda}(t,\gamma(t),\dot{\gamma}(t))\big)-\partial_{q}L_{\lambda}(t,\gamma(t),\dot{\gamma}(t))=0\;\;\forall t\in[0,\tau],\\
(\gamma(0),\gamma(\tau))\in{\bf N}\quad\hbox{and}\\
\partial_{v}L_{\lambda}(0,\gamma(0),\dot{\gamma}(0))[v_{0}]=\partial_{v}L_{\lambda}(\tau,\gamma(\tau),\dot{\gamma}(\tau))[v_{1}]\;\;\forall(v_{0},v_{1})\in T_{(\gamma(0),\gamma(\tau))}{\bf N}\end{array}\right\}

(1.1)

with respect to a continuous family
\{\gamma_{\lambda}\,|\,\lambda\in\Lambda\}
of solutions of this problem,
where the superposed dot denotes the derivative with respect to
t
.
If every neighborhood of
(\mu,\gamma_{\mu})
in
\Lambda\times C^{1}([0,\tau];{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
contains a point
(\lambda,\alpha_{\lambda})\notin\{(\lambda,\gamma_{\lambda})\,|\,\lambda\in\Lambda\}

satisfying (
1.1
) we say
(\mu,\gamma_{\mu})
to be a
bifurcation point
of (
1.1
)
in
\Lambda\times C^{1}([0,\tau];{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
with respect to the trivial branch
\{(\lambda,\gamma_{\lambda})\,|\,\lambda\in\Lambda\}
.

Observe that solutions of (
1.1
) correspond to critical points of

C^{2}
functionals defined by

\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}(\gamma)=\int^{\tau}_{0}L_{\lambda}(t,\gamma(t),\dot{\gamma}(t))dt

(1.2)

on the Banach manifold

C^{1}_{\bf N}([0,\tau];M)=\{\gamma\in C^{1}([0,\tau];M)\,|\,(\gamma(0),\gamma(\tau))\in{\bf N}\}.

(1.3)

Following
[
6
, p.175]
, we define the Morse index
m^{-}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda},\gamma_{\lambda})
and nullity

m^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda},\gamma_{\lambda})
of
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}
at

\gamma_{\lambda}\in C^{1}_{\bf N}([0,\tau];M)
, respectively, as

\displaystyle m^{-}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda},\gamma_{\lambda})

\displaystyle=\sup\{\dim L\mid L\subset T_{\gamma_{\lambda}}C^{1}_{\bf N}([0,\tau];M)\text{ is a linear subspace such that}

\displaystyle\qquad D^{2}\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}(\gamma_{\lambda})\text{ is negative definite on }L\bigr\},

(1.4)

\displaystyle m^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda},\gamma_{\lambda})

\displaystyle=\dim\ker\bigl(D^{2}\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}(\gamma_{\lambda})\bigr),

(1.5)

where
T_{\gamma_{\lambda}}C^{1}_{\bf N}([0,\tau];M)
is
the tangent space of
C^{1}_{\bf N}([0,\tau];M)
at
\gamma_{\lambda}
, i.e.,
the Banach space of all
C^{1}
-sections
\xi
of the pullback bundle
\gamma_{\lambda}^{*}TM

that satisfy the boundary condition
(\xi(0),\xi(\tau))\in T_{(\gamma_{\lambda}(0),\gamma_{\lambda}(\tau))}\mathbf{N}
.
Using
m^{-}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda},\gamma_{\lambda})
and
m^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda},\gamma_{\lambda})
,
which depend only on the second variation

D^{2}\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}(\gamma_{\lambda})

of the functional
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}
at

\gamma_{\lambda}\in C^{1}_{\bf N}([0,\tau];M)
(cf.
[
3
, §5]

for the precise expression of

D^{2}\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}(\gamma_{\lambda})
in terms of covariant derivatives),
we address the following questions:

(1)

Under what conditions there is a bifurcation point
(\mu,\gamma_{\mu})
in the above sense?

(2)

What are the necessary (resp. sufficient) condition for a given point
(\mu,\gamma_{\mu})
to be a bifurcation point in the above sense?

(3)

How is the solutions of (
1.1
) distributed near a bifurcation point
(\mu,\gamma_{\mu})
as above ?

Roughly speaking, our answers are:

(a)

If
\Lambda
is path-connected and there exist two points
\lambda^{+},\lambda^{-}\in\Lambda

for which the Morse index intervals

[m^{-}({\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}}),m^{-}({\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})+m^{0}({\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})]

and

[m^{-}({\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}}),m^{-}({\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})+m^{0}({\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})]

are disjoint, and either

m^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})=0
or
m^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})=0
,
then there exists a point
\mu
on any path connecting
\lambda^{+}
to
\lambda^{-}
in
\Lambda

such that
(\mu,\gamma_{\mu})
is a bifurcation point for the family as described as above.

(b)

If
\Lambda
is first countable and there exist two points
\lambda^{+},\lambda^{-}\in\Lambda
in any neighborhood of some
\mu\in\Lambda

satisfying the properties as in (a), then
(\mu,\gamma_{\mu})
is a bifurcation point. Conversely, for
a bifurcation point
(\mu,\gamma_{\mu})
it must hold that
m^{0}({\mathcal{E}}_{\mu},\gamma_{\mu})>0
.

(c)

If
\Lambda
is a real interval,
\mu\in{\rm Int}(\Lambda)
, then the solutions of (
1.1
)
near a bifurcation point
(\mu,\gamma_{\mu})
have alternative bifurcations of Rabinowitz’s type (as in
[
30
]
) provided that

m^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda},\gamma_{\lambda})=0
for each
\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\{\mu\}
near
\mu
, and

m^{-}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda},\gamma_{\lambda})
take, respectively, values
m^{-}(\mathcal{E}_{\mu},\gamma_{\mu})
and

m^{-}(\mathcal{E}_{\mu},\gamma_{\mu})+m^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{\mu},\gamma_{\mu})

as
\lambda\in\Lambda
varies in two deleted half neighborhoods of
\mu
.

The precise assumptions and main results are organized in the following three subsections.
The proofs are built upon the abstract theory from
[
19
,
21
]
, combined with techniques in Appendix A of
[
21
]
.
As an application, Theorem
1.9
establishes a rigorous one-to-one correspondence between focal points and bifurcation phenomena along Euler-Lagrange curves of (
1.15
), providing a complete classification of branching behaviors near critical instants.

1.5
Bifurcations of Lagrangian system trajectories connecting two submanifolds

Assumption 1.1
.

Let
(M,g)
be as in Assumption 1.0 in Introduction.
For a real
\tau>0
and a topological space
\Lambda
,
let
L:\Lambda\times[0,\tau]\times TM\to{\mathbb{R}}
be a continuous function such that
for each
C^{3}
chart
\alpha:U_{\alpha}\to\alpha(U_{\alpha})\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
and the induced bundle
chart
T\alpha:TM|_{U_{\alpha}}\to\alpha(U_{\alpha})\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
the function

L^{\alpha}:\Lambda\times[0,\tau]\times\alpha(U_{\alpha})\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R},\;(\lambda,t,q,v)\mapsto L(\lambda,t,(T\alpha)^{-1}(q,v))

is
C^{2}
with respect to
(t,q,v)
and strictly convex with respect to
v
, and
all its partial derivatives also depend continuously on
(\lambda,t,q,v)
.
Let
S_{0}
and
S_{1}
be two boundaryless and connected submanifolds of
M
of dimensions less than
\dim M
.

By
[
28
, Theorem 4.2]
, for each integer
1\leq i\leq 4
,
C^{i}([0,\tau];M)
is
a
C^{5-i}
Banach manifold modeled on the Banach space
C^{i}([0,\tau];{\mathbb{R}}^{n})
with the tangent space

T_{\gamma}C^{i}([0,\tau];M)=C^{i}(\gamma^{\ast}TM)=\{\xi\in C^{i}([0,\tau];\mathbb{R}^{N})\,|\,\xi(t)\in T_{\gamma(t)}M\;\forall t\}

(1.6)

at
\gamma\in C^{i}([0,\tau];M)
.
Thus

C^{1}_{S_{0}\times S_{1}}([0,\tau];M):=\left\{\gamma\in C^{1}([0,\tau];M)\,|\,(\gamma(0),\gamma(\tau))\in S_{0}\times S_{1}\right\}

(1.7)

is a
C^{4}
Banach submanifold of
C^{1}([0,\tau];M)
.
Its tangent space at
\gamma\in C^{1}_{S_{0}\times S_{1}}([0,\tau];M)
is

C^{1}_{S_{0}\times S_{1}}(\gamma^{\ast}TM):=\left\{\xi\in C^{1}(\gamma^{\ast}TM)\,|\,(\xi(0),\xi(\tau))\in T_{(\gamma(0),\gamma(1))}(S_{0}\times S_{1})\right\},

(1.8)

which is dense in the Hilbert subspace

W^{1,2}_{S_{0}\times S_{1}}(\gamma^{\ast}TM):=\left\{\xi\in W^{1,2}(\gamma^{\ast}TM)\,|\,(\xi(0),\xi(\tau))\in T_{(\gamma(0),\gamma(1))}(S_{0}\times S_{1})\right\}

(1.9)

of
W^{1,2}(\gamma^{\ast}TM)
(consisting of
all
W^{1,2}
-sections of the pull-back bundle
\gamma^{\ast}TM\to[0,\tau]
) with inner product
given by

\langle\xi,\eta\rangle_{1,2}=\int^{\tau}_{0}\langle\xi(t),\eta(t)\rangle_{g}dt+\int^{\tau}_{0}\langle\frac{D\xi}{dt}(t),\frac{D\eta}{dt}(t)\rangle_{g}dt,

(1.10)

where
\frac{D\xi}{dt}
is the
W^{1,2}
covariant
derivative of
\xi
along
\gamma
,
defined via the Levi-Civita connection of the metric
g

in (
A.2
).
Hereafter
\langle u,v\rangle_{g}=g(u,v)
for
u,v\in TM
.

For each
\lambda\in\Lambda
, as in the proof of the first claim in
[
20
, Proposition 4.2]
we get that

\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}:C^{1}_{S_{0}\times S_{1}}([0,\tau];M)\to\mathbb{R},\;\gamma\mapsto\int^{\tau}_{0}L_{\lambda}(t,\gamma(t),\dot{\gamma}(t))dt

(1.11)

is a
C^{2}
functional.
A path
\gamma_{0}\in C^{1}_{S_{0}\times S_{1}}([0,\tau];M)
is a critical point of
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}

if and only if it belongs to
C^{2}([0,\tau];M)
and satisfies the Euler-Lagrange equation

\frac{d}{dt}\big(\partial_{v}L_{\lambda}(t,\gamma(t),\dot{\gamma}(t))\big)-\partial_{q}L_{\lambda}(t,\gamma(t),\dot{\gamma}(t))=0\;\forall t\in[0,\tau]

(1.12)

and the boundary condition

\left.\begin{array}[]{ll}(\gamma(0),\gamma(\tau))\in S_{0}\times S_{1}\quad\hbox{and}\\
\partial_{v}L_{\lambda}(0,\gamma(0),\dot{\gamma}(0))[v_{0}]=0\quad\forall v_{0}\in T_{\gamma(0)}S_{0},\\
\partial_{v}L_{\lambda}(\tau,\gamma(\tau),\dot{\gamma}(\tau))[v_{1}]=0\quad\forall v_{1}\in T_{\gamma(\tau)}S_{1}.\end{array}\right\}

(1.13)

By
[
6
]
, the second-order differential
D^{2}\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}(\gamma_{0})
of
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}
at such a critical point
\gamma_{0}

can be extended into a continuous symmetric bilinear form on
W^{1,2}_{S_{0}\times S_{1}}(\gamma^{\ast}TM)

with finite Morse index and nullity

m^{-}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda},\gamma_{0})\quad\hbox{and}\quad m^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda},\gamma_{0}),

(1.14)

which coincide with those defined by (
1.4
) and (
1.5
)
for
\mathbf{N}=S_{0}\times S_{1}
, respectively.

Assumption 1.2
.

Under Assumption
1.1
, for each
\lambda\in\Lambda
let

\gamma_{\lambda}\in C^{2}([0,\tau];M)
satisfy (
1.12
)-(
1.13
).
It is also assumed that
\Lambda\times[0,\tau]\ni(\lambda,t)\mapsto\gamma_{\lambda}(t)\in M
and

\Lambda\times[0,\tau]\ni(\lambda,t)\mapsto\dot{\gamma}_{\lambda}(t)\in TM
are continuous,
that is, for any
C^{2}
coordinate chart
\phi:W\to\phi(W)\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
, maps

(\lambda,t)\mapsto(\phi\circ\gamma_{\lambda})(t),\quad(\lambda,t)\mapsto\frac{d}{dt}(\phi\circ\gamma_{\lambda})(t)

are continuous.

Definition 1.3
.

Let
X=C^{1}_{S_{0}\times S_{1}}([0,\tau];M)
(or
C^{2}_{S_{0}\times S_{1}}([0,\tau];M)
).
For
\mu\in\Lambda
,
we call
(\mu,\gamma_{\mu})
a
bifurcation point
of the problem (
1.12
)–(
1.13
)
in
\Lambda\times X
with respect to the branch
\{(\lambda,\gamma_{\lambda})\,|\,\lambda\in\Lambda\}

if there exists a point
(\lambda_{0},\gamma_{0})
in any neighborhood of
(\mu,\gamma_{\mu})
in
\Lambda\times X

such that
\gamma_{0}\neq\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}

is a solution of (
1.12
)–(
1.13
) with
\lambda=\lambda_{0}
.
Moreover,
(\mu,\gamma_{\mu})
is said to be a
bifurcation point along sequences
of the problem (
1.12
)–(
1.13
)
in
\Lambda\times X
with respect to the branch
\{(\lambda,\gamma_{\lambda})\,|\,\lambda\in\Lambda\}

if there exists a sequence
\{(\lambda_{k},\gamma^{k})\}_{k\geq 1}
in
\Lambda\times X
,
converging to
(\mu,\gamma_{\mu})
such that each
\gamma^{k}\neq\gamma_{\lambda_{k}}

is a solution of (
1.12
)–(
1.13
) with
\lambda=\lambda_{k}
,
k=1,2,\cdots
.
(These two notions are equivalent if
\Lambda
is first countable.)

Recall that an isolated critical point
p
of a
C^{1}
-functional
f
on a Banach manifold
\mathcal{M}

is called
homological visible
if there exists a nonzero critical group
C_{m}(f,p;\mathbf{K})
for some
Abel group
\mathbf{K}
.

Theorem 1.4
.

Let Assumptions
1.1
,
1.2
be satisfied, and
\mu\in\Lambda
be such that

1
1

1

This assumption is to guarantee the existence of a Riemannian metric
g
on
M
such that

S_{0}
(resp.
S_{1}
) is totally geodesic near
\gamma_{\mu}(0)
(resp.
\gamma_{\mu}(\tau)
)
when we reduce the problems to Euclidean spaces in Section
3.1.1
.
Therefore it is not needed if
M
is an open subset in Euclidean spaces and
S_{0}
and
S_{1}
are linear subspaces.
Actually, when
\gamma_{\mu}(0)=\gamma_{\mu}(\tau)
we only need a weaker condition that there exists a coordinate chart

(U,\varphi)
around this point on
M
such that
\varphi(S_{0}\cap S_{1}\cap U)
is the intersection
\varphi(U)
of
the union of two linear subspaces in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

\gamma_{\mu}(0)\neq\gamma_{\mu}(\tau)

in the case
\dim S_{0}>0
and
\dim S_{1}>0
.

(I)

(
Necessary condition
):

Suppose that
(\mu,\gamma_{\mu})
is a bifurcation point along sequences of the problem (
1.12
)–(
1.13
)
with respect to the branch
\{(\lambda,\gamma_{\lambda})\,|\,\lambda\in\Lambda\}
in
\Lambda\times C^{1}_{S_{0}\times S_{1}}([0,\tau];M)
.
Then
m^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{\mu},\gamma_{\mu})>0
.

(II)

(
Sufficient condition
):

Suppose that
\Lambda
is first countable and that there exist two sequences in
\Lambda
converging to
\mu
,
(\lambda_{k}^{-})
and

(\lambda_{k}^{+})
, such that one of the following conditions is satisfied:

(II.1)

For each
k\in\mathbb{N}
, either
\gamma_{\lambda^{+}_{k}}
is not an isolated critical point of
{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}_{k}}
,
or
\gamma_{\lambda^{-}_{k}}
is not an isolated critical point of
{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}_{k}}
,
or
\gamma_{\lambda^{+}_{k}}

(
resp.
\gamma_{\lambda^{-}_{k}}
)

is an isolated critical point of
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda^{+}_{k}}

(

\mathcal{E}_{\lambda^{-}_{k}}
)
and

C_{m}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda^{+}_{k}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}_{k}};{\bf K})

are not isomorphic for some Abel group

and some

.

(II.2)

For each
k\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists

such that
\gamma_{\lambda}
is an either non-isolated or homological visible critical point of

\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}
, and

\left.\begin{array}[]{ll}&[m^{-}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda_{k}^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}_{k}}),m^{-}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda_{k}^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}_{k}})+m^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda_{k}^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}_{k}})]\\
&\cap[m^{-}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}_{k}}),m^{-}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}_{k}})+m^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}_{k}})]=\emptyset.\end{array}\right\}

[formula]

(II.3)

For each
k\in\mathbb{N}
, (
\ast_{k}
) holds true,
and either
m^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda_{k}^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}_{k}})=0
or
m^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}_{k}})=0
.

Then there exists a sequence
\{(\lambda_{k},\gamma^{k})\}_{k\geq 1}
in

\hat{\Lambda}\times C^{2}_{S_{0}\times S_{1}}([0,\tau];M)
converging to

(\mu,\gamma_{\mu})
such that each
\gamma^{k}\neq\gamma_{\lambda_{k}}
is a solution of the problem
(
1.12
)–(
1.13
) with
\lambda=\lambda_{k}
,

k=1,2,\cdots
, where

\hat{\Lambda}=\{\mu,\lambda^{+}_{k},\lambda^{-}_{k}\,|\,k\in\mathbb{N}\}
.
In particular,
(\mu,\gamma_{\mu})
is a bifurcation point of the problem
(
1.12
)–(
1.13
) in
\hat{\Lambda}\times C^{2}_{S_{0}\times S_{1}}([0,\tau];M)
with respect to the branch
\{(\lambda,\gamma_{\lambda})\,|\,\lambda\in\hat{\Lambda}\}

(
and so
\{(\lambda,\gamma_{\lambda})\,|\,\lambda\in\Lambda\}
)
.

Theorem 1.5

(
Existence for bifurcations
)
.

Let Assumptions
1.1
,
1.2
be satisfied,
and let
\Lambda
be path-connected. Suppose that
there exist two points
\lambda^{+},\lambda^{-}\in\Lambda
such that
one of the following conditions is satisfied:

(i)

Either
\gamma_{\lambda^{+}}
is not an isolated critical point of
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda^{+}}
,
or
\gamma_{\lambda^{-}}
is not an isolated critical point of
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda^{-}}
,
or
\gamma_{\lambda^{+}}

(
resp.
\gamma_{\lambda^{-}}
)
is an isolated critical point of

\mathcal{E}_{\lambda^{+}}

(
resp.
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda^{-}}
)
and

C_{m}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}};{\bf K})
and
C_{m}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}};{\bf K})
are not isomorphic for some Abel group
{\bf K}
and some
m\in\mathbb{Z}
.

(ii)

[m^{-}({\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}}),m^{-}({\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})+m^{0}({\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})]\cap[m^{-}({\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}}),m^{-}({\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})+m^{0}({\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})]\\
=\emptyset
,
and there exists
\lambda\in\{\lambda^{+},\lambda^{-}\}
such that
\gamma_{\lambda}
is an either non-isolated or homological visible critical point of

\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}
.

(iii)

[m^{-}({\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}}),m^{-}({\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})+m^{0}({\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})]\cap[m^{-}({\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}}),m^{-}({\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})+m^{0}({\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})]\\
=\emptyset
,
and either
m^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})=0
or
m^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})=0
.

Then for any path
\alpha:[0,1]\to\Lambda
connecting
\lambda^{+}
to
\lambda^{-}
such that
\gamma_{\alpha(s)}(0)\neq\gamma_{\alpha(s)}(\tau)

for any
s\in[0,1]
in the case
\dim S_{0}>0
and
\dim S_{1}>0
, there exists
a sequence
(\lambda_{k})\subset\alpha([0,1])
converging to some
\mu\in\alpha([0,1])
, and
solutions
\gamma^{k}\neq\gamma_{\lambda_{k}}
of the problem (
1.12
)–(
1.13
)
with
\lambda=\lambda_{k}
,
k=1,2,\cdots
, such that
\|\gamma^{k}-\gamma_{\lambda_{k}}\|_{C^{2}([0,\tau];\mathbb{R}^{N})}\to 0

as
k\to\infty
.
(
In particular,

(\mu,\gamma_{\mu})
is a bifurcation point along sequences of the problem (
1.12
)–(
1.13
) in

\Lambda\times C^{2}_{S_{0}\times S_{1}}([0,\tau];M)
with respect to the branch
\{(\lambda,\gamma_{\lambda})\,|\,\lambda\in\Lambda\}
.
)

Moreover,
\mu
is not equal to
\lambda^{+}

(
resp.
\lambda^{-}
)
if
m^{0}_{\tau}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})=0

(
resp.
m^{0}_{\tau}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})=0
)
.

Theorem 1.6

(
Alternative bifurcations of Rabinowitz’s type
)
.

Under Assumptions
1.1
,
1.2
with
\Lambda
being a real interval,
let
\mu\in{\rm Int}(\Lambda)
satisfy
\gamma_{\mu}(0)\neq\gamma_{\mu}(\tau)

(
if
\dim S_{0}>0
and
\dim S_{1}>0
)
and
m^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{\mu},\gamma_{\mu})>0
.
If
m^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda},\gamma_{\lambda})=0
for each
\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\{\mu\}
near
\mu
, and

m^{-}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda},\gamma_{\lambda})
take, respectively, values
m^{-}(\mathcal{E}_{\mu},\gamma_{\mu})
and

m^{-}(\mathcal{E}_{\mu},\gamma_{\mu})+m^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{\mu},\gamma_{\mu})

as
\lambda\in\Lambda
varies in two deleted half neighborhoods of
\mu
,
then one of the following alternatives occurs:

(i)

The problem (
1.12
)–(
1.13
)
with
\lambda=\mu
has a sequence of solutions,
\gamma_{k}\neq\gamma_{\mu}
,
k=1,2,\cdots
,
which converges to
\gamma_{\mu}
in
C^{2}([0,\tau],M)
.

(ii)

For every
\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\{\mu\}
near
\mu
there is a solution
\alpha_{\lambda}\neq\gamma_{\lambda}
of
(
1.12
)–(
1.13
) with parameter value
\lambda
,
such that
\alpha_{\lambda}-\gamma_{\lambda}

converges to zero in
C^{2}([0,\tau],{\mathbb{R}}^{N})
as
\lambda\to\mu
.

(
Recall that we have assumed
M\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
.
)

(iii)

For a given neighborhood
\mathcal{W}
of
\gamma_{\mu}
in
C^{1}([0,\tau],M)
,
there is a one-sided neighborhood
\Lambda^{0}
of
\mu
such that
for any
\lambda\in\Lambda^{0}\setminus\{\mu\}
, (
1.12
)–(
1.13
)
with parameter value
\lambda

has at least two distinct solutions in
\mathcal{W}
,
\gamma_{\lambda}^{1}\neq\gamma_{\lambda}
and
\gamma_{\lambda}^{2}\neq\gamma_{\lambda}
,
which can also be chosen to satisfy
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}(\gamma_{\lambda}^{1})\neq\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}(\gamma_{\lambda}^{2})

provided that
m^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{\mu},\gamma_{\mu})>1
and (
1.12
)–(
1.13
) with parameter value
\lambda

has only finitely many distinct solutions in
\mathcal{W}
.

When
M
is an open subset in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, the conditions in Assumptions
1.1
,
1.2
in theorems above
can be weakened, see Theorems
3.5
,
3.6
,
3.7
.

Assumption 1.7
.

Let
S_{0}
be a boundaryless submanifold of
M
of dimension
\dim S_{0}<\dim M
,
and let
L:[0,\tau]\times TM\to{\mathbb{R}}
be
C^{3}
and fiberwise strictly convex,
that is, for each
(t,q,v)\in[0,\tau]\times TM
the bilinear form
\partial_{vv}L(t,q,v)
is positive definite.

Under Assumption
1.7
, a
C^{2}
curve
\gamma:[0,\lambda]\to M
with
\lambda\in(0,\tau]
is called
a
Euler-Lagrange curve of
L
emanating perpendicularly from
S_{0}
if it solves the following boundary problem

\left.\begin{array}[]{ll}&\frac{d}{dt}\big(\partial_{v}L(t,\gamma(t),\dot{\gamma}(t))\big)-\partial_{q}L(t,\gamma(t),\dot{\gamma}(t))=0,\;0\leq t\leq\lambda,\\
&\gamma(0)\in S_{0}\quad\hbox{and}\quad\partial_{v}L(0,\gamma(0),\dot{\gamma}(0))[v]=0\;\forall v\in T_{\gamma(0)}S_{0}.\end{array}\right\}

(1.15)

In particular, if
S_{0}
consists of a point
p
we call

\gamma
a
Euler-Lagrange curve of
L
starting at
p
.
Since
L
is
C^{3}
, with a local coordinate chart
it may follow from
[
4
, Proposition 4.3]
that the Euler-Lagrange curves of
L
are
C^{3}
.
Clearly, for each
s\in(0,\lambda]
the Euler-Lagrange curve
\gamma_{s}:=\gamma|_{[0,s]}
of
L

emanating perpendicularly from
S_{0}
is a critical point of the
C^{2}
functional

\mathcal{L}_{S_{0},s}(\alpha):=\int^{s}_{0}L(t,\alpha(t),\dot{\alpha}(t))dt

(1.16)

on the
C^{4}
Banach manifold

C^{1}_{S_{0}\times\{\gamma(s)\}}([0,s];M)=\left\{\alpha\in C^{1}([0,s];M)\,\big|\,\alpha(0)\in S_{0},\alpha(s)=\gamma(s))\right\}.

(1.17)

We say
s\in(0,\lambda]
to be a
S_{0}
-focal point along
\gamma

if the linearization of (
1.15
) on
[0,s]
(called
the the
Jacobi equation of the functional

\mathcal{L}_{S_{0},s}
) has nonzero solutions, i.e.,
the second order differential
D^{2}\mathcal{L}_{S_{0},s}(\gamma_{s})
of

\mathcal{L}_{S_{0},s}
at
\gamma_{s}
is degenerate; moreover

\dim{\rm Ker}(D^{2}\mathcal{L}_{S_{0},s}(\gamma_{s}))
is called the
multiplicity
of
s
,
denoted by

\nu^{S_{0}}_{\gamma}(s)\quad\text{or}\quad m^{0}(\mathcal{L}_{S_{0},s},\gamma_{s}).

(1.18)

As done in
[
29
, Definition 6.1]
for geodesics,
similar to Jacobi’s original
definition of conjugate points along an extremal of quadratic functionals (cf.
[
9
, Definition 4, page 114]
) we introduce:

Definition 1.8
.

Under Assumption
1.7
,
\mu\in(0,\lambda)

is called a
bifurcation instant for
(S_{0},\gamma)
if there exists a sequence
(t_{k})\subset(0,\lambda]
converging to
\mu

and a sequence of Euler-Lagrange curves of
L
emanating perpendicularly from
S_{0}
,
\gamma^{k}:[0,t_{k}]\to M
, such that

\displaystyle\gamma^{k}(t_{k})=\gamma(t_{k})\;\hbox{for all $k\in{\mathbb{N}}$},

(1.19)

\displaystyle 0<\|\gamma^{k}-\gamma|_{[0,t_{k}]}\|_{C^{1}([0,t_{k}],\mathbb{R}^{N})}\to 0\;\hbox{ as $k\to\infty$}.

(1.20)

As proved in Lemma
2.6
(ii), using local coordinate charts
we can derive from the basic existence, uniqueness and smoothness theorem of ODE solutions that
the limit of (
1.20
) is equivalent to any one of the following two conditions:

\bullet

\|\gamma^{k}-\gamma|_{[0,t_{k}]}\|_{C^{2}([0,t_{k}],\mathbb{R}^{N})}\to 0
as
k\to\infty
.

\bullet

\gamma^{k}(0)\to\gamma(0)
and
\dot{\gamma}^{k}(0)\to\dot{\gamma}(0)
.

Theorem 1.9
.

Under Assumption
1.7
, let
\gamma:[0,\tau]\to M
be
a Euler-Lagrange curve of
L
emanating perpendicularly from
S_{0}
.
Then:

(i)

There exist only finitely many
S_{0}
-focal points along
\gamma
.

(ii)

If
\mu\in(0,\tau]
is a bifurcation instant for
(S_{0},\gamma)
, then it is a
S_{0}
-focal point along
\gamma
.

(iii)

If
\mu\in(0,\tau)
is a
S_{0}
-focal point along
\gamma
, then it is a bifurcation instant for
(S_{0},\gamma)
,
and one of the following alternatives occurs:

(iii-1)

There exists a sequence of distinct
C^{3}
Euler-Lagrange curves of
L

emanating perpendicularly from
S_{0}
and ending at
\gamma(\mu)
,

\alpha_{k}:[0,\mu]\to M
,
\alpha_{k}\neq\gamma|_{[0,\mu]}
,
k=1,2,\cdots
, such that

\alpha_{k}\to\gamma|_{[0,\mu]}
in
C^{2}([0,\mu],\mathbb{R}^{N})
as
k\to\infty
.

(iii-2)

For every
\lambda\in(0,\tau)\setminus\{\mu\}
near
\mu
there
exists a
C^{3}
Euler-Lagrange curves of
L
emanating perpendicularly from
S_{0}
and ending at
\gamma(\lambda)
,

\alpha_{\lambda}:[0,\lambda]\to M
,
\alpha_{\lambda}\neq\gamma|_{[0,\lambda]}
, such that

\|\alpha_{\lambda}-\gamma|_{[0,\lambda]}\|_{C^{2}([0,\lambda],\mathbb{R}^{N})}\to 0
as
\lambda\to\mu
.

(iii-3)

For a given small
\epsilon>0
there is a one-sided neighborhood
\Lambda^{\ast}
of
\mu
such that
for any
\lambda\in\Lambda^{\ast}\setminus\{\mu\}
, there exist at least two distinct
C^{3}

Euler-Lagrange curves of
L
emanating perpendicularly from
S_{0}
and ending at
\gamma(\lambda)
,

\beta^{i}_{\lambda}:[0,\lambda]\to M
,
\beta_{\lambda}^{i}\neq\gamma|_{[0,\lambda]}
,
i=1,2
,
to satisfy the condition that
\|\beta^{i}_{\lambda}-\gamma|_{[0,\lambda]}\|_{C^{1}([0,\lambda],\mathbb{R}^{N})}<\epsilon
,
i=1,2
.
Moreover, if the multiplicity of
\gamma(\mu)
as a
S_{0}
-focal point along
\gamma
is greater than one and
there exist only finitely many distinct
C^{3}
Euler-Lagrange curves of
L

emanating perpendicularly from
S_{0}
and ending at
\gamma(\lambda)
,

\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{m}
, such that
\|\alpha_{i}-\gamma|_{[0,\lambda]}\|_{C^{1}([0,\lambda],\mathbb{R}^{N})}<\epsilon
,
i=1,\cdots,m
,
then the above two distinct
C^{3}
L-curves
\beta_{\lambda}^{i}\neq\gamma|_{[0,\lambda]}
,
i=1,2
,
can also be chosen to satisfy

\int^{\lambda}_{0}L(t,\beta_{\lambda}^{1}(t),\dot{\beta}_{\lambda}^{1}(t))dt\neq\int^{\lambda}_{0}L(t,\beta_{\lambda}^{2}(t),\dot{\beta}_{\lambda}^{2}(t))dt.

(1.21)

Remark 1.10
.

If
M
is an open subset in the Euclidean space, we may assume that
L

in Assumption
1.7
and Theorem
1.9

is
C^{2}
, see Theorems
3.5
,
3.7
.

1.6
Bifurcations of Lagrangian trajectories with endpoints constrained in
{\rm Graph}(\mathbb{I}_{g})

Assumption 1.11
.

Let
(M,g,\mathbb{I}_{g})
be as in Assumption 1.0 in Introduction,
and let
(\tau,\Lambda,L)
be as in Assumption
1.1
.
For each
\lambda\in\Lambda
let
\gamma_{\lambda}\in C^{2}([0,\tau];M)
satisfy
the following boundary problem

\left.\begin{array}[]{ll}&\frac{d}{dt}\big(\partial_{v}L_{\lambda}(t,\gamma(t),\dot{\gamma}(t))\big)-\partial_{q}L_{\lambda}(t,\gamma(t),\dot{\gamma}(t))=0,\;0\leq t\leq\tau,\\
&\mathbb{I}_{g}(\gamma(0))=\gamma(\tau)\quad\hbox{and}\quad d\mathbb{I}_{g}(\gamma(0))\big[\frac{\partial L_{\lambda}}{\partial v}(0,\gamma(0),\dot{\gamma}(0))\big]=\frac{\partial L_{\lambda}}{\partial v}(\tau,\gamma(\tau),\dot{\gamma}(\tau)).\end{array}\right\}

(1.22)

Suppose also that
\Lambda\times{\mathbb{R}}\ni(\lambda,t)\mapsto\gamma_{\lambda}(t)\in M
and

\Lambda\times{\mathbb{R}}\ni(\lambda,t)\mapsto\dot{\gamma}_{\lambda}(t)\in TM
are continuous.

Consider the following
C^{4}
Banach submanifold of
C^{1}([0,\tau];M)
of codimension
n
,

C^{1}_{\mathbb{I}_{g}}([0,\tau];M):=\{\gamma\in C^{1}([0,\tau];M)\,|\,\mathbb{I}_{g}(\gamma(0))=\gamma(\tau)\}.

(1.23)

Its tangent space at
\gamma\in C^{1}_{\mathbb{I}_{g}}([0,\tau];M)
is

C^{1}_{\mathbb{I}_{g}}(\gamma^{\ast}TM):=T_{\gamma}C^{1}_{\mathbb{I}_{g}}([0,\tau];M)=\{\xi\in C^{1}(\gamma^{\ast}TM)\,|\,d\mathbb{I}_{g}(\gamma(0))[\xi(0)]=\xi(\tau)\},

(1.24)

which is dense in the Hilbert subspace

W^{1,2}_{\mathbb{I}_{g}}(\gamma^{\ast}TM):=\{\xi\in H^{1}(\gamma^{\ast}TM)\,|\,d\mathbb{I}_{g}(\gamma(0))[\xi(0)]=\xi(\tau)\}

(1.25)

of
W^{1,2}(\gamma^{\ast}TM)
.

For each
\lambda\in\Lambda
, by the proof of
[
20
, Proposition 4.2]
we have a
C^{2}
functional

\mathscr{E}_{\lambda}:C^{1}_{\mathbb{I}_{g}}([0,\tau];M)\to\mathbb{R},\;\gamma\mapsto\int^{\tau}_{0}L_{\lambda}(t,\gamma(t),\dot{\gamma}(t))dt.

(1.26)

[
4
, Proposition 4.2]
shows that

\gamma\in C^{1}_{\mathbb{I}_{g}}([0,\tau];M)
is
a critical point of
\mathscr{E}_{\lambda}

if and only if it belongs to
C^{3}([0,\tau];M)
and satisfy (
1.22
).
By
[
6
]
, the second-order differential
D^{2}\mathscr{E}_{\lambda}(\gamma)
of
\mathscr{E}_{\lambda}
at such a critical point
\gamma

can be extended into a continuous symmetric bilinear form on
W^{1,2}_{\mathbb{I}_{g}}(\gamma^{\ast}TM)

with finite Morse index and nullity

m^{-}_{\tau}(\mathscr{E}_{\lambda},\gamma)\quad\hbox{and}\quad m^{0}_{\tau}(\mathscr{E}_{\lambda},\gamma),

(1.27)

which coincide with those defined by (
1.4
) and (
1.5
)
for
\mathbf{N}=\text{Graph}(\mathbb{I}_{g})
, respectively.

Definition 1.12
.

In Definition
1.3
, “

X=C^{1}_{S_{0}\times S_{1}}([0,\tau];M)
(or
C^{2}_{S_{0}\times S_{1}}([0,\tau];M)
)”
is replaced by “
X=C^{1}_{\mathbb{I}_{g}}([0,\tau];M)
(or
C^{2}_{\mathbb{I}_{g}}([0,\tau];M)
)”,
and “(
1.12
)–(
1.13
)”
is replaced by “(
1.22
)”.

Theorem 1.13
.

Let Assumption
1.11
be satisfied, and
\mu\in\Lambda
.

(I)

(
Necessary condition
):

Suppose that
(\mu,\gamma_{\mu})
is a bifurcation point along sequences of
(
1.22
) with respect to the branch
\{(\lambda,\gamma_{\lambda})\,|\,\lambda\in\Lambda\}
in
C^{1}_{\mathbb{I}_{g}}([0,\tau];M)
.
Then
m^{0}(\mathscr{E}_{\mu},\gamma_{\mu})>0
.

(II)

(
Sufficient condition
):

Suppose that
\Lambda
is first countable and that there exist two sequences in
\Lambda
converging to
\mu
,
(\lambda_{k}^{-})
and

(\lambda_{k}^{+})
, such that one of the following conditions is satisfied:

(II.1)

For each
k\in\mathbb{N}
, either
\gamma_{\lambda^{+}_{k}}
is not an isolated critical point of
{\mathscr{E}}_{\lambda^{+}_{k}}
,
or
\gamma_{\lambda^{-}_{k}}
is not an isolated critical point of
{\mathscr{E}}_{\lambda^{-}_{k}}
,
or
\gamma_{\lambda^{+}_{k}}

(
resp.
\gamma_{\lambda^{-}_{k}}
)

is an isolated critical point of
\mathscr{E}_{\lambda^{+}_{k}}

(
resp.
\mathscr{E}_{\lambda^{-}_{k}}
)
and

C_{m}(\mathscr{E}_{\lambda^{+}_{k}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}_{k}};{\bf K})
and
C_{m}(\mathscr{E}_{\lambda^{-}_{k}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}_{k}};{\bf K})

are not isomorphic for some Abel group
{\bf K}
and some
m\in\mathbb{Z}
.

(II.2)

For each
k\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists
\lambda\in\{\lambda^{+}_{k},\lambda^{-}_{k}\}

such that
\gamma_{\lambda}
is an either nonisolated or homological visible critical point of

\mathscr{E}_{\lambda}
, and

\left.\begin{array}[]{ll}&[m^{-}(\mathscr{E}_{\lambda_{k}^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}_{k}}),m^{-}(\mathscr{E}_{\lambda_{k}^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}_{k}})+m^{0}(\mathscr{E}_{\lambda_{k}^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}_{k}})]\\
&\cap[m^{-}(\mathscr{E}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}_{k}}),m^{-}(\mathscr{E}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}_{k}})+m^{0}(\mathscr{E}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}_{k}})]=\emptyset.\end{array}\right\}

[formula]

(II.3)

For each
k\in\mathbb{N}
, (
2\ast_{k}
) holds true,
and either
m^{0}(\mathscr{E}_{\lambda_{k}^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}_{k}})=0
or
m^{0}(\mathscr{E}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}_{k}})=0
.

Then there exists a sequence
\{(\lambda_{k},\gamma^{k})\}_{k\geq 1}
in

\hat{\Lambda}\times C^{2}_{\mathbb{I}_{g}}([0,\tau];M)
converging to

(\mu,\gamma_{\mu})
such that each
\gamma^{k}\neq\gamma_{\lambda_{k}}
is a solution of the problem
(
1.22
) with
\lambda=\lambda_{k}
,

k=1,2,\cdots
, where

\hat{\Lambda}=\{\mu,\lambda^{+}_{k},\lambda^{-}_{k}\,|\,k\in\mathbb{N}\}
.
In particular,
(\mu,\gamma_{\mu})
is a bifurcation point of the problem
(
1.22
) in
\hat{\Lambda}\times C^{2}_{\mathbb{I}_{g}}([0,\tau];M)

with respect to the branch
\{(\lambda,\gamma_{\lambda})\,|\,\lambda\in\hat{\Lambda}\}

(
and so
\{(\lambda,\gamma_{\lambda})\,|\,\lambda\in\Lambda\}
)
.

Theorem 1.14

(
Existence for bifurcations
)
.

Let Assumption
1.11
be satisfied,
and let
\Lambda
be path-connected. Suppose that
there exist two points
\lambda^{+},\lambda^{-}\in\Lambda
such that
one of the following conditions is satisfied:

(i)

Either
\gamma_{\lambda^{+}}
is not an isolated critical point of
\mathscr{E}_{\lambda^{+}}
,
or
\gamma_{\lambda^{-}}
is not an isolated critical point of
\mathscr{E}_{\lambda^{-}}
,
or
\gamma_{\lambda^{+}}

(
resp.
\gamma_{\lambda^{-}}
)
is an isolated critical point of

\mathscr{E}_{\lambda^{+}}

(
resp.
\mathscr{E}_{\lambda^{-}}
)
and

C_{m}(\mathscr{E}_{\lambda^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}};{\bf K})
and
C_{m}(\mathscr{E}_{\lambda^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}};{\bf K})
are not isomorphic for some Abel group
{\bf K}
and some
m\in\mathbb{Z}
.

(ii)

[m^{-}({\mathscr{E}}_{\lambda^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}}),m^{-}({\mathscr{E}}_{\lambda^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})+m^{0}({\mathscr{E}}_{\lambda^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})]\cap[m^{-}({\mathscr{E}}_{\lambda^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}}),m^{-}({\mathscr{E}}_{\lambda^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})+m^{0}({\mathscr{E}}_{\lambda^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})]\\
=\emptyset
,
and there exists
\lambda\in\{\lambda^{+},\lambda^{-}\}
such that
\gamma_{\lambda}
is an either non-isolated or homological visible critical point of
\mathscr{E}_{\lambda}
.

(iii)

[m^{-}({\mathscr{E}}_{\lambda^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}}),m^{-}({\mathscr{E}}_{\lambda^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})+m^{0}({\mathscr{E}}_{\lambda^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})]\cap[m^{-}({\mathscr{E}}_{\lambda^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}}),m^{-}({\mathscr{E}}_{\lambda^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})+m^{0}({\mathscr{E}}_{\lambda^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})]\\
=\emptyset
,
and either
m^{0}(\mathscr{E}_{\lambda^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})=0
or
m^{0}(\mathscr{E}_{\lambda^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})=0
.

Then for any path
\alpha:[0,1]\to\Lambda
connecting
\lambda^{+}
to
\lambda^{-}
there exists
a sequence
(\lambda_{k})\subset\alpha([0,1])
converging to some
\mu\in\alpha([0,1])
, and
solutions
\gamma^{k}\neq\gamma_{\lambda_{k}}
of the problem (
1.22
)
with
\lambda=\lambda_{k}
,
k=1,2,\cdots
, such that
\|\gamma^{k}-\gamma_{\lambda_{k}}\|_{C^{2}([0,\tau];\mathbb{R}^{N})}\to 0

as
k\to\infty
.
(
In particular,

(\mu,\gamma_{\mu})
is a bifurcation point along sequences of the problem (
1.22
) in

\Lambda\times C^{2}_{\mathbb{I}_{g}}([0,\tau];M)
with respect to the branch
\{(\lambda,\gamma_{\lambda})\,|\,\lambda\in\Lambda\}
.
)

Moreover,
\mu
is not equal to
\lambda^{+}

(
resp.
\lambda^{-}
)

if
m^{0}_{\tau}(\mathscr{E}_{\lambda^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})=0

(
resp.
m^{0}_{\tau}(\mathscr{E}_{\lambda^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})=0
)
.

Theorem 1.15

(
Alternative bifurcations of Rabinowitz’s type
)
.

Under Assumption
1.11
with
\Lambda
being a real interval,
let
\mu\in{\rm Int}(\Lambda)
satisfy
m^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{\mu},\gamma_{\mu})>0
.
If
m^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda},\gamma_{\lambda})=0
for each
\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\{\mu\}
near
\mu
, and

m^{-}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda},\gamma_{\lambda})
take, respectively, values
m^{-}(\mathcal{E}_{\mu},\gamma_{\mu})
and

m^{-}(\mathcal{E}_{\mu},\gamma_{\mu})+m^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{\mu},\gamma_{\mu})

as
\lambda\in\Lambda
varies in two deleted half neighborhoods of
\mu
,
then one of the following alternatives occurs:

(i)

The problem (
1.22
)
with
\lambda=\mu
has a sequence of solutions,
\gamma_{k}\neq\gamma_{\mu}
,
k=1,2,\cdots
,
which converges to
\gamma_{\mu}
in
C^{2}([0,\tau],M)
.

(ii)

For every
\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\{\mu\}
near
\mu
there is a solution
\alpha_{\lambda}\neq\gamma_{\lambda}
of
(
1.22
) with parameter value
\lambda
,
such that
\alpha_{\lambda}-\gamma_{\lambda}

converges to zero in
C^{2}([0,\tau],{\mathbb{R}}^{N})
as
\lambda\to\mu
.

(
Recall that we have assumed
M\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
.
)

(iii)

For a given neighborhood
\mathcal{W}
of
\gamma_{\mu}
in
C^{1}_{\mathbb{I}_{g}}([0,\tau];M)
,
there is a one-sided neighborhood
\Lambda^{0}
of
\mu
such that
for any
\lambda\in\Lambda^{0}\setminus\{\mu\}
, (
1.22
) with parameter value
\lambda

has at least two distinct solutions in
\mathcal{W}
,
\gamma_{\lambda}^{1}\neq\gamma_{\lambda}
and
\gamma_{\lambda}^{2}\neq\gamma_{\lambda}
,
which can also be chosen to satisfy
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}(\gamma_{\lambda}^{1})\neq\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}(\gamma_{\lambda}^{2})

provided that
m^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{\mu},\gamma_{\mu})>1
and (
1.22
) with parameter value
\lambda

has only finitely many distinct solutions in
\mathcal{W}
.

1.7
Bifurcations of generalized periodic solutions in non-autonomous Lagrangian systems

Assumption 1.16
.

In Assumption
1.11
, the interval
[0,\tau]
is replaced by
\mathbb{R}
, and

L
is also required to be truly dependent on time
t\in\mathbb{R}
and
\mathbb{I}_{g}
-invariant in the
following sense:

L(\lambda,t+\tau,\mathbb{I}_{g}(x),d\mathbb{I}_{g}(x)[v])=L(\lambda,t,x,v)\quad\forall(\lambda,t,x,v)\in\Lambda\times\mathbb{R}\times TM.

(1.28)

The problem (
1.22
) is replaced by

\left.\begin{array}[]{ll}&\frac{d}{dt}\big(\partial_{v}L_{\lambda}(t,\gamma(t),\dot{\gamma}(t))\big)-\partial_{q}L_{\lambda}(t,\gamma(t),\dot{\gamma}(t))=0\;\;\forall t\in\mathbb{R},\\
&\mathbb{I}_{g}(\gamma(t))=\gamma(t+\tau)\quad\forall t\in\mathbb{R}.\end{array}\right\}

(1.29)

Solutions of (
1.29
) are also called

\mathbb{I}_{g}
-periodic trajectories with period
\tau
(
[
5
]
).
When
\mathbb{I}_{g}
generates a cyclic group, that is, it is of finite order
p\in\mathbb{N}
,
every
\mathbb{I}_{g}
-periodic trajectory is
p\tau
-periodic.

Since the functional in (
1.26
) is
C^{2}
,
(or by the proof of
[
20
, Proposition 4.2]
) we have a
C^{2}

functional on a
C^{4}
Banach manifold
\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g})
,

\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda}(\gamma)=\int^{\tau}_{0}L_{\lambda}(t,\gamma(t),\dot{\gamma}(t))dt

(1.30)

where

\mathcal{X}^{i}_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g}):=\{\gamma\in C^{i}(\mathbb{R},M)\,|\,\mathbb{I}_{g}(\gamma(t))=\gamma(t+\tau)\;\forall t\},\quad i=0,1,2,\cdots.

(1.31)

A
C^{2}
curve
\gamma:\mathbb{R}\to M
satisfies (
1.29
) if and only if it is a critical point of
\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda}
.
(If
\gamma
is a nonconstant solution of (
1.29
), for any
\theta\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}
,
\gamma(\theta+\cdot)

cannot be a solution of (
1.29
) again because
L
is truly dependent on time
t\in\mathbb{R}
.)
Clearly, a solution
\gamma
of the problem (
1.29
) restricts to a solution

\gamma|_{[0,\tau]}
of (
1.22
).
Conversely, any solution
\gamma^{\ast}
of (
1.22
) may extend into that of (
1.29
),

\gamma:\mathbb{R}\to M
, via

\gamma(t)=(\mathbb{I}_{g})^{k}(\gamma^{\ast}(t-k\tau))\;\hbox{if $k\tau<t\leq(k+1)\tau$ with $\pm k\in\mathbb{N}$}.

(1.32)

Moreover, for a solution
\gamma
of (
1.29
) we call

m^{-}_{\tau}(\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda},\gamma):=m^{-}_{\tau}(\mathscr{E}_{\lambda},\gamma|_{[0,\tau]})\quad\hbox{and}\quad m^{0}_{\tau}(\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda},\gamma):=m^{0}_{\tau}(\mathscr{E}_{\lambda},\gamma|_{[0,\tau]})

(1.33)

the Morse index and nullity of
\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda}
at
\gamma
, respectively, where

m^{-}_{\tau}(\mathscr{E}_{\lambda},\gamma|_{[0,\tau]})
and
m^{0}_{\tau}(\mathscr{E}_{\lambda},\gamma|_{[0,\tau]})

are as in (
1.27
). These are well-defined by
[
5
, §4]
.

Theorem
1.13
,
1.14
,
1.15

immediately leads to the following two results, respectively.

Theorem 1.17
.

Let Assumption
1.16
be satisfied, and
\mu\in\Lambda
.

(I)

(
Necessary condition
):

Suppose that
(\mu,\gamma_{\mu})
is a bifurcation point along sequences of the problem
(
1.29
) in
\Lambda\times\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g})
with respect to the branch

\{(\lambda,\gamma_{\lambda})\,|\,\lambda\in\Lambda\}
.
Then
m^{0}_{\tau}(\mathfrak{E}_{\mu},\gamma_{\mu})\neq 0
.

(II)

(
Sufficient condition
):

Suppose that
\Lambda
is first countable and that there exist two sequences in
\Lambda
converging to
\mu
,
(\lambda_{k}^{-})
and

(\lambda_{k}^{+})
, such that one of the following conditions is satisfied:

(II.1)

For each
k\in\mathbb{N}
, either
\gamma_{\lambda^{+}_{k}}
is not an isolated critical point of

{\mathfrak{E}}_{\lambda^{+}_{k}}
,
or
\gamma_{\lambda^{-}_{k}}
is not an isolated critical point of
{\mathfrak{E}}_{\lambda^{-}_{k}}
,
or
\gamma_{\lambda^{+}_{k}}

(
resp.
\gamma_{\lambda^{-}_{k}}
)
is an isolated critical point
of
\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda^{+}_{k}}

(
resp.
\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda^{-}_{k}}
)
and

C_{m}(\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda^{+}_{k}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}_{k}};{\bf K})
and
C_{m}(\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda^{-}_{k}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}_{k}};{\bf K})

are not isomorphic for some Abel group
{\bf K}
and some
m\in\mathbb{Z}
.

(II.2)

For each
k\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists
\lambda\in\{\lambda^{+}_{k},\lambda^{-}_{k}\}

such that
\gamma_{\lambda}
is an either nonisolated or homological visible critical point of

\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda}
, and

\left.\begin{array}[]{ll}&[m^{-}(\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda_{k}^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}_{k}}),m^{-}(\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda_{k}^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}_{k}})+m^{0}(\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda_{k}^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}_{k}})]\\
&\cap[m^{-}(\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}_{k}}),m^{-}(\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}_{k}})+m^{0}(\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}_{k}})]=\emptyset.\end{array}\right\}

[formula]

(II.3)

For each
k\in\mathbb{N}
, (
3\ast_{k}
) holds true,
and either
m^{0}(\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda_{k}^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}_{k}})=0
or
m^{0}(\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}_{k}})=0
.

Then there exists a sequence
\{(\lambda_{k},\gamma^{k})\}_{k\geq 1}
in

\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathcal{X}^{2}_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g})
converging to

(\mu,\gamma_{\mu})
such that each
\gamma^{k}\neq\gamma_{\lambda_{k}}
is a solution of the problem
(
1.29
) with
\lambda=\lambda_{k}
,

k=1,2,\cdots
, where

\hat{\Lambda}=\{\mu,\lambda^{+}_{k},\lambda^{-}_{k}\,|\,k\in\mathbb{N}\}
.
In particular,
(\mu,\gamma_{\mu})
is a bifurcation point of the problem
(
1.29
) in
\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathcal{X}^{2}_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g})

with respect to the branch
\{(\lambda,\gamma_{\lambda})\,|\,\lambda\in\hat{\Lambda}\}

(
and so
\{(\lambda,\gamma_{\lambda})\,|\,\lambda\in\Lambda\}
)
.

Theorem 1.18

(
Existence for bifurcations
)
.

Let Assumption
1.16
be satisfied, and let
\Lambda
be path-connected.
Suppose that
there exist two points
\lambda^{+},\lambda^{-}\in\Lambda
such that
one of the following conditions is satisfied:

(i)

Either
\gamma_{\lambda^{+}}
is not an isolated critical point of
\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda^{+}}
,
or
\gamma_{\lambda^{-}}
is not an isolated critical point of
\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda^{-}}
,
or
\gamma_{\lambda^{+}}

(
resp.
\gamma_{\lambda^{-}}
)
is an isolated critical point of

\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda^{+}}

(
resp.
\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda^{-}}
)
and

C_{m}(\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}};{\bf K})
and
C_{m}(\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}};{\bf K})
are not isomorphic for some Abel group
{\bf K}
and some
m\in\mathbb{Z}
.

(ii)

[m^{-}({\mathfrak{E}}_{\lambda^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}}),m^{-}({\mathfrak{E}}_{\lambda^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})+m^{0}({\mathfrak{E}}_{\lambda^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})]\cap[m^{-}({\mathfrak{E}}_{\lambda^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}}),m^{-}({\mathfrak{E}}_{\lambda^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})+m^{0}({\mathfrak{E}}_{\lambda^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})]\\
=\emptyset
,
and there exists
\lambda\in\{\lambda^{+},\lambda^{-}\}
such that
\gamma_{\lambda}
is an either non-isolated or homological visible critical point of
\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda}
.

(iii)

[m^{-}({\mathfrak{E}}_{\lambda^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}}),m^{-}({\mathfrak{E}}_{\lambda^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})+m^{0}({\mathfrak{E}}_{\lambda^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})]\cap[m^{-}({\mathfrak{E}}_{\lambda^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}}),m^{-}({\mathfrak{E}}_{\lambda^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})+m^{0}({\mathfrak{E}}_{\lambda^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})]\\
=\emptyset
,
and either
m^{0}(\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})=0
or
m^{0}(\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})=0
.

Then for any path
\alpha:[0,1]\to\Lambda
connecting
\lambda^{+}
to
\lambda^{-}
there exists
a sequence
(\lambda_{k})\subset\alpha([0,1])
converging to
\mu\in\alpha([0,1])
, and
solutions
\gamma^{k}\neq\gamma_{\lambda_{k}}
of the problem (
1.29
)
with
\lambda=\lambda_{k}
,
k=1,2,\cdots
, such that
(\gamma^{k}-\gamma_{\lambda_{k}})|_{[0,\tau]}\to 0
in
C^{2}([0,\tau];\mathbb{R}^{N})

as
k\to\infty
.
(
In particular,

(\mu,\gamma_{\mu})
is a bifurcation point along sequences of the problem (
1.29
) in

\Lambda\times\mathcal{X}^{2}_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g})
with respect to the branch
\{(\lambda,\gamma_{\lambda})\,|\,\lambda\in\Lambda\}
.

)

Moreover,
\mu
is not equal to
\lambda^{+}

(
resp.
\lambda^{-}
)

if
m^{0}_{\tau}(\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})=0

(
resp.
m^{0}_{\tau}(\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})=0
)
.

Theorem 1.19

(
Alternative bifurcations of Rabinowitz’s type
)
.

Under Assumption
1.16

with
\Lambda
being a real interval,
let
\mu\in{\rm Int}(\Lambda)
satisfy
m^{0}_{\tau}(\mathfrak{E}_{\mu},\gamma_{\mu})\neq 0
.
Suppose that
m^{0}_{\tau}(\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda},\gamma_{\lambda})=0
for each
\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\{\mu\}
near
\mu
, and
that
m^{-}_{\tau}(\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda},\gamma_{\lambda})
take, respectively, values
m^{-}_{\tau}(\mathfrak{E}_{\mu},\gamma_{\mu})
and

m^{-}_{\tau}(\mathfrak{E}_{\mu},\gamma_{\mu})+m^{0}_{\tau}(\mathfrak{E}_{\mu},\gamma_{\mu})

as
\lambda\in\Lambda
varies in two deleted half neighborhoods of
\mu
.
Then one of the following alternatives occurs:

(i)

The problem (
1.29
)
with
\lambda=\mu
has a sequence of solutions,
\gamma_{k}\neq\gamma_{\mu}
,
k=1,2,\cdots
,
such that
\gamma_{k}\to\gamma_{\mu}
in
\mathcal{X}^{2}_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g})

(or equivalently
\gamma_{k}|_{[0,\tau]}-\gamma_{\mu}|_{[0,\tau]}

converges to zero in
C^{2}([0,\tau],{\mathbb{R}}^{N})
).

(ii)

For every
\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\{\mu\}
near
\mu
there exists a solution
\alpha_{\lambda}\neq\gamma_{\lambda}
of
(
1.29
) with parameter value
\lambda
,
such that
\|(\alpha_{\lambda}-\gamma_{\lambda})|_{[0,\tau]}\|_{C^{2}([0,\tau],{\mathbb{R}}^{N})}\to 0

as
\lambda\to\mu
.

(iii)

For a given neighborhood
\mathcal{W}
of

\gamma_{\mu}
in
\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g})
,
there exists a one-sided neighborhood
\Lambda^{0}
of
\mu
such that
for any
\lambda\in\Lambda^{0}\setminus\{\mu\}
, the problem (
1.29
) with parameter value
\lambda

has at least two distinct solutions in
\mathcal{W}
,
\gamma_{\lambda}^{1}\neq\gamma_{\lambda}
and
\gamma_{\lambda}^{2}\neq\gamma_{\lambda}
,
which can also be chosen to satisfy
\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda}(\gamma_{\lambda}^{1})\neq\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda}(\gamma_{\lambda}^{2})

provided that
m^{0}_{\tau}(\mathfrak{E}_{\mu},\gamma_{\mu})>1
and the problem (
1.29
) with parameter value

\lambda\in\Lambda^{0}\setminus\{\mu\}
has only finitely many distinct solutions in
\mathcal{W}
.

1.8
Bifurcations of brake orbits in Lagrangian systems

Assumption 1.20
.

Let
(M,g)
be as in Assumption 1.0 in Introduction.
For a real
\tau>0
and a topological space
\Lambda
,
let
L:\Lambda\times{\mathbb{R}}\times TM\to{\mathbb{R}}
be a continuous function satisfying

L(\lambda,-t,q,-v)=L(\lambda,t,q,v)=L(\lambda,t+\tau,q,v)\quad\forall(\lambda,t,q,v)\in\Lambda\times{\mathbb{R}}\times TM.

(1.34)

Suppose that for each
C^{3}
chart
\alpha:U_{\alpha}\to\alpha(U_{\alpha})\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
and the induced bundle
chart
T\alpha:TM|_{U_{\alpha}}\to\alpha(U_{\alpha})\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
the function

L^{\alpha}:\Lambda\times\mathbb{R}\times\alpha(U_{\alpha})\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R},\;(\lambda,t,q,v)\mapsto L(\lambda,t,(T\alpha)^{-1}(q,v))

is
C^{2}
with respect to
(t,q,v)
and strictly convex with respect to
v
, and
all its partial derivatives also depend continuously on
(\lambda,t,q,v)
. (Here
L
is allowed to be independent of time.)

Consider the following problem

\left.\begin{array}[]{ll}&\frac{d}{dt}\big(\partial_{v}L_{\lambda}(t,\gamma(t),\dot{\gamma}(t))\big)-\partial_{x}L_{\lambda}(t,\gamma(t),\dot{\gamma}(t))=0\;\forall t\in\mathbb{R}\\
&\gamma(-t)=\gamma(t)=\gamma(t+\tau)\quad\forall t\in\mathbb{R}\end{array}\right\}

(1.35)

and
C^{4}
Banach manifolds

EC^{1}(S_{\tau};M):=\{\gamma\in C^{1}({\mathbb{R}};M)\,|\,\gamma(t+\tau)=\gamma(t)\,\&\,\gamma(-t)=\gamma(t)\;\forall t\in{\mathbb{R}}\}.

(1.36)

Solutions of (
1.35
) are called
brake orbits
.
Assumption
1.20
assures that the solutions of (
1.35
) are critical points of the
C^{2}
functionals

{\mathcal{L}}^{E}_{\lambda}:EC^{1}(S_{\tau};M)\to{\mathbb{R}},\;\gamma\mapsto\int^{\tau}_{0}{L}_{\lambda}(t,\gamma(t),\dot{\gamma}(t))dt\in{\mathbb{R}},\quad\lambda\in\Lambda.

(1.37)

For a critical point
\gamma
of
{\mathcal{L}}^{E}_{\lambda}
,
the second-order differential
D^{2}\mathcal{L}^{E}_{\lambda}(\gamma)
can be extended into a continuous symmetric bilinear form on
W^{1,2}(\gamma^{\ast}TM)

with finite Morse index and nullity

m^{-}_{\tau}(\mathcal{L}^{E}_{\lambda},\gamma)\quad\hbox{and}\quad m^{0}_{\tau}(\mathcal{L}^{E}_{\lambda},\gamma).

(1.38)

Assumption 1.21
.

For each
\lambda\in\Lambda
let
\gamma_{\lambda}\in EC^{1}(S_{\tau};M)\cap C^{2}(S_{\tau};M)
satisfy
(
1.35
) and the maps
\Lambda\times{\mathbb{R}}\ni(\lambda,t)\to\gamma_{\lambda}(t)\in M
and

\Lambda\times{\mathbb{R}}\ni(\lambda,t)\mapsto\dot{\gamma}_{\lambda}(t)\in TM
are continuous.

For
\mu\in\Lambda
we call
(\mu,\gamma_{\mu})
a
bifurcation point along sequences
of the problem (
1.35
)
in
\Lambda\times EC^{1}(S_{\tau};M)
with respect to the branch
\{(\lambda,\gamma_{\lambda})\,|\,\lambda\in\Lambda\}

if there exists a sequence
\{(\lambda_{k},\gamma^{k})\}_{k\geq 1}
in
\Lambda\times EC^{1}(S_{\tau};M)

converging to
(\mu,\gamma_{\mu})
, such that each
\gamma^{k}\neq\gamma_{\lambda_{k}}

is a solution of (
1.35
) with
\lambda=\lambda_{k}
,
k=1,2,\cdots
.

Theorem 1.22
.

Let Assumptions
1.20
,
1.21
be satisfied.

(I)

(
Necessary condition
):

Suppose that
(\mu,\gamma_{\mu})
is a bifurcation point along sequences of the problem (
1.35
).
Then
m^{0}_{\tau}(\mathcal{L}^{E}_{\mu},\gamma_{\mu})>0
.

(II)

(
Sufficient condition
):

Let
\Lambda
be first countable.

\Lambda
converging to
\mu
,
(\lambda_{k}^{-})
and

(\lambda_{k}^{+})

\mathcal{L}^{E}_{\lambda^{+}_{k}}
,
or
\gamma_{\lambda^{-}_{k}}

\mathcal{L}^{E}_{\lambda^{-}_{k}}
,
or
\gamma_{\lambda^{+}_{k}}

(

\gamma_{\lambda^{-}_{k}}

is an isolated critical point of
\mathcal{L}^{E}_{\lambda^{+}_{k}}

(

)
and

are not isomorphic for some Abel group
{\bf K}
and some

.

(II.2)

For each
k\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists
\lambda\in\{\lambda^{+}_{k},\lambda^{-}_{k}\}

such that

is an either nonisolated or homological visible critical point of

&\cap[m^{-}(\mathcal{L}^{E}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}_{k}}),m^{-}(\mathcal{L}^{E}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}_{k}})+m^{0}(\mathcal{L}^{E}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}_{k}})]=\emptyset.\end{array}\right\}

[formula]

For each

, (
4\ast_{k}
) holds true,
and either

or

.

Then there exists a sequence
\{(\lambda_{k},\gamma^{k})\}_{k\geq 1}
in

\hat{\Lambda}\times EC^{1}(S_{\tau};M)
converging to

(\mu,\gamma_{\mu})
such that each
\gamma^{k}\neq\gamma_{\lambda_{k}}
is a solution of the problem
(
1.35
) with
\lambda=\lambda_{k}
,

k=1,2,\cdots
, where

\hat{\Lambda}=\{\mu,\lambda^{+}_{k},\lambda^{-}_{k}\,|\,k\in\mathbb{N}\}
.
In particular,
(\mu,\gamma_{\mu})
is a bifurcation point of the problem
(
1.35
) in
\hat{\Lambda}\times EC^{1}(S_{\tau};M)

with respect to the branch
\{(\lambda,\gamma_{\lambda})\,|\,\lambda\in\hat{\Lambda}\}

(
and so
\{(\lambda,\gamma_{\lambda})\,|\,\lambda\in\Lambda\}
)
.

Theorem 1.23

(
Existence for bifurcations
)

,

is not an isolated critical point of

,

\gamma_{\lambda^{-}}

\mathcal{L}^{E}_{\lambda^{-}}
,

(

\gamma_{\lambda^{-}}

,
and there exists

(iii)

[m^{-}(\mathcal{L}^{E}_{\lambda^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}}),m^{-}(\mathcal{L}^{E}_{\lambda^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})+m^{0}(\mathcal{L}^{E}_{\lambda^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})]\cap[m^{-}(\mathcal{L}^{E}_{\lambda^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}}),m^{-}(\mathcal{L}^{E}_{\lambda^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})+m^{0}(\mathcal{L}^{E}_{\lambda^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})]\\
=\emptyset
,
and either
m^{0}(\mathcal{L}^{E}_{\lambda^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})=0

\alpha:[0,1]\to\Lambda

to

)
with

,

, such that

as

.
(

is a bifurcation point along sequences of the problem (
1.35
)
in

with respect to the branch

.
)

Moreover,
\mu
is not equal to
\lambda^{+}

(
resp.
\lambda^{-}
)
if
m^{0}_{\tau}(\mathcal{L}^{E}_{\lambda^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})=0

(
resp.
m^{0}_{\tau}(\mathcal{L}^{E}_{\lambda^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})=0
)
.

Theorem 1.24

(
Alternative bifurcations of Rabinowitz’s type
)
.

Under Assumptions
1.20
,
1.21
with
\Lambda
being a real interval,
let
\mu\in{\rm Int}(\Lambda)
satisfy
m^{0}_{\tau}(\mathcal{L}^{E}_{\mu},\gamma_{\mu})>0
.
Suppose that
m^{0}_{\tau}(\mathcal{L}^{E}_{\lambda},\gamma_{\lambda})=0
for each
\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\{\mu\}
near
\mu
, and
that
m^{-}_{\tau}(\mathcal{L}^{E}_{\lambda},\gamma_{\lambda})
take, respectively, values
m^{-}_{\tau}(\mathcal{L}^{E}_{\mu},\gamma_{\mu})
and

m^{-}_{\tau}(\mathcal{L}^{E}_{\mu},\gamma_{\mu})+m^{0}_{\tau}(\mathcal{L}^{E}_{\mu},\gamma_{\mu})

as
\lambda\in\Lambda
varies in two deleted half neighborhoods of
\mu
.
Then one of the following alternatives occurs:

(i)

The problem (
1.35
)
with
\lambda=\mu
has a sequence of solutions,
\gamma_{k}\neq\gamma_{\mu}
,
k=1,2,\cdots
,
which converges to
\gamma_{\mu}
in
C^{2}(S_{\tau},M)
.

(ii)

For every
\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\{\mu\}
near
\mu
there exists a solution
\alpha_{\lambda}\neq\gamma_{\lambda}
of
(
1.35
) with parameter value
\lambda
, such that
\alpha_{\lambda}-\gamma_{\lambda}

converges to zero in
C^{2}(S_{\tau},{\mathbb{R}}^{N})
as
\lambda\to\mu
.

(
Recall that
M\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
.
)

(iii)

For a given neighborhood
\mathcal{W}
of
\gamma_{\mu}
in
C^{2}(S_{\tau},M)
,
there exists a one-sided neighborhood
\Lambda^{0}
of
\mu
such that
for any
\lambda\in\Lambda^{0}\setminus\{\mu\}
, (
1.35
) with parameter value
\lambda

has at least two distinct solutions in
\mathcal{W}
,
\gamma_{\lambda}^{1}\neq\gamma_{\lambda}
and
\gamma_{\lambda}^{2}\neq\gamma_{\lambda}
,
which can also be chosen to satisfy
\mathcal{L}^{E}_{\lambda}(\gamma_{\lambda}^{1})\neq\mathcal{L}^{E}_{\lambda}(\gamma_{\lambda}^{2})

provided that
m^{0}_{\tau}(\mathcal{L}^{E}_{\mu},\gamma_{\mu})>1
and (
1.35
) with parameter value
\lambda

has only finitely many solutions in
\mathcal{W}
.

As noted in Remark
2.8
, when
M
is an open subset in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
the conditions in the above theorems may be weakened suitably.

Remark 1.25
.

Clearly, if the Lagrangian
L
in Assumption
1.20

comes from a family of
C^{6}
Riemannian metrics
\{h_{\lambda}\,|\,\lambda\in\Lambda\}

on
M
, i.e.,
L(\lambda,t,x,v)=(h_{\lambda})_{x}(v,v)
for all
(\lambda,t,x,v)
,
as direct consequences of the above results we immediately obtain many bifurcation theorems of geodesics on Riemannian manifolds.
See
[
23
, Part B]
.

Remark 1.26
.

A natural question is how to verify the conditions required in Theorems
1.6
,
1.15
,
1.19
, and
1.24
. We address this question in
Section
6
with Appendix
B
.

1.9
Organization of the article

Section
2
introduces the basic notation and presents some
technical lemmas that will be used in the subsequent sections.

In Section
3
, we discuss bifurcations of Lagrangian system trajectories connecting two submanifolds. By reducing the problem to Euclidean spaces, we prove the main results presented in Section
1.5
, namely, Theorems
1.4
,
1.5
,
1.6
, and
1.9
. The proof of an important lemma, Lemma
3.1
, is deferred to Appendix
A
.

In Section
4
, we study
bifurcations of Lagrangian trajectories with endpoints related by an isometry.
The main results of Section
1.6
—Theorems
1.13
,
1.14
and
1.15
—are proved by a reduction to Euclidean spaces.

Section
5
deals with bifurcations of brake orbits in Lagrangian systems. Based on a reduction to Euclidean spaces (using Claim
A.1
, to be proved in Appendix
A
), we prove the main results of Section
1.8
: Theorems
1.22
,
1.23
, and
1.24
.

In Section
6
, using the abstract results in Appendix
B
, we derive more readily applicable corollaries of the main results. We then apply them to bifurcation problems concerning physical Lagrangians with potential and electromagnetic forces, specifically presenting bifurcation results for the planar forced simple pendulum.

Appendix
A
provides the proofs of Lemma
3.1
and Claim
A.1
.

In Appendix
B
, we study the dependence on parameters of the Morse indices of a family of abstract functionals defined on a separable real Hilbert space at a common critical point.

Acknowledgements.
I would like to thank the anonymous referee for his/her many valuable comments and suggestions regarding the mathematical content and the writing, which have greatly improved the revised version.

2
Preliminaries and notation

This section collects the standard notations, function spaces, and basic technical lemmas used throughout the paper. It aims to make the paper self-contained and to clarify the meaning of symbols introduced in subsequent sections.

2.1
Notation and Conventions

•

Vectors in
\mathbb{R}^{m}
are understood as column vectors;

(\cdot,\cdot)_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}
and
|\cdot|
denote, respectively,
the standard Euclidean inner product and norm in
\mathbb{R}^{m}
.

•

The transpose of a matrix
M
is denoted by
M^{\top}
.

•

For a Hilbert space
H
,
\mathscr{L}_{s}(H)
denotes the space of continuous linear
self-adjoint operators on
H
. We also use
\mathcal{L}_{s}(\mathbb{R}^{m})

to denote the set of real symmetric matrices of order
m
).

•

For normed (real) linear spaces
X
and
Y
,
\mathscr{L}(X,Y)
is the space of
continuous linear mappings from
X
to
Y
. Call
X^{\ast}:=\mathscr{L}(X,\mathbb{R})

the dual (or adjoint) space of
X
.

•

For a map
f
from normed (real) linear spaces
X
to
Y
,
let
Df(x)
denote the Gâteaux derivative of
f
at
x\in X
,
and let
df(x)
(or
f^{\prime}(x)
) denotes the Fréchet derivative of
f
at
x\in X
.
Both are elements of the space
\mathscr{L}(X,Y)
.
We may also use the notation
f^{\prime}(x)
for the Gâteaux derivative
Df(x)

when no confusion arises.

•

For a real functional
f
on a Hilbert space
H
, the
the derivative
f^{\prime}(x)
of
f
at
x\in H
belongs to the dual space
H^{\ast}
.
By the Riesz representation theorem,
f^{\prime}(x)
corresponds to a unique
element in

H
, which we call the gradient of
f
at
x
, denoted by
\nabla f(x)
.

The Fréchet (or Gâteaux) derivative of
\nabla f
at
x\in H

is denoted by
f^{\prime\prime}(x)
. This is an element of
\mathscr{L}_{s}(H)
.
More precisely,
f^{\prime\prime}(x)
is identified with the second derivative
(f^{\prime})^{\prime}(x)\in\mathscr{L}(H;H^{\ast})
,
which corresponds to a symmetric bilinear form on
H
via the Riesz representation theorem
(identifying
H^{\ast}
with
H
). This is equivalent to the derivative

D(\nabla f)(x)
.

•

L^{p}([0,\tau];\mathbb{R}^{m})
(
1\leq p<\infty
) denotes the space of (equivalence classes of)
p
-integrable functions from
[0,\tau]
to
\mathbb{R}^{m}
, equipped with the norm
\|u\|_{p}=(\int_{0}^{\tau}|u(t)|^{p})^{1/p}
.
In particular,
L^{2}([0,\tau];\mathbb{R}^{m})

becomes a Hilbert space equipped with the inner product

(u,v)_{2}=\int_{0}^{\tau}(u(t),v(t))_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}dt.

(2.1)

•

W^{1,p}([0,\tau];\mathbb{R}^{m})
(
1\leq p<\infty
)
denotes the Sobolev space of absolutely continuous functions with
p
-integrable derivatives,
equipped with the norm

\|u\|_{1,p}=\left(\int_{0}^{\tau}|u(t)|^{p}dt+\int_{0}^{\tau}|\dot{u}(t)|^{p}dt\right)^{1/p}.

In particular,
W^{1,2}([0,\tau];\mathbb{R}^{m})
becomes a Hilbert space equipped with the inner product

(u,v)_{1,2}=\int_{0}^{\tau}[(u(t),v(t))_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}+(\dot{u},\dot{v})_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}]dt.

(2.2)

•

C^{k}([0,\tau];M)
: The Banach manifold of
C^{k}
-smooth curves from
[0,\tau]
to
M
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
.
C^{0}([0,\tau];M)
denotes the topological space
of continuous curves from
[0,\tau]
to
M
. When
M\subset\mathbb{R}^{N}
,
it has the induced subspace topology from the Banach space
C^{0}([0,\tau];\mathbb{R}^{N})

with norm

\|u\|_{C^{0}}=\max\{|u(t)|_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}\mid t\in[0,\tau]\}
.

•

C^{1}_{\mathbf{N}}([0,\tau];M)
: The Banach submanifold of
C^{1}([0,\tau];M)
defined by the boundary condition
(\gamma(0),\gamma(\tau))\in\mathbf{N}
, where
\mathbf{N}\subset M\times M
is a submanifold. See (
1.3
).

•

C_{\mathbb{I}_{g}}^{1}([0,\tau];M)
is defined as
C^{1}_{\mathbf{N}}([0,\tau];M)

when
\mathbf{N}
is the graph
\operatorname{Graph}(\mathbb{I}_{g})
of
an isometry
\mathbb{I}_{g}
on
(M,g)
. See (
1.23
).

•

\mathcal{X}^{i}_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g})
: The space of
\mathbb{I}_{g}
-periodic

C^{i}
curves (see (
1.31
)).

•

EC^{1}(S_{\tau};M)
: The space of
even and
\tau
-periodic
C^{1}
curves (see (
1.36
)).

•

W^{1,2}(\gamma^{*}TM)
: The Hilbert space of
W^{1,2}
-sections of the pullback bundle
\gamma^{*}TM
, where
\gamma\in W^{1,2}([0,\tau];M)
. It is equipped with inner product
given by (
1.10
). When
\gamma
belongs to

W^{1,2}_{S_{0}\times S_{1}}([0,\tau];M)
(resp.
W^{1,2}_{\mathbb{I}_{g}}([0,\tau];M)
),
the space
W^{1,2}(\gamma^{*}TM)
contains

W^{1,2}_{S_{0}\times S_{1}}(\gamma^{\ast}TM)
(resp.

W^{1,2}_{\mathbb{I}_{g}}(\gamma^{\ast}TM)
. These subspaces are defined in
(
1.9
)) and (
1.25
), respectively.

•

C^{1}(\gamma^{\ast}TM)
: The Banach space of
C^{1}
-sections of the pullback bundle
\gamma^{*}TM
, where
\gamma\in C^{1}([0,\tau];M)
. See (
1.6
).
When
\gamma
belongs to
C^{1}_{S_{0}\times S_{1}}([0,\tau];M)
(resp.
C^{1}_{\mathbb{I}_{g}}([0,\tau];M)
),
the space
C^{1}(\gamma^{\ast}TM)
contains the subspace
C^{1}_{S_{0}\times S_{1}}(\gamma^{\ast}TM)

(resp.
C^{1}_{\mathbb{I}_{g}}(\gamma^{\ast}TM)
). These subspaces are defined in
(
1.8
) and (
1.24
), respectively.

•

\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}
:
The action functional associated with a parameterized Lagrangian function

L:\Lambda\times[0,\tau]\times TM\to\mathbb{R}
,
on
C^{1}_{\bf N}([0,\tau];M)
, specifically on the subspace

C^{1}_{S_{0}\times S_{1}}([0,\tau];M)
. See (
1.11
) and (
1.2
).

•

\mathcal{L}_{S_{0},s}
: The action functional
associated with a Lagrangian function
L:[0,\tau]\times TM\to\mathbb{R}
,
defined on
C^{1}_{S_{0}\times\{\gamma(s)\}}([0,\tau];M)

for a given
C^{2}
curve
\gamma:[0,\lambda]\to M
with
\lambda\in(0,\tau]
that solves (
1.15
), where
s\in(0,\lambda]
. See (
1.16
)
for the explicit expression.

•

\mathscr{E}_{\lambda}
:
The action functional on
C^{1}_{\mathbb{I}_{g}}([0,\tau];M)

associated with a parameterized Lagrangian function

L:\Lambda\times[0,\tau]\times TM\to\mathbb{R}
.
See (
1.26
).

•

\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda}
: The action functional
on
\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g})
associated with a parameterized Lagrangian function

L:\Lambda\times\mathbb{R}\times TM\to\mathbb{R}
satisfying (
1.28
).
See (
1.30
).

•

\mathcal{L}^{E}_{\lambda}
: The action functional on

EC^{1}(S_{\tau};M)
associated with a parameterized Lagrangian function

L:\Lambda\times\mathbb{R}\times TM\to\mathbb{R}
satisfying (
1.34
).
See (
1.37
).

•

m^{-}(F,p)
(resp.
m^{0}(F,p)
): The Morse index (resp. nullity) of a critical point
p
of a functional
F
, where
F
denotes one of the functionals
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}
,
\mathcal{L}_{S_{0},s}
,
\mathscr{E}_{\lambda}
,
\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda}
, and
\mathcal{L}^{E}_{\lambda}
.
See (
1.14
), (
1.18
),
(
1.27
), (
1.33
), and (
1.38
).

2.2
Technical lemmas

For
1\leq p<\infty
, we adopt the standard convention of identifying
a function
u\in L^{p}([0,\tau];{\mathbb{R}}^{n})
with a chosen representative of their equivalence class.
Consequently, we identify every
u\in W^{1,p}([0,\tau];\mathbb{R}^{n})
with its (unique) continuous representative. Therefore, we have

W^{1,p}([0,\tau];\mathbb{R}^{n})\hookrightarrow C^{0}([0,\tau];\mathbb{R}^{n})
and

\left.\begin{array}[]{ll}\|u\|_{C^{0}}\leq(\tau+1)\|u\|_{1,1},\quad\forall u\in W^{1,1}([0,\tau];\mathbb{R}^{n}),\\
\|u\|_{C^{0}}\leq(\sqrt{\tau}+1/\sqrt{\tau})\|u\|_{1,2},\quad\forall u\in W^{1,2}([0,\tau];\mathbb{R}^{n}).\end{array}\right\}

(2.3)

Lemma 2.1

(
[
16
, Lemma 2.1]
)
.

Given positive numbers
c>0
and
C_{1}\geq 1
, choose positive
parameters
0<\varepsilon<\delta<\frac{2c}{3C_{1}}
. Then:

(i)

There exists
a
C^{\infty}
function
\psi_{\varepsilon,\delta}:[0,\infty)\to{\mathbb{R}}

such that:
\psi^{\prime}_{\varepsilon,\delta}>0
and
\psi_{\varepsilon,\delta}
is convex on

(\varepsilon,\infty)
,
\psi_{\varepsilon,\delta}
vanishes in
[0,\varepsilon)
and is equal to
the affine function
\kappa t+\varrho_{0}
on
[\delta,\infty)
,
where
\kappa>0
and
\varrho_{0}<0
are suitable constants.

(ii)

There exists a
C^{\infty}
function

\phi_{\mu,b}:[0,\infty)\to{\mathbb{R}}
depending on parameters
\mu>0
and

b>0
, such that:
\phi_{\mu,b}
is nondecreasing and concave (and
hence
\phi_{\mu,b}^{\prime\prime}\leq 0
), and equal to the affine
function
\mu t-\mu\delta
on
[0,\delta]
, and equal to constant

b>0
on
[\frac{2c}{3C_{1}},\infty)
.

(iii)

Under the above assumptions,
\psi_{\varepsilon,\delta}(t)+\phi_{\mu,b}(t)-b=\kappa t+\varrho_{0}
for any
t\geq\frac{2c}{3C_{1}}
(and hence for
t\geq\frac{2c}{3}
). Moreover,

\psi_{\varepsilon,\delta}(t)+\phi_{\mu,b}(t)-b\geq-\mu\delta-b
for any
t\geq 0
, and
\psi_{\varepsilon,\delta}(t)+\phi_{\mu,b}(t)-b=-\mu\delta-b
if and only if
t=0
.

(iv)

Under the assumptions (i)-(ii), suppose that the constant
\mu>0
satisfies

\displaystyle\mu+\frac{\varrho_{0}}{\delta-\varepsilon}>0\quad\hbox{and}\quad\mu\delta+b+\varrho_{0}>0.

(2.4)

Then
\psi_{\varepsilon,\delta}(t)+\phi_{\mu,b}(t)-b\leq\kappa t+\varrho_{0}
for all
t\geq\varepsilon
, and

\psi_{\varepsilon,\delta}(t)+\phi_{\mu,b}(t)-b\leq\kappa t+\varrho_{0}
for all
t\in[0,\varepsilon]
if
\kappa\geq\mu
.

Assumption 2.2
.

For a real
\tau>0
, a topology space
\Lambda
,
let
L:\Lambda\times[0,\tau]\times U\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be a continuous function such that
the following partial derivatives

\partial_{t}L(\cdot),\;\partial_{q}L(\cdot),\;\partial_{v}L(\cdot),\;\partial_{tv}L(\cdot),\;\partial_{vt}L(\cdot),\;\partial_{qv}L(\cdot),\;\partial_{vq}L(\cdot),\;\partial_{qq}L(\cdot),\;\partial_{vv}L(\cdot)

exist and depend continuously on
(\lambda,t,q,v)\in\Lambda\times[0,\tau]\times U\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.
Moreover,
\Lambda\times[0,\tau]\times U\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\ni(\lambda,t,q,v)\mapsto{L}(\lambda,t,q,v)
is
convex with respect to
v
, that is, the second partial derivative
\partial_{vv}{L}(\lambda,t,q,v)
is positive semi-definite
as a quadratic form.

Under Assumption
2.2
let
E
be a real orthogonal matrix of order
n
such that
(EU)\cap U\neq\emptyset
.
Consider the Lagrangian boundary value problem on
U
:

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left(\partial_{v}L_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))\right)-\partial_{q}L_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))=0,

(2.5)

\displaystyle E(x(0))=x(\tau)\quad\hbox{and}\quad(E^{\top})^{-1}\left[\partial_{v}L_{\lambda}(0,x(0),\dot{x}(0))\right]=\partial_{v}L_{\lambda}(\tau,x(\tau),\dot{x}(\tau)).

(2.6)

Assumption 2.3
.

For a real
\tau>0
, a topological space
\Lambda
,
a real orthogonal matrix
E
of order
n
, and an
E
-invariant path-connected
open subset
U\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
let
L:\Lambda\times\mathbb{R}\times U\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be a continuous function such that
the following partial derivatives

\partial_{t}L(\cdot),\;\partial_{q}L(\cdot),\;\partial_{v}L(\cdot),\;\partial_{tv}L(\cdot),\;\partial_{vt}L(\cdot),\;\partial_{qv}L(\cdot),\;\partial_{vq}L(\cdot),\;\partial_{qq}L(\cdot),\;\partial_{vv}L(\cdot)

exist and depend continuously on
(\lambda,t,q,v)\in\Lambda\times[0,\tau]\times U\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.
Moreover, for each
(\lambda,t,q)\in\Lambda\times{\mathbb{R}}\times U
,
{L}(\lambda,t,q,v)
is
convex in
v
, and satisfies

\displaystyle L(\lambda,t+\tau,Eq,Ev)=L(\lambda,t,q,v)\quad\forall(\lambda,t,q,v)\in\Lambda\times{\mathbb{R}}\times U\times\mathbb{R}^{n}.

(2.7)

Lemma 2.4
.

Under Assumption
2.2
, let the topological space
\Lambda
be
either
compact
or
sequentially compact
. Suppose that for some real
\rho>0

the function
B^{n}_{\rho}(0)\ni v\mapsto{L}_{\lambda}(t,q,v)
is strictly convex
for each
(\lambda,t,q)\in\Lambda\times[0,\tau]\times U
.
Then for any given real
0<\rho_{0}<\rho
there exists a continuous function

\tilde{L}:\Lambda\times[0,\tau]\times U\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
and a constant
\kappa>0
satisfying the following properties:

(i)

\tilde{L}
is equal to
L
on
\Lambda\times[0,\tau]\times U\times B^{n}_{\rho_{0}}(0)
.

(ii)

The partial derivatives

\partial_{t}\tilde{L}(\cdot),\;\partial_{q}\tilde{L}(\cdot),\;\partial_{v}\tilde{L}(\cdot),\;\partial_{tv}\tilde{L}(\cdot),\;\partial_{vt}\tilde{L}(\cdot),\;\partial_{qv}\tilde{L}(\cdot),\;\partial_{vq}\tilde{L}(\cdot),\;\partial_{qq}\tilde{L}(\cdot),\;\partial_{vv}\tilde{L}(\cdot)

exist and depend continuously on
(\lambda,t,q,v)
.

(iii)

\mathbb{R}^{n}\ni v\mapsto\tilde{L}_{\lambda}(t,q,v)

is strictly convex for each
(\lambda,t,q)\in\Lambda\times[0,\tau]\times U
,
that is, the second partial derivative
\partial_{vv}\tilde{L}_{\lambda}(t,x,v)

is positive definite as a quadratic form.

(iv)

For any given compact subset
S\subset U
,
there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\tilde{L}_{\lambda}(t,q,v)\geq\kappa|v|^{2}-C,\quad\forall(\lambda,t,q,v)\in\Lambda\times[0,\tau]\times S\times\mathbb{R}^{n}.

(v)

For each
(\lambda,t,q)
, if
L(\lambda,t,q,v)
is even in
v
then

\tilde{L}_{\lambda}(t,q,v)
can be required to be even in
v
.

(vi)

If
U
is a symmetric open neighborhood of the origin in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, and for each
(\lambda,t)

the function
L(\lambda,t,q,v)
is even in
(q,v)
then
\tilde{L}(\lambda,t,q,v)
can be also required to be even in
(q,v)
.

(vii)

For each
(\lambda,q)
, if
L(\lambda,t,q,v)
is even in
(t,v)
, then
\tilde{L}(\lambda,t,q,v)
can be chosen to be even in
(t,v)
.

(viii)

If
L
is independent of time
t
, so is
\tilde{L}
.

(ix)

If Assumption
2.2
is replaced by Assumption
2.3
,
the function
\tilde{L}
given by (
2.8
) may be replaced by

\tilde{L}:\Lambda\times{\mathbb{R}}\times U\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}},\;(\lambda,t,q,v)\mapsto{L}(\lambda,t,q,v)+\psi_{\rho_{0},\rho_{1}}(|v|^{2}),

which also satisfies (
2.7
) because
E
is
a real orthogonal matrix.

Proof.

Fix a positive real
\rho_{1}\in(\rho_{0},\rho)
. By Lemma
2.1
(
[
16
, Lemma 2.1]
)
we have a
C^{\infty}
convex function
\psi_{\rho_{0},\rho_{1}}:[0,\infty)\to{\mathbb{R}}

such that
\psi^{\prime}_{\rho_{0},\rho_{1}}(t)>0
for

t\in(\rho_{0}^{2},\infty)
,
\psi_{\rho_{0},\rho_{1}}(t)=0
for
t\in[0,\rho_{0}^{2})
and

\psi_{\rho_{0},\rho_{1}}(t)=\kappa t+\varrho_{0}
for
t\in[\rho_{1}^{2},\infty)
,
where
\kappa>0
and
\varrho_{0}<0
are suitable constants. We conclude

\tilde{L}:\Lambda\times[0,\tau]\times U\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}},\;(\lambda,t,q,v)\mapsto{L}(\lambda,t,q,v)+\psi_{\rho_{0},\rho_{1}}(|v|^{2})

(2.8)

to satisfy the desired requirements. By Assumption
2.2
and the choice of
\psi_{\rho_{0},\rho_{1}}

it is clear that
\tilde{L}
satisfies (i)-(ii).

In order to see that
\tilde{L}
satisfies (iii), note that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t\partial s}\psi_{\rho_{0},\rho_{1}}(|v+su+tu|^{2})\Bigm|_{s=0,t=0}=2\psi^{\prime}_{\rho_{0},\rho_{1}}(|v|^{2})|u|^{2}+4\psi^{\prime\prime}_{\rho_{0},\rho_{1}}(|v|^{2})\bigl(({v},{u})_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\bigr)^{2}

(cf. the proof of
[
16
, Lemma 2.1]
) and therefore

\partial_{vv}\tilde{L}(\lambda,t,q,v)[u,u]=\partial_{vv}{L}(\lambda,t,q,v)[u,u]+2\psi^{\prime}_{\rho_{0},\rho_{1}}(|v|^{2})|u|^{2}+4\psi^{\prime\prime}_{\rho_{0},\rho_{1}}(|v|^{2})\bigl(({v},{u})_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\bigr)^{2}.

Since
\psi^{\prime}_{\rho_{0},\rho_{1}}\geq 0
and
\psi^{\prime\prime}_{\rho_{0},\rho_{1}}(t)\geq 0
,
by Assumption
2.2
we deduce

\partial_{vv}\tilde{L}(\lambda,t,q,v)[u,u]\geq\partial_{vv}{L}(\lambda,t,q,v)[u,u]>0\quad\hbox{for $|v|<\rho$ and $u\neq 0$}.

Moreover, if
|v|>\rho_{1}
and
u\neq 0
we get
\partial_{vv}\tilde{L}(\lambda,t,q,v)[u,u]\geq 2\kappa|u|^{2}

since
\partial_{vv}{L}(\lambda,t,q,v)[u,u]\geq 0
.
Hence
\tilde{L}(\lambda,t,q,v)
is strictly convex in
v
.

Let us prove (iv).
Fixing
v_{0}\in B^{n}_{\rho}(0)\setminus B^{n}_{\rho_{1}}(0)
,
by
[
7
, Proposition 1.2.10]
we get

\displaystyle\tilde{L}(\lambda,t,q,v)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\tilde{L}(\lambda,t,q,v_{0})+\partial_{v}\tilde{L}(\lambda,t,q,v_{0})[v-v_{0}]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle{L}(\lambda,t,q,v_{0})+\partial_{v}{L}(\lambda,t,q,v_{0})[v-v_{0}]+2\psi^{\prime}_{\rho_{0},\rho_{1}}(|v_{0}|^{2})({v},v-v_{0})_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hskip-25.60747pt{L}(\lambda,t,q,v_{0})-\partial_{v}{L}(\lambda,t,q,v_{0})[v_{0}]+\partial_{v}{L}(\lambda,t,q,v_{0})[v]-2\kappa({v},{v}_{0})_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}+2\kappa|v|^{2}

for all
v\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Since
2\kappa|({v},{v}_{0})_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|\leq\kappa|v|^{2}/2+2\kappa|v_{0}|^{2}
and

|\partial_{v}{L}(\lambda,t,q,v_{0})[v]|=\bigg|\sum^{n}_{j=1}\frac{\partial L}{\partial v_{j}}(\lambda,t,q,v_{0})v_{j}\bigg|\leq\frac{1}{\kappa}\sum^{n}_{j=1}\Big|\frac{\partial L}{\partial v_{j}}(\lambda,t,q,v_{0})\Big|^{2}+\frac{\kappa}{4}|v|^{2},

we derive

\displaystyle\tilde{L}(\lambda,t,q,v)\geq{L}(\lambda,t,q,v_{0})-\partial_{v}{L}(\lambda,t,q,v_{0})[v_{0}]-\frac{1}{\kappa}\sum^{n}_{j=1}\Big|\frac{\partial L}{\partial v_{j}}(\lambda,t,q,v_{0})\Big|^{2}-2\kappa|v_{0}|^{2}+\frac{5\kappa}{4}|v|^{2}

for all
v\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Since
\Lambda
is either compact or sequential compact, so is
\Lambda\times[0,\tau]\times S
,
in either case we can always derive that both
L(\lambda,t,q,v_{0})
and
\partial_{v}L(\lambda,t,q,v_{0})
are bounded on

\Lambda\times[0,\tau]\times S
. Therefore there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\tilde{L}(\lambda,t,q,v)\geq\frac{5\kappa}{4}|v|^{2}-C,\quad\forall(\lambda,t,q,v)\in\Lambda\times[0,\tau]\times S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}.

Other conclusions are clear by the above construction.
∎

Assumption 2.5
.

Under Assumption
2.2
, for each
\lambda\in\Lambda
, let
x_{\lambda}:[0,\tau]\to U

be a
C^{2}
path satisfying (
2.5
). Suppose: (i)
\Lambda\times[0,\tau]\ni(\lambda,t)\to x_{\lambda}(t)\in U
and
\Lambda\times[0,\tau]\ni(\lambda,t)\mapsto\dot{x}_{\lambda}(t)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
are continuous;
(ii) for any compact or sequential compact subset
\hat{\Lambda}\subset\Lambda

there exists
\rho>0
such that

\sup\big\{|\dot{x}_{\lambda}(t)|\;\big|\,(\lambda,t)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau]\big\}<\rho

and that
