\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau]\times U\times B^{n}_{\rho}(0)\ni(\lambda,t,q,v)\mapsto{L}_{\lambda}(t,q,v)
is
strictly convex with respect to
v
.

Lemma 2.6
.

Under Assumption
2.5
, the following holds:

(i)

\Lambda\times[0,\tau]\ni(\lambda,t)\mapsto\ddot{x}_{\lambda}(t)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
is continuous.

(ii)

If there exists a sequence
(\lambda_{k})\subset\Lambda
converging to
\mu\in\Lambda

and solutions
x_{k}\in C^{2}([0,\tau],U)\setminus\{x_{\lambda_{k}}\}
of (
2.5
) with
\lambda=\lambda_{k}\in\Lambda
,
k=1,2,\cdots
, such that

\|x_{k}-x_{\lambda_{k}}\|_{C^{1}}\to 0
, then
\|x_{k}-x_{\lambda_{k}}\|_{C^{2}}\to 0
as
k\to\infty
.

Proof.

Step 1
[
Prove (i)
]. Since
x_{\lambda}
is
C^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{vt}L_{\lambda}(t,x_{\lambda}(t),\dot{x}_{\lambda}(t))+\partial_{vq}L_{\lambda}(t,x_{\lambda}(t),\dot{x}_{\lambda}(t))\dot{x}_{\lambda}(t)

\displaystyle+\partial_{vv}L_{\lambda}(t,x_{\lambda}(t),\dot{x}_{\lambda}(t))\ddot{x}_{\lambda}(t)-\partial_{q}L_{\lambda}(t,x_{\lambda}(t),\dot{x}_{\lambda}(t))=0

and therefore

\displaystyle\ddot{x}_{\lambda}(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[\partial_{vv}L_{\lambda}(t,x_{\lambda}(t),\dot{x}_{\lambda}(t))]^{-1}\partial_{q}L_{\lambda}(t,x_{\lambda}(t),\dot{x}_{\lambda}(t))

\displaystyle-[\partial_{vv}L_{\lambda}(t,x_{\lambda}(t),\dot{x}_{\lambda}(t))]^{-1}\partial_{vt}L_{\lambda}(t,x_{\lambda}(t),\dot{x}_{\lambda}(t))

\displaystyle-[\partial_{vv}L_{\lambda}(t,x_{\lambda}(t),\dot{x}_{\lambda}(t))]^{-1}\partial_{vq}L_{\lambda}(t,x_{\lambda}(t),\dot{x}_{\lambda}(t))\dot{x}_{\lambda}(t)

because (ii) in Assumption
2.5
implies that the matrixes
\partial_{vv}L_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))
are positive definite and
therefore invertible.
Moreover, by Assumption
2.2
maps

\displaystyle(\lambda,t,q,v)\mapsto\partial_{vt}L_{\lambda}(t,q,v),\qquad(\lambda,t,q,v)\mapsto\partial_{vq}L_{\lambda}(t,q,v),

\displaystyle(\lambda,t,q,v)\mapsto\partial_{vv}L_{\lambda}(t,q,v),\qquad(\lambda,t,q,v)\mapsto\partial_{q}L_{\lambda}(t,q,v)

are continuous. The desired conclusion may follow from these, (i) in Assumption
2.5
and the above equality directly.

Step 2
[
Prove (ii)
]. Let
\hat{\Lambda}=\{\mu,\lambda_{k}\,|\,k\in\mathbb{N}\}
. It is a
sequential compact subset of
\Lambda
. By the assumption (ii) in Assumption
2.5
there exists
\rho>0
such that

\sup\{|\dot{x}_{\lambda}(t)|\,|\,(\lambda,t)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau]\}<\rho

and that
\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau]\times U\times B^{n}_{\rho}(0)\ni(\lambda,t,q,v)\mapsto{L}_{\lambda}(t,q,v)
is
strictly convex with respect to
v
.
In particular, we may obtain
0<M_{1}<M_{2}<\infty
such that

M_{1}I_{n}\leq\partial_{vv}{L}_{\mu}(t,x_{\mu}(t),\dot{x}_{\mu}(t))\leq M_{2}I_{n},\quad\forall t\in[0,\tau].

Suppose that there exists a sequence
(t_{i})\subset[0,\tau]
such that for each
i=1,2,\cdots
,

\partial_{vv}{L}_{\lambda_{k_{i}}}(t_{i},x_{\lambda_{k_{i}}}(t_{i}),\dot{x}_{\lambda_{k_{i}}}(t_{i}))<\frac{1}{2}M_{1}I_{n}\quad\hbox{or}\quad\partial_{vv}{L}_{\lambda_{k_{i}}}(t_{i},x_{\lambda_{k_{i}}}(t_{i}),\dot{x}_{\lambda_{k_{i}}}(t_{i}))>\frac{1}{2}M_{2}I_{n}.

We can assume
t_{i}\to t_{0}\in[0,\tau]
. By (i) in Assumption
2.5

and the continuity of the map

\Lambda\times[0,\tau]\times U\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\ni(\lambda,t,q,v)\mapsto\partial_{vv}{L}(\lambda,t,q,v)\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n},

we derive

\partial_{vv}{L}_{\mu}(t_{0},x_{\mu}(t_{0}),\dot{x}_{\mu}(t_{0}))\leq\frac{1}{2}M_{1}I_{n}\quad\hbox{or}\quad\partial_{vv}{L}_{\mu}(t_{0},x_{\mu}(t_{0}),\dot{x}_{\mu}(t_{0}))\geq\frac{1}{2}M_{2}I_{n}.

This contradiction shows that if
k
is large enough then

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}M_{1}I_{n}\leq\partial_{vv}{L}_{\lambda_{k}}(t,x_{\lambda_{k}}(t),\dot{x}_{\lambda_{k}}(t))\leq\frac{1}{2}M_{2}I_{n},\quad\forall t\in[0,\tau].

(2.9)

Similarly, since
\|x_{k}-x_{\lambda_{k}}\|_{C^{1}}\to 0
, for sufficiently large
k
we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}M_{1}I_{n}\leq\partial_{vv}{L}_{\lambda_{k}}(t,x_{k}(t),\dot{x}_{k}(t))\leq\frac{1}{2}M_{2}I_{n},\quad\forall t\in[0,\tau].

(2.10)

Note that for each
k\in\mathbb{N}
, both
(\lambda_{k},x_{k})
and
(\lambda_{k},x_{\lambda_{k}})
satisfy
(
2.5
), that is,

\displaystyle\partial_{vv}L_{\lambda_{k}}(t,x_{\lambda_{k}}(t),\dot{x}_{\lambda_{k}}(t))\ddot{x}_{\lambda_{k}}(t)+\partial_{tv}L_{\lambda_{k}}(t,x_{\lambda_{k}}(t),\dot{x}_{\lambda_{k}}(t))

\displaystyle+\partial_{qv}L_{\lambda_{k}}(t,x_{\lambda_{k}}(t),\dot{x}_{\lambda_{k}}(t))\dot{x}_{\lambda_{k}}(t)-\partial_{q}L_{\lambda_{k}}(t,x_{\lambda_{k}}(t),\dot{x}_{\lambda_{k}}(t))=0,

(2.11)

\displaystyle\partial_{vv}L_{\lambda_{k}}(t,x_{k}(t),\dot{x}_{k}(t))\ddot{x}_{k}(t)+\partial_{tv}L_{\lambda_{k}}(t,x_{k}(t),\dot{x}_{k}(t))

\displaystyle+\partial_{qv}L_{\lambda_{k}}(t,x_{k}(t),\dot{x}_{k}(t))\dot{x}_{k}(t)-\partial_{q}L_{\lambda_{k}}(t,x_{k}(t),\dot{x}_{k}(t))=0.

(2.12)

By contradiction, passing to subsequences (if necessary) suppose that there exists
\varepsilon>0
and a sequence
(t_{k})\subset[0,\tau]
converging to
t_{0}

such that
|\ddot{x}_{\lambda_{k}}(t_{k})-\ddot{x}_{k}(t_{k})|\geq\varepsilon
for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
. Then
for each large
k
, (
2.9
) and (
2.10
) imply that matrixes

\left(\partial_{vv}L_{\lambda_{k}}(t_{k},x_{\lambda_{k}}(t_{k}),\dot{x}_{\lambda_{k}}(t_{k}))\right)\quad\hbox{and}\quad\left(\partial_{vv}L_{\lambda_{k}}(t_{k},x_{\lambda_{k}}(t_{k}),\dot{x}_{\lambda_{k}}(t_{k}))\right)

are invertible. It follows from (
2.11
) and (
2.12
) that for each large
k
,

\displaystyle\varepsilon

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\ddot{x}_{\lambda_{k}}(t_{k})-\ddot{x}_{k}(t_{k})|

(2.13)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigl|\left(\partial_{vv}L_{\lambda_{k}}(t_{k},x_{\lambda_{k}}(t_{k}),\dot{x}_{\lambda_{k}}(t_{k}))\right)^{-1}\partial_{tv}L_{\lambda_{k}}(t_{k},x_{\lambda_{k}}(t_{k}),\dot{x}_{\lambda_{k}}(t_{k}))

\displaystyle-\left(\partial_{vv}L_{\lambda_{k}}(t_{k},x_{k}(t_{k}),\dot{x}_{k}(t_{k}))\right)^{-1}\partial_{tv}L_{\lambda_{k}}(t_{k},x_{k}(t_{k}),\dot{x}_{k}(t_{k}))

\displaystyle+\left(\partial_{vv}L_{\lambda_{k}}(t_{k},x_{\lambda_{k}}(t_{k}),\dot{x}_{\lambda_{k}}(t_{k}))\right)^{-1}\partial_{qv}L_{\lambda_{k}}(t_{k},x_{\lambda_{k}}(t_{k}),\dot{x}_{\lambda_{k}}(t_{k}))\dot{x}_{\lambda_{k}}(t_{k})

\displaystyle-\left(\partial_{vv}L_{\lambda_{k}}(t_{k},x_{k}(t_{k}),\dot{x}_{k}(t_{k}))\right)^{-1}\partial_{qv}L_{\lambda_{k}}(t_{k},x_{k}(t_{k}),\dot{x}_{k}(t_{k}))\dot{x}_{k}(t_{k})

\displaystyle-\left(\partial_{vv}L_{\lambda_{k}}(t_{k},x_{\lambda_{k}}(t_{k}),\dot{x}_{\lambda_{k}}(t_{k}))\right)^{-1}\partial_{q}L_{\lambda_{k}}(t_{k},x_{\lambda_{k}}(t_{k}),\dot{x}_{\lambda_{k}}(t_{k}))

\displaystyle+\left(\partial_{vv}L_{\lambda_{k}}(t_{k},x_{k}(t_{k}),\dot{x}_{k}(t_{k}))\right)^{-1}\partial_{q}L_{\lambda_{k}}(t_{k},x_{k}(t_{k}),\dot{x}_{k}(t_{k}))\bigr|.

By (i) in Assumption
2.5
,
|x_{\lambda_{k}}(t_{k})-x_{\mu}(t_{0})|\to 0
and
|\dot{x}_{\lambda_{k}}(t_{k})-\dot{x}_{\mu}(t_{0})|\to 0
. Moreover

\displaystyle|x_{k}(t_{k})-x_{\mu}(t_{0})|\leq|x_{\lambda_{k}}(t_{k})-x_{\mu}(t_{0})|+\|x_{\lambda_{k}}-x_{k}\|_{C^{0}}\to 0,

\displaystyle|\dot{x}_{k}(t_{k})-\dot{x}_{\mu}(t_{0})|\leq|\dot{x}_{\lambda_{k}}(t_{k})-\dot{x}_{\mu}(t_{0})|+\|\dot{x}_{\lambda_{k}}-\dot{x}_{k}\|_{C^{0}}\to 0.

Letting
k\to\infty
in (
2.13
), by Assumption
2.2
we get
\varepsilon\leq 0
.
This contradiction shows
\|\ddot{x}_{\lambda_{k}}-\ddot{x}_{k}\|_{C^{0}}\to 0
.
Combing the condition
\|x_{k}-x_{\lambda_{k}}\|_{C^{1}}\to 0
, we arrive at

\|x_{k}-x_{\lambda_{k}}\|_{C^{2}}\to 0
as
k\to\infty
.
∎

Note
: the continuity of
\partial_{tv}L
is used in the proof of Lemma
2.6
.

Under Assumption
2.5
, for a given compact or sequential compact subset
\hat{\Lambda}\subset\Lambda

there exist positive numbers
0<\rho_{0}<\rho
such that

\rho_{00}:=\sup\big\{|\dot{x}_{\lambda}(t)|\;\big|\,(\lambda,t)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau]\big\}<\rho_{0}<\rho

and that

\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau]\times U\times B^{n}_{\rho}(0)\ni(\lambda,t,q,v)\mapsto{L}_{\lambda}(t,q,v)

is strictly convex with respect to
v
.
By Lemma
2.4
we have an associated continuous function

\tilde{L}:\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau]\times U\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
.
Since a subset of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
is compact if and only if it is sequential compact,
whether
\hat{\Lambda}
is compact or sequential compact
the continuous map
\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau]\ni(\lambda,t)\mapsto x_{\lambda}(t)\in\mathbb{R}^{n}

has a compact image set and therefore
we can choose
\delta>0
so small that the compact subset

S:=Cl\bigl(\cup_{\lambda\in\hat{\Lambda}}\cup_{t\in[0,\tau]}(x_{\lambda}(t)+B^{n}_{\delta}(0))\bigr)

is contained in
U
.
Lemma
2.4
(iv) yields a constant
C>0
such that

\tilde{L}_{\lambda}(t,q,v)\geq\kappa|v|^{2}-C,\quad\forall(\lambda,t,q,v)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau]\times S\times\mathbb{R}^{n}.

Define

\hat{L}:\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau]\times B^{n}_{\delta}(0)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}},\;(\lambda,t,q,v)\mapsto\tilde{L}(\lambda,t,q+x_{\lambda}(t),v+\dot{x}_{\lambda}(t))

(2.14)

and
\hat{L}_{\lambda}(\cdot)=\hat{L}(\lambda,\cdot)
for
\lambda\in\hat{\Lambda}
.
By Lemmas
2.4
,
2.6
, we obtain:

Lemma 2.7
.

(A)

The function
\hat{L}
is continuous; partial derivatives

\partial_{t}\hat{L}(\cdot),\;\partial_{q}\hat{L}(\cdot),\;\partial_{v}\hat{L}(\cdot),\;\partial_{tv}\hat{L}(\cdot),\;\partial_{vt}\hat{L}(\cdot),\;\partial_{qv}\hat{L}(\cdot),\;\partial_{vq}\hat{L}(\cdot),\;\partial_{qq}\hat{L}(\cdot),\;\partial_{vv}\hat{L}(\cdot)

exist and depend continuously on
(\lambda,t,q,v)
.

(B)

For each
(\lambda,t,q)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau]\times B^{n}_{\delta}(0)
,
\hat{L}_{\lambda}(t,q,v)
is
strictly convex in
v
, and

\hat{L}_{\lambda}(t,q,v)\geq\kappa|v+\dot{x}_{\lambda}(t)|^{2}-C\geq\frac{\kappa}{2}|v|^{2}-\kappa\rho^{2}-C

(2.15)

for all
(\lambda,t,q,v)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau]\times B^{n}_{\delta}(0)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.

(C)

For each
(\lambda,t,q)
, if each
x_{\lambda}
is constant
and
L(\lambda,t,q,v)
is even in
v
then

\hat{L}_{\lambda}(t,q,v)
can be required to be even in
v
.

(D)

If
U
is a symmetric open neighborhood of the origin in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
x_{\lambda}\equiv 0\;\forall\lambda
, and for each
(\lambda,t)

the function
L(\lambda,t,q,v)
is even in
(q,v)
, then

\hat{L}(\lambda,t,q,v)
can be also required to be even in
(q,v)
.

(E)

For each
(\lambda,q)
, if
L(\lambda,t,q,v)
is even in
(t,v)
,
and
x_{\lambda}\equiv 0\;\forall\lambda
,
then
\hat{L}(\lambda,t,q,v)
can be chosen to be even in
(t,v)
.

(F)

If
L
is independent of time
t
, so is
\hat{L}
.

(G)

If Assumption
2.2
is replaced by Assumption
2.3
, and
Ex_{\lambda}(t)=x_{\lambda}(t)\;\forall t\in\hat{\Lambda}
,
then the function
\hat{L}
given by (
2.14
) may be replaced by

\hat{L}:\hat{\Lambda}\times{\mathbb{R}}\times U\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}},\;(\lambda,t,q,v)\mapsto{L}(\lambda,t,q+x_{\lambda}(t),v+\dot{x}_{\lambda}(t))+\psi_{\rho_{0},\rho_{1}}(|v+\dot{x}_{\lambda}(t)|^{2}),

which also satisfies (
2.7
) because
E
is
a real orthogonal matrix.

Remark 2.8
.

For a given positive number
\rho_{0}>0
, replacing

\tilde{L}^{\ast}
and
\iota
by
\hat{L}
and
\delta
in the proof of Lemma
3.8

we may obtain a continuous function
\check{L}:\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau]\times B^{n}_{3\delta/4}(0)\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}}

satisfying (L1)-(L6) in Lemma
3.8
with
\tilde{L}^{\ast}=\hat{L}
and
\iota=\delta
,
and Lemma
3.8
(L0) without
\partial_{t}\check{L}(\cdot)
.
Because of Remark
3.12
, as in the proofs of Theorems
1.4
,
1.5
,
1.6

in Section
3
we may obtain the corresponding versions of these theorems under weaker Assumptions
2.2
,
2.5
.
Similarly, when
M
is an open subset in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
the conditions in Theorem
1.9
may be weakened suitably.

3
Proofs of Theorems
1.4
,
1.5
,
1.6
and
1.9

3.1
Proofs of Theorems
1.4
,
1.5
,
1.6

3.1.1
Reduction to Euclidean spaces

Since
\gamma_{\mu}(0)\neq\gamma_{\mu}(\tau)
, we may choose the
C^{6}
Riemannian metric
g
on
M
so that

S_{0}
(resp.
S_{1}
) is totally geodesic near
\gamma_{\mu}(0)
(resp.
\gamma_{\mu}(\tau)
).
(Indeed, by the definition of submanifolds there exists a coordinate chart
(U,\varphi)
around

\gamma_{\mu}(0)
(resp.
\gamma_{\mu}(\tau)
) on
M
such that
\varphi(S_{0}\cap U)=\varphi(U)\cap V_{0}

(resp.
\varphi(S_{1}\cap U)=\varphi(U)\cap V_{1}
) for some linear subspace
V_{0}
(resp.
V_{1}
) in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
.
Then extending the pullback of the standard metric on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
to
U
yields a required metric.)
There exists a fibrewise convex open neighborhood
\mathcal{U}(0_{TM})
of the zero section of
TM

such that the exponential map of
g
gives rise to
C^{5}
immersion

\mathbb{F}:\mathcal{U}(0_{TM})\to M\times M,\;(q,v)\mapsto(q,\exp_{q}(v)),

(3.1)

(cf. Appendix
A
). By (
A.4
),

d\mathbb{F}(q,0_{q}):T_{(q,0_{q})}\to T_{(q,q)}(M\times M)=T_{q}M\times T_{q}M
is an isomorphism for each
q\in M
.
Since
\mathbb{F}
is injective on the closed subset
0_{TM}\subset TM
, it follows from Exercise 7 in

[
10
, page 41]
that
\mathbb{F}|_{\mathcal{W}(0_{TM})}
is a
C^{5}
embedding of some smaller open neighborhood

\mathcal{W}(0_{TM})\subset\mathcal{U}(0_{TM})
of
0_{TM}
.
Note that
\mathbb{F}(0_{TM})
is equal to the diagonal
\Delta_{M}
in
M\times M
, and that
\gamma_{\mu}([0,\tau])
is compact.
We may choose a number
\iota>0
such that

(
\clubsuit
)

the closure
\bar{\bf U}_{3\iota}(\gamma_{\mu}([0,\tau]))
of

{\bf U}_{3\iota}(\gamma_{\mu}([0,\tau])):=\{p\in M\,|\,d_{g}(p,\gamma_{\mu}([0,\tau]))<3\iota\}
is
a compact neighborhood of
\gamma_{\mu}([0,\tau])
in
M
, and

\bar{\bf U}_{3\iota}(\gamma_{\mu}([0,\tau]))\times\bar{\bf U}_{3\iota}(\gamma_{\mu}([0,\tau]))

is contained in the image of
\mathbb{F}|_{\mathcal{W}(0_{TM})}
;

(
\spadesuit
)

\{(q,v)\in TM\,|\,q\in\bar{\bf U}_{3\iota}(\gamma_{\mu}([0,\tau])),\;|v|_{q}\leq 3\iota\}\subset\mathcal{W}(0_{TM})
.

Then
3\iota
is less than the injectivity
radius of
g
at each point on
\bar{\bf U}_{3\iota}(\gamma_{\mu}([0,\tau]))
.
Let us take a path
\overline{\gamma}\in C^{7}([0,\tau];M)
such that

\displaystyle\overline{\gamma}(0)=\gamma_{\mu}(0),\quad\overline{\gamma}(\tau)=\gamma_{\mu}(\tau),\quad\hbox{and}\quad{\rm dist}_{g}(\gamma_{\mu}(t),\overline{\gamma}(t))<\iota\;\forall t\in[0,\tau].

(3.2)

We first assume:

\displaystyle d_{g}(\gamma_{\lambda}(t),\overline{\gamma}(t))<\iota,\quad\forall(\lambda,t)\in\Lambda\times[0,\tau].

(3.3)

(For cases of Theorems
1.4
,
1.6
,
by contradiction we may use nets to prove that (
3.3
)
is satisfied after shrinking
\Lambda
toward
\mu
.)
Then (
3.2
) and (
3.3
) imply

\displaystyle d_{g}(\gamma_{\lambda}(t),\gamma_{\mu}([0,\tau]))\leq d_{g}(\gamma_{\lambda}(t),\gamma_{\mu}(t))<2\iota,\quad\forall(\lambda,t)\in\Lambda\times[0,\tau].

(3.4)

Using a unit orthogonal parallel
C^{5}
frame field along

\overline{\gamma}
,
[0,\tau]\ni t\mapsto(e_{1}(t),\cdots,e_{n}(t))
, we get a
C^{5}
map

\displaystyle\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}:[0,\tau]\times B^{n}_{2\iota}(0)\to M,\;(t,x)\mapsto\exp_{\overline{\gamma}(t)}\Big(\sum^{n}_{i=1}x_{i}e_{i}(t)\Big)

(3.5)

(Note that the tangent map
d\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}:T([0,\tau]\times B^{n}_{2\iota}(0))\to TM
is
C^{4}
.)
By Step 1 in
[
16
, §4]
there exist two linear subspaces
of
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
,
V_{0}
and
V_{1}
, such that
v\in V_{0}
(resp.
v\in V_{1}
) if and only if

\sum^{n}_{k=1}v_{k}e_{k}(0)\in T_{\gamma(0)}S_{0}
(resp.
\sum^{n}_{k=1}v_{k}e_{k}(\tau)\in T_{\gamma(0)}S_{1}
).
By
[
28
, Theorem 4.2]
,

C^{1}_{S_{0}\times S_{1}}([0,\tau];M)
is a
C^{4}
Banach manifold;
and it follows from
[
28
, Theorem 4.3]
that the map

\displaystyle\Phi_{\overline{\gamma}}:C^{1}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}([0,\tau];B^{n}_{2\iota}(0))\to C^{1}_{S_{0}\times S_{1}}([0,\tau];M)

(3.6)

defined by
\Phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(\xi)(t)=\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(t,\xi(t))

gives a
C^{2}
coordinate chart around
\overline{\gamma}
on
C^{1}_{S_{0}\times S_{1}}([0,\tau];M)
,
where

C^{k}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}([0,\tau];B^{n}_{2\iota}(0))=\{\xi\in C^{k}([0,\tau];B^{n}_{2\iota}(0))\,|\,\xi(0)\in V_{0},\;\xi(\tau)\in V_{1}\}\quad\hbox{with $k\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}$}.

Moreover, it is clear that

\Phi_{\overline{\gamma}}\left(C^{1}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}([0,\tau];B^{n}_{2\iota}(0))\right)=\big\{\gamma\in C^{1}_{S_{0}\times S_{1}}([0,\tau],M)\,\big|\,\sup_{t}{\rm dist}_{g}(\gamma(t),\overline{\gamma}(t))<2\iota\big\}.

(
Note
:
\Phi_{\overline{\gamma}}
also defines an at least
C^{1}
map from
C^{2}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}([0,\tau];B^{n}_{2\iota}(0))
to

C^{2}_{S_{0}\times S_{1}}([0,\tau];M)
.)

By (
3.3
), for each
\lambda\in\Lambda
there exists a unique map

{\bf u}_{\lambda}:[0,\tau]\to B^{n}_{\iota}(0)
such that

\gamma_{\lambda}(t)=\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(t,{\bf u}_{\lambda}(t))=\exp_{\overline{\gamma}(t)}\Big(\sum^{n}_{i=1}{\bf u}_{\lambda}^{i}(t)e_{i}(t)\Big),\quad t\in[0,\tau].

Clearly,
{\bf u}_{\lambda}
satisfies the first assertion of the following lemma,
whose proof will be given in Appendix
A
.

Lemma 3.1
.

{\bf u}_{\mu}(0)=0={\bf u}_{\mu}(\tau)
,
{\bf u}_{\lambda}\in C^{2}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}([0,\tau];B^{n}_{\iota}(0))
, and

(\lambda,t)\mapsto{\bf u}_{\lambda}(t)\quad\hbox{and}\quad(\lambda,t)\mapsto\dot{\bf u}_{\lambda}(t)

are continuous as maps from
\Lambda\times[0,\tau]
to
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.

Define
L^{\ast}:\Lambda\times[0,\tau]\times B^{n}_{2\iota}(0)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
by

\displaystyle L^{\ast}(\lambda,t,q,v)=L^{\ast}_{\lambda}(t,q,v)=L_{\lambda}\left(t,\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(t,q),D_{t}\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(t,q)+D_{q}\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(t,q)[v]\right)

(3.7)

Since
\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}
is
C^{5}
, by Assumption
1.1
,
L^{\ast}

is
C^{2}
with respect to
(t,q,v)
and strictly convex with respect to
v
, and
all its partial derivatives also depend continuously on
(\lambda,t,q,v)
.
Moreover,
{\bf u}_{\lambda}
solves the following boundary problem:

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\big(\partial_{v}L^{\ast}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))\big)-\partial_{q}{L}^{\ast}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))=0,

\displaystyle\left.\begin{array}[]{ll}x\in C^{2}([0,\tau];B^{n}_{\iota}(0)),\;(x(0),x(\tau))\in V_{0}\times V_{1}\quad\hbox{and}\\
\partial_{v}{L}^{\ast}_{\lambda}(0,x(0),\dot{x}(0))[v_{0}]=\partial_{v}{L}^{\ast}_{\lambda}(\tau,x(\tau),\dot{x}(\tau))[v_{1}]\\
\qquad\forall(v_{0},v_{1})\in V_{0}\times V_{1}.\end{array}\right\}

By Lemmas
2.6
,
3.1
we directly obtain:

Lemma 3.2
.

\Lambda\times[0,\tau]\ni(\lambda,t)\mapsto\ddot{\bf u}_{\lambda}(t)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}

is continuous.

(
Note
: This result is necessary for us to derive that
\partial_{vt}\tilde{L}^{\ast}
is continuous in Proposition
3.3
.)

Define
\tilde{L}^{\ast}:\Lambda\times[0,\tau]\times B^{n}_{\iota}(0)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
by

\tilde{L}^{\ast}(\lambda,t,q,v)=\tilde{L}^{\ast}_{\lambda}(t,q,v)={L}^{\ast}(\lambda,t,q+{\bf u}_{\lambda}(t),v+\dot{\bf u}_{\lambda}(t)).

(3.9)

Then

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\tilde{L}^{\ast}(\lambda,t,q,v)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{t}{L}^{\ast}(\lambda,t,q+{\bf u}_{\lambda}(t),v+\dot{\bf u}_{\lambda}(t))

\displaystyle+\partial_{q}{L}^{\ast}(\lambda,t,q+{\bf u}_{\lambda}(t),v+\dot{\bf u}_{\lambda}(t))\dot{\bf u}_{\lambda}(t)

\displaystyle+\partial_{v}{L}^{\ast}(\lambda,t,q+{\bf u}_{\lambda}(t),v+\dot{\bf u}_{\lambda}(t))\ddot{\bf u}_{\lambda}(t),

\displaystyle\partial_{q}\tilde{L}^{\ast}(\lambda,t,q,v)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{q}{L}^{\ast}(\lambda,t,q+{\bf u}_{\lambda}(t),v+\dot{\bf u}_{\lambda}(t))

\displaystyle\partial_{v}\tilde{L}^{\ast}(\lambda,t,q,v)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{v}{L}^{\ast}(\lambda,t,q+{\bf u}_{\lambda}(t),v+\dot{\bf u}_{\lambda}(t)).

By these and Lemmas
3.1
,
3.2

it is not hard to see that
\tilde{L}^{\ast}
satisfies the following:

Proposition 3.3
.

(a)

\tilde{L}^{\ast}
is continuous, and the following partial derivatives

\partial_{t}\tilde{L}^{\ast}(\cdot),\;\partial_{q}\tilde{L}^{\ast}(\cdot),\;\partial_{v}\tilde{L}^{\ast}(\cdot),\;\partial_{tv}\tilde{L}^{\ast}(\cdot),\;\partial_{vt}\tilde{L}^{\ast}(\cdot),\;\partial_{qv}\tilde{L}^{\ast}(\cdot),\;\partial_{vq}\tilde{L}^{\ast}(\cdot),\;\partial_{qq}\tilde{L}^{\ast}(\cdot),\;\partial_{vv}\tilde{L}^{\ast}(\cdot)

exist and depend continuously on
(\lambda,t,q,v)
.

(b)

For each
(\lambda,t,q)\in\Lambda\times[0,\tau]\times B^{n}_{\iota}(0)
,
\tilde{L}^{\ast}_{\lambda}(t,q,v)
is
strictly convex in
v
.

Clearly,
\tilde{L}^{\ast}
satisfies Assumption
2.2
, and Assumption
2.5
with
x_{\lambda}\equiv 0\;\forall\lambda
.

Remark 3.4
.

Actually, for our next arguments in this section it suffices that
\tilde{L}^{\ast}
satisfies (a) and the following weaker condition:

(b’)

\tilde{L}^{\ast}(\lambda,t,q,v)
is
convex in
v
, and for any compact or sequential compact subset
\hat{\Lambda}\subset\Lambda
there exists
\rho>0
such that

\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau]\times B^{n}_{\iota}(0)\times B^{n}_{\rho}(0)\ni(\lambda,t,q,v)\mapsto\tilde{L}^{\ast}(\lambda,t,q,v)
is
strictly convex with respect to
v
.

This means: In Assumption
1.1
we may only require that

L
is fiberwise convex; but in Assumption
1.2

we need to add the condition:
for any compact or sequential compact subset
\hat{\Lambda}\subset\Lambda
there exist
0<\rho_{0}<\rho

such that
\sup\{|\dot{\gamma}_{\lambda}(t)|_{g}\,|\,(\lambda,t)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau]\}<\rho_{0}
and

L
is fiberwise strictly convex in
(\lambda,t,q,v)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau]\times TM\,|\,|v|_{g}<\rho\}
.

The condition (a) in Proposition
3.3
assures that each functional

\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda}:C^{1}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}([0,\tau];B^{n}_{\iota}(0))\to{\mathbb{R}},\;x\mapsto\int^{1}_{0}\tilde{L}^{\ast}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))dt

(3.10)

is
C^{2}
, and satisfies

\tilde{\cal E}^{\ast}_{\lambda}(x)={\cal E}_{\lambda}\left(\Phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(x+{\bf u}_{\lambda})\right)\;\forall x\in C^{1}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}([0,\tau];B^{n}_{\iota}(0))\quad\hbox{and}\quad d\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda}(0)=0.

(3.11)

Hence for each
\lambda\in\Lambda
,

x\in C^{1}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}([0,\tau];B^{n}_{\iota}(0))
satisfies

d\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda}(x)=0
if and only if
\gamma:=\Phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(x+{\bf u}_{\lambda})
satisfies

d{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}(\gamma)=0
; and in this case
m^{-}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda},x)=m^{-}({\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda},\gamma)
and

m^{0}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda},x)=m^{0}({\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda},\gamma)
.
In particular, we have

m^{-}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda},0)=m^{-}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda},\gamma_{\lambda})\quad\hbox{and}\quad m^{0}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda},0)=m^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda},\gamma_{\lambda}).

(3.12)

By
[
4
, Proposition 4.2]

the critical points of
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda}

correspond to the solutions of the following boundary problem:

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\big(\partial_{v}\tilde{L}^{\ast}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))\big)-\partial_{q}\tilde{L}^{\ast}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))=0,

(3.13)

\displaystyle\left.\begin{array}[]{ll}x\in C^{2}([0,\tau];B^{n}_{\iota}(0)),\;(x(0),x(\tau))\in V_{0}\times V_{1}\quad\hbox{and}\\
\partial_{v}\tilde{L}^{\ast}_{\lambda}(0,x(0),\dot{x}(0))[v_{0}]=0\quad\forall v_{0}\in V_{0},\\
\partial_{v}\tilde{L}^{\ast}_{\lambda}(\tau,x(\tau),\dot{x}(\tau))[v_{1}]=0\quad\forall v_{1}\in V_{1}.\end{array}\right\}

Let
W^{1,2}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}([0,\tau];B^{n}_{\iota}(0))=\{\xi\in W^{1,2}([0,\tau];B^{n}_{\iota}(0))\,|\,(\xi(0),\xi(\tau))\in V_{0}\times V_{1}\}
.
The following three theorems may be, respectively, viewed as corresponding results of Theorems
1.4
,
1.5
,

1.6
provided that
\tilde{L}^{\ast}
satisfies (a) and (b) in Proposition
3.3
.

Theorem 3.5
.

(I)

(
Necessary condition
):

Suppose that
(\mu,0)\in\Lambda\times C^{1}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}([0,\tau];B^{n}_{\iota}(0))
is a
bifurcation point along sequences of the problem (
3.13
)–(
3.1.1
)
with respect to the trivial branch
\{(\lambda,0)\,|\,\lambda\in\Lambda\}
in
\Lambda\times C^{1}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}([0,\tau];B^{n}_{\iota}(0))
.
Then
m^{0}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\mu},0)>0
.

(II)

(
Sufficient condition
):

Suppose that
\Lambda
is first countable and that there exist two sequences in
\Lambda
converging to
\mu
,
(\lambda_{k}^{-})
and

(\lambda_{k}^{+})
, such that one of the following conditions is satisfied:

(II.1)

For each
k\in\mathbb{N}
, either
0
is not an isolated critical point of
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{+}_{k}}
,
or
0
is not an isolated critical point of
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{-}_{k}}
,
or
0
is an isolated critical point of
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{+}_{k}}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{-}_{k}}
and

C_{m}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{+}_{k}},0;{\bf K})
and
C_{m}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{-}_{k}},0;{\bf K})
are not isomorphic for some Abel group
{\bf K}
and some
m\in\mathbb{Z}
.

(II.2)

For each
k\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists
\lambda\in\{\lambda^{+}_{k},\lambda^{-}_{k}\}
such that

0
is an either non-isolated or homological visible critical point of

\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda}
, and

[m^{-}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{k}^{-}},0),m^{-}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{k}^{-}},0)+m^{0}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{k}^{-}},0)]\cap[m^{-}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},0),m^{-}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},0)+m^{0}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},0)]=\emptyset.

(II.3)

[m^{-}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{k}^{-}},0),m^{-}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{k}^{-}},0)+m^{0}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{k}^{-}},0)]\cap[m^{-}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},0),m^{-}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},0)+m^{0}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},0)]=\emptyset
,
and either
m^{0}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{k}^{-}},0)=0
or
m^{0}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},0)=0

for each
k\in\mathbb{N}
.

Then there exists a sequence
\{(\lambda_{k},x_{k})\}_{k\geq 1}
in
\hat{\Lambda}\times C^{2}([0,\tau],{\mathbb{R}}^{n})

converging to
(\mu,0)
such that each
x_{k}\neq 0
is a solution of the problem (
3.13
)–(
3.1.1
)
with
\lambda=\lambda_{k}
,
k=1,2,\cdots
,
where
\hat{\Lambda}=\{\mu,\lambda^{+}_{k},\lambda^{-}_{k}\,|\,k\in\mathbb{N}\}
.

Theorem 3.6

(
Existence for bifurcations
)
.

Let
\Lambda
be connected. For
\lambda^{-},\lambda^{+}\in\Lambda

suppose that
one of the following conditions is satisfied:

(i)

Either
0
is not an isolated critical point of
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{+}}
,
or
0
is not an isolated critical point of
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{-}}
,
or
0
is an isolated critical point of
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{+}}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{-}}
and

C_{m}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{+}},0;{\bf K})
and

C_{m}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{-}},0;{\bf K})

are not isomorphic for some Abel group
{\bf K}
and some
m\in\mathbb{Z}
.

(ii)

[m^{-}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{-}},0),m^{-}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{-}},0)+m^{0}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{-}},0)]\cap[m^{-}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{+}},0),m^{-}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{+}},0)+m^{0}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{+}},0)]=\emptyset
,
and there exists
\lambda\in\{\lambda^{+},\lambda^{-}\}
such that
0
is an either non-isolated or homological visible critical point of

\mathcal{E}^{\ast}_{\lambda}
.

(iii)

[m^{-}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{-}},0),m^{-}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{-}},0)+m^{0}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{-}},0)]\cap[m^{-}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{+}},0),m^{-}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{+}},0)+m^{0}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{+}},0)]=\emptyset
,
and either
m^{0}(\mathcal{E}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{+}},0)=0
or
m^{0}(\mathcal{E}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{-}},0)=0
.

Then for any path
\alpha:[0,1]\to\Lambda
connecting
\lambda^{+}
to
\lambda^{-}
there exists
a sequence
(t_{k})\subset[0,1]
converging to some
\bar{t}\in[0,1]
, and
a nonzero solution
x_{k}
of the problem (
1.12
)–(
1.13
)
with
\lambda=\alpha(t_{k})
for each
k\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\|x_{k}\|_{C^{2}([0,\tau];\mathbb{R}^{n})}\to 0

as
k\to\infty
.
Moreover,
\alpha(\bar{t})
is not equal to
\lambda^{+}
(resp.
\lambda^{-}
) if
m^{0}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{+}},0)=0
(resp.
m^{0}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{-}},0)=0
).

Theorem 3.7

(
Alternative bifurcations of Rabinowitz’s type
)
.

Let
\Lambda
be a real interval and
\mu\in{\rm Int}(\Lambda)
. Suppose that
m^{0}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\mu},0)>0
,
and that
m^{0}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda},0)=0
for each
\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\{\mu\}
near
\mu
, and

m^{-}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda},0)
take, respectively, values
m^{-}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\mu},0)
and

m^{-}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\mu},0)+m^{0}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\mu},0)

as
\lambda\in\Lambda
varies in two deleted half neighborhoods of
\mu
.
Then one of the following alternatives occurs:

(i)

The problem (
3.13
)–(
3.1.1
)
with
\lambda=\mu
has a sequence of solutions,
x_{k}\neq 0
,
k=1,2,\cdots
,
which converges to
0
in
C^{2}([0,\tau],{\mathbb{R}}^{n})
.

(ii)

For every
\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\{\mu\}
near
\mu
there is a solution
y_{\lambda}\neq 0
of
(
3.13
)–(
3.1.1
) with parameter value
\lambda
,
such that
y_{\lambda}
converges to zero in
C^{2}([0,\tau],{\mathbb{R}}^{n})
as
\lambda\to\mu
.

(iii)

For a given neighborhood
\mathfrak{W}
of
0\in C^{1}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}([0,\tau];B^{n}_{\iota}(0))
,
there is a one-sided neighborhood
\Lambda^{0}
of
\mu
such that
for any
\lambda\in\Lambda^{0}\setminus\{\mu\}
, the problem (
3.13
)–(
3.1.1
) with parameter value
\lambda

has at least two distinct solutions in
\mathfrak{W}
,
y_{\lambda}^{1}\neq 0
and
y_{\lambda}^{2}\neq 0
,
which can also be required to satisfy
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda}(y^{1}_{\lambda})\neq\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda}(y^{2}_{\lambda})

provided that
m^{0}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\mu},0)>1
and the problem
(
3.13
)–(
3.1.1
) with parameter value
\lambda

has only finitely many solutions in
\mathfrak{W}
.

Theorems
1.4
,
1.6
are derived from
Theorems
3.5
,
3.7
, respectively.
We first admit them and postpone their proof to Section
3.1.3
.
Theorem
3.6
can only lead to Theorem
1.5

under the assumption (
3.3
). We shall directly prove Theorem
1.5

in Section
3.1.4
.

3.1.2
Proofs of Theorems
1.4
,
1.6

Proof of Theorem
1.4
.

(I)

By the assumption there exists a sequence in
\Lambda\times C^{1}_{S_{0}\times S_{1}}([0,\tau];M)

converging to
(\mu,\gamma_{\mu})
,
\{(\lambda_{k},\gamma^{k})\}_{k\geq 1}
,
such that each
\gamma^{k}\neq\gamma_{\lambda_{k}}

is a solution of (
1.12
)–(
1.13
) with
\lambda=\lambda_{k}
,
k=1,2,\cdots
.
After removing the finite terms (if necessary) we may assume that all
\gamma^{k}
are contained in the image of the chart

\Phi_{\overline{\gamma}}
in (
3.6
). Then for each
k\in\mathbb{N}
there exists a unique

{\bf u}^{k}\in C^{2}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}([0,\tau];B^{n}_{\iota}(0))
such that

\Phi_{\overline{\gamma}}({\bf u}^{k})=\gamma^{k}
.
Since
\gamma_{\lambda_{k}}\neq\gamma^{k}
,
d\mathcal{E}_{\lambda_{k}}(\gamma_{\lambda_{k}})=0
and
d\mathcal{E}_{\lambda_{k}}(\gamma^{k})=0
,
we obtain
{\bf u}^{k}\neq{\bf u}_{\lambda_{k}}
, and
d\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda_{k}}({\bf u}_{\lambda_{k}})=0

and
d\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda_{k}}({\bf u}^{k})=0
.
Recall that we have assumed
M\subset\mathbb{R}^{N}
.
Assumption
1.2
implies that
\gamma_{\lambda}-\gamma_{\mu}\to 0
in
C^{1}([0,\tau];\mathbb{R}^{N})
as
\lambda\to\mu
.
Moreover,
\gamma^{k}\to\gamma_{\mu}
in
C^{1}_{S_{0}\times S_{1}}([0,\tau];M)\subset C^{1}([0,\tau];\mathbb{R}^{N})

as
k\to\infty
. Therefore
\|\gamma^{k}-\gamma_{\lambda_{k}}\|_{C^{1}([0,\tau];\mathbb{R}^{N})}\to 0
as
k\to\infty
.
This implies that
\|{\bf u}^{k}-{\bf u}_{\lambda_{k}}\|_{C^{1}([0,\tau];\mathbb{R}^{n})}\to 0
as
k\to\infty
.
In particular, there exists an integer
k_{0}>0
such that
\|{\bf u}^{k}-{\bf u}_{\lambda_{k}}\|_{C^{1}([0,\tau];\mathbb{R}^{n})}<\iota

for all
k\geq k_{0}
. Since
{\bf u}^{k}=({\bf u}^{k}-{\bf u}_{\lambda_{k}})+{\bf u}_{\lambda_{k}}
, by the arguments below (
3.10
)
we get
d\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{k}}({\bf u}^{k}-{\bf u}_{\lambda_{k}})=0
for all
k\geq k_{0}
.
These show that
(\mu,0)\in\Lambda\times C^{1}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}([0,\tau];B^{n}_{\iota}(0))
is a bifurcation point
along sequences of the problem
(
3.13
)–(
3.1.1
)
in
\Lambda\times C^{1}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}([0,\tau];B^{n}_{\iota}(0))
with respect to the trivial branch
\{(\lambda,0)\,|\,\lambda\in\Lambda\}
.
Then Theorem
3.5
(I) concludes
m^{0}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\mu},0)>0
,
and therefore
m^{0}({\mathcal{E}}_{\mu},\gamma_{\mu})>0
by (
3.12
).

(II)

Follow the above notations. By the assumption and (
3.12
) we get that for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle[m^{-}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{k}^{-}},0),m^{-}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{k}^{-}},0)+m^{0}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{k}^{-}},0)]\cap[m^{-}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},0),m^{-}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},0)+m^{0}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},0)]

\displaystyle=[m^{-}_{\tau}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda_{k}^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda_{k}^{-}}),m^{-}_{\tau}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda_{k}^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda_{k}^{-}})+m^{0}_{\tau}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda_{k}^{-}},\gamma_{\lambda_{k}^{-}})]

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\cap[m^{-}_{\tau}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda_{k}^{+}}),m^{-}_{\tau}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda_{k}^{+}})+m^{0}_{\tau}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda_{k}^{+}})]=\emptyset

and either
m^{0}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{k}^{-}},0)=m^{0}_{\tau}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda_{k}^{-}})=0
or
m^{0}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},0)=m^{0}_{\tau}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},\gamma_{\lambda_{k}^{+}})=0
.
By Theorem
3.5
(II)
we have a sequence
\{(\lambda_{k},{\bf v}^{k})\}_{k\geq 1}\subset\{\mu,\lambda^{+}_{k},\lambda^{-}_{k}\,|\,k\in\mathbb{N}\}\times C^{2}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}([0,\tau];B^{n}_{\iota}(0))

such that

\lambda_{k}\to\mu
and
0<\|{\bf v}^{k}\|_{C^{2}}\to 0
,
and that each
{\bf v}^{k}
is a solution of (
3.13
)–(
3.1.1
) with
\lambda=\lambda_{k}
,
k=1,2,\cdots
.
Therefore for
k
large enough,
\gamma^{k}:=\Phi_{\overline{\gamma}}({\bf v^{k}}+{\bf u}_{\lambda_{k}})

defined by
\Phi_{\overline{\gamma}}({\bf v^{k}}+{\bf u}_{\lambda_{k}})(t)=\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(t,{\bf v^{k}}(t)+{\bf u}_{\lambda_{k}}(t))

is a solution of (
1.12
)–(
1.13
) with
\lambda=\lambda_{k}
,

\gamma^{k}\neq\gamma_{\lambda_{k}}
, and as
k\to\infty
we have

\gamma^{k}\to\gamma_{\mu}
in
C^{2}_{S_{0}\times S_{1}}([0,\tau];\mathbb{R}^{N})

because
\Phi_{\overline{\gamma}}
is also a
C^{1}
map from
C^{2}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}([0,\tau];B^{n}_{2\iota}(0))
to

C^{2}_{S_{0}\times S_{1}}([0,\tau];M)
as noted below (
3.6
).
Theorem
1.4
(II) is proved.

∎

Proof of Theorem
1.6
.

Follow the above notations. By the assumption and (
3.12
),
we obtain that
m^{0}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\mu},0)\neq 0
,
and that
m^{0}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda},0)=0

for each
\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\{\mu\}
near
\mu
, and

m^{-}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda},0)
take, respectively,
values
m^{-}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\mu},0)
and

m^{-}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\mu},0)+m^{0}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\mu},0)

as
\lambda\in\Lambda
varies in two deleted half neighborhoods of
\mu
.
Therefore one of the conclusions (i)-(iii) in Theorem
3.7
occurs.

Let
(x_{k})
be as in (i) in Theorem
3.7
.
Since
\|x_{k}\|_{C^{2}}\to 0
, we can choose
k_{0}>0
such that
\|x_{k}\|_{C^{2}}<\iota
for
k\geq k_{0}
.
Then for each
k\geq k_{0}
,
\gamma^{k}:=\Phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(x_{k}+{\bf u}_{\mu})\neq\gamma_{\mu}

is a solution of (
1.12
)–(
1.13
) with
\lambda=\mu
, and as above we may deduce that

\gamma^{k}\to\gamma_{\mu}
in
C^{2}_{S_{0}\times S_{1}}([0,\tau];\mathbb{R}^{N})
as
k\to\infty
.
This is, (i) of Theorem
1.6
occurs.

For
y_{\lambda}\neq 0
in (ii) in Theorem
3.7
, we can shrink
\Lambda
toward
\mu

so that
\|y_{\lambda}\|_{C^{2}}<\iota
for all
\lambda\in\Lambda
. Then

\alpha_{\lambda}:=\Phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(y_{\lambda}+{\bf u}_{\lambda})\neq\gamma_{\lambda}

is a solution of (
1.12
)–(
1.13
) with parameter value
\lambda
,
and
\alpha_{\lambda}-\gamma_{\lambda}

converges to zero in
C^{2}([0,\tau],{\mathbb{R}}^{N})
as
\lambda\to\mu
.
Namely, (ii) of Theorem
1.6
occurs.

For a given neighborhood
\mathcal{W}
of
\gamma_{\mu}
in
C^{1}([0,\tau],M)
,
let us choose a neighborhood
\mathfrak{W}
of
0\in C^{1}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}([0,\tau];B^{n}_{\iota}(0))

such that
\Phi_{\overline{\gamma}}\left({\bf u}_{\mu}+\mathfrak{W}\right)\subset\mathcal{W}
.
Let
\Lambda^{0}
,
y_{\lambda}^{1}\neq 0
and
y_{\lambda}^{2}\neq 0
be as in (iii) in Theorem
3.7
.
Put
\gamma^{i}_{\lambda}:=\Phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(y^{i}_{\lambda}+{\bf u}_{\lambda})\neq\gamma_{\lambda}
,
i=1,2
.
Both sit in
\mathcal{W}
and are distinct solutions of (
1.12
)–(
1.13
) with parameter value
\lambda
.
Suppose that
m^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{\mu},\gamma_{\mu})=m^{0}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\mu},0)>1
and
(
1.12
)–(
1.13
) with parameter value
\lambda

has only finitely many distinct solutions in
\mathcal{W}
.
Then the problem (
3.13
)–(
3.1.1
) with parameter value
\lambda

has only finitely many solutions in
\mathfrak{W}
as well.
In this case (iii) in Theorem
3.7
concludes that
the above
y_{\lambda}^{1}\neq 0
and
y_{\lambda}^{2}\neq 0

are chosen to satisfies
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda}(y^{1}_{\lambda})\neq\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda}(y^{2}_{\lambda})
,
which implies
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}(\gamma_{\lambda}^{1})\neq\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}(\gamma_{\lambda}^{2})
.
Hence (iii) in Theorem
3.7
occurs.
∎

3.1.3
Proofs of Theorems
3.5
,
3.6
,
3.7

We need to make modifications for the Lagrangian
\tilde{L}^{\ast}
in (
3.9
).

Lemma 3.8
.

Given a positive number
\rho_{0}>0
and a subset
\hat{\Lambda}\subset\Lambda
which is either compact or sequential compact,
there exists a continuous function
\check{L}:\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau]\times B^{n}_{3\iota/4}(0)\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}}

satisfying the following properties for some constants
\check{\kappa}>0
and
0<\check{c}<\check{C}
:

(L0)

The following partial derivatives

\partial_{t}\check{L}(\cdot),\;\partial_{q}\check{L}(\cdot),\;\partial_{v}\check{L}(\cdot),\;\partial_{tv}\check{L}(\cdot),\;\partial_{vt}\check{L}(\cdot),\;\partial_{qv}\check{L}(\cdot),\;\partial_{vq}\check{L}(\cdot),\;\partial_{qq}\check{L}(\cdot),\;\partial_{vv}\check{L}(\cdot)

exist and depend continuously on
(\lambda,t,q,v)
. (These are all used in the proof of Proposition
3.11
.)

(L1)

\check{L}
and
\tilde{L}^{\ast}
are equal in
\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau]\times B^{n}_{3\iota/4}(0)\times B^{n}_{\rho_{0}}(0)
.

(L2)

\partial_{vv}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,q,v)\geq\check{c}I_{n},\quad\forall(\lambda,t,q,v)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau]\times B^{n}_{3\iota/4}(0)\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

(L3)

\bigl|\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial q_{i}\partial q_{j}}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,q,v)\bigr|\leq\check{C}(1+|v|^{2}),\quad\bigl|\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial q_{i}\partial v_{j}}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,q,v)\bigr|\leq\check{C}(1+|v|),\quad\hbox{and}\\
\bigl|\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial v_{i}\partial v_{j}}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,q,v)\bigr|\leq\check{C},\quad\forall(\lambda,t,q,v)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau]\times B^{n}_{3\iota/4}(0)\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

(L4)

\check{L}(\lambda,t,q,v)\geq\check{\kappa}|v|^{2}-\check{C}

for all
(\lambda,t,q,v)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau]\times B^{n}_{3\iota/4}(0)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.

(L5)

|\partial_{q}\check{L}(\lambda,t,q,v)|\leq\check{C}(1+|v|^{2})
and
|\partial_{v}\check{L}(\lambda,t,q,v)|\leq\check{C}(1+|v|)

for all
(\lambda,t,q,v)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau]\times B^{n}_{3\iota/4}(0)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.

(L6)

|\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,q,v)|\leq\check{C}(1+|v|^{2})
for all
(\lambda,t,q,v)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau]\times{B}^{n}_{3\iota/4}(0)\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

Proof.

Step 1
. Fix a positive number
\rho_{1}>\rho_{0}
.
As in the proof of Lemma
2.4
,
we may choose a
C^{\infty}
convex function
\psi_{\rho_{0},\rho_{1}}:[0,\infty)\to{\mathbb{R}}

such that
\psi^{\prime}_{\rho_{0},\rho_{1}}(t)>0
for

t\in(\rho_{0}^{2},\infty)
,
\psi_{\rho_{0},\rho_{1}}(t)=0
for
t\in[0,\rho_{0}^{2})
and

\psi_{\rho_{0},\rho_{1}}(t)=\kappa t+\varrho_{0}
for
t\in[\rho_{1}^{2},\infty)
,
where
\kappa>0
and
\varrho_{0}<0
are suitable constants. Define

\tilde{L}^{\ast\ast}:\Lambda\times[0,\tau]\times B^{n}_{\iota}(0)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
by

\tilde{L}^{\ast\ast}(\lambda,t,q,v)=\tilde{L}^{\ast}(\lambda,t,q,v)+\psi_{\rho_{0},\rho_{1}}(|v|^{2}).

(3.18)

It possess the same properties as
\check{L}
in (L0) and also satisfies

\tilde{L}^{\ast\ast}(\lambda,t,q,v)=\tilde{L}^{\ast}(\lambda,t,q,v),\quad\forall(\lambda,t,q,v)\in\Lambda\times[0,\tau]\times B^{n}_{\iota}(0)\times B^{n}_{\rho_{0}}(0).

(3.19)

Since the closure of
B^{n}_{3\iota/4}(0)
is a compact subset in
B^{n}_{\iota}(0)
,
and
\hat{\Lambda}
is either compact or sequential compact,
by Lemma
2.4
(or the proof of Lemma
2.4
(iv))
there exists a constant
C^{\prime}>0
such that

\tilde{L}^{\ast\ast}(\lambda,t,q,v)\geq\kappa|v|^{2}-C^{\prime},\quad\forall(\lambda,t,q,v)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau]\times\overline{B^{n}_{3\iota/4}(0)}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}.

(3.20)

Step 2
.
Take a smooth function
\Gamma:{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}
such that
\Gamma(s)=s
for
s\leq 1
and
that
\Gamma(s)
is constant for
s\geq 2
.
Fix positive numbers
\rho>\rho_{1}
and
\vartheta
such that

\vartheta\geq\max\{\tilde{L}^{\ast\ast}_{\lambda}(q,v)\,|\,(\lambda,q,v)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau]\times\overline{B^{n}_{3\iota/4}(0)}\times\bar{B}^{n}_{\rho}(0)\}.

Define
\tilde{L}^{\ast\ast\ast}:\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau]\times\overline{B^{n}_{3\iota/4}(0)}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
by

\tilde{L}^{\ast\ast\ast}=\vartheta\Gamma(\tilde{L}^{\ast\ast}/\vartheta)
.
Then the choice of
\vartheta
implies

\tilde{L}^{\ast\ast\ast}(\lambda,t,q,v)=\tilde{L}^{\ast\ast}(\lambda,t,q,v),\quad\forall(\lambda,t,q,v)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau]\times\overline{B^{n}_{3\iota/4}(0)}\times B^{n}_{\rho}(0).

(3.21)

By (
3.20
),
\tilde{L}^{\ast\ast\ast}

is equal to a constant
C^{\prime\prime}
outside
\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau]\times\overline{B^{n}_{3\iota/4}(0)}\times B^{n}_{R}(0)
for a large
R>\rho
.
Because of this fact and

\displaystyle\partial_{vv}\tilde{L}^{\ast\ast}(\lambda,t,q,v)[u,u]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{vv}\tilde{L}^{\ast}(\lambda,t,q,v)[u,u]+2\psi^{\prime}_{\rho_{0},\rho_{1}}(|v|^{2})|u|^{2}

(3.22)

\displaystyle+4\psi^{\prime\prime}_{\rho_{0},\rho_{1}}(|v|^{2})\bigl(({v},{u})_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\bigr)^{2}

for each
(\lambda,t,q,v)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau]\times B^{n}_{\iota}(0)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
, there exist positive constants
\Upsilon
and
C_{0}^{\prime}
such that

\displaystyle\partial_{vv}\tilde{L}^{\ast\ast\ast}_{\lambda}(t,q,v)[u,u]\geq-\Upsilon|u|^{2}\quad\forall(\lambda,t,q,v,u)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau]\times\overline{B^{n}_{3\iota/4}(0)}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n},

(3.23)

\displaystyle\tilde{L}^{\ast\ast\ast}(\lambda,t,q,v)\geq\kappa|v|^{2}-C^{\prime}_{0},\quad\forall(\lambda,t,q,v)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau]\times\overline{B^{n}_{3\iota/4}(0)}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}.

(3.24)

Choose a smooth function
\Xi:[0,\infty)\to{\mathbb{R}}
such that:

\Xi^{\prime}\geq 0
,

\Xi
is convex on
[\rho_{0}^{2},\infty)
, vanishes in
[0,\rho_{0}^{2})
, and is equal
to

the affine function
\Upsilon s+\Theta
on
[\rho^{2},\infty)
, where
\Theta<0
is a suitable constant.

(See
[
16
, Lemma 2.1]
or
[
1
, §5]
).
Define

\check{L}:\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau]\times\overline{B^{n}_{3\iota/4}(0)}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}},\;(\lambda,t,q,v)\mapsto\tilde{L}^{\ast\ast\ast}(\lambda,t,q,v)+\Xi(|v|^{2}).

(3.25)

Since
\tilde{L}^{\ast\ast\ast}=\vartheta\Gamma(\tilde{L}^{\ast\ast}/\vartheta)
,
it clearly satisfies (L0) by (
3.18
).
(L1) may follow from (
3.19
), (
3.21
)
and the fact that
\Xi
vanishes in
[0,\rho_{0}^{2})
.
(
3.24
) leads to (L4) because
\Xi\geq 0
.

Let us prove that
\check{L}
satisfies (L2) and (L3).

\bullet

If
|v|<\rho
, by (
3.21
)
\check{L}(\lambda,t,q,v)=\tilde{L}^{\ast\ast\ast}(\lambda,t,q,v)+\Xi(|v|^{2})=\tilde{L}^{\ast\ast}(\lambda,t,q,v)+\Xi(|v|^{2})
and so

\displaystyle\partial_{vv}\check{L}(\lambda,t,q,v)[u,u]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{vv}\tilde{L}^{\ast\ast}(\lambda,t,q,v)[u,u]+\partial_{vv}(\Xi(|v|^{2}))[u,u]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{vv}\tilde{L}^{\ast}(\lambda,t,q,v)[u,u]+2\psi^{\prime}_{\rho_{0},\rho_{1}}(|v|^{2})|u|^{2}

\displaystyle+4\psi^{\prime\prime}_{\rho_{0},\rho_{1}}(|v|^{2})\bigl(({v},{u})_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\bigr)^{2}+2\psi^{\prime}_{\rho_{0},\rho_{1}}(|v|^{2})|u|^{2}

\displaystyle+2\Xi^{\prime}(|v|^{2})|u|^{2}+4\Xi^{\prime\prime}(|v|^{2})\bigl(({v},{u})_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\bigr)^{2}

because of (
3.22
) and the equality

\partial_{vv}(\Xi(|v|^{2}))[u,u]=2\Xi^{\prime}(|v|^{2})|u|^{2}+4\Xi^{\prime\prime}(|v|^{2})\bigl(({v},{u})_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\bigr)^{2}
for all
u\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.
Recall that
\psi^{\prime}_{\rho_{0},\rho_{1}}\geq 0
,
\psi^{\prime\prime}_{\rho_{0},\rho_{1}}\geq 0
,
\Xi^{\prime}\geq 0
and
\Xi^{\prime\prime}\geq 0
.
Since both
\partial_{vv}\check{L}(\lambda,t,q,v)
and
\partial_{vv}\tilde{L}^{\ast\ast}(\lambda,t,q,v)

depend continuously on
(\lambda,t,q,v)
we deduce

\partial_{vv}\check{L}(\lambda,t,q,v)\geq\partial_{vv}\tilde{L}^{\ast}(\lambda,t,q,v),\quad\forall(\lambda,t,q,v)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau]\times\overline{B^{n}_{3\iota/4}(0)}\times\overline{B^{n}_{\rho}(0)}.

(3.26)

\bullet

If
|v|\geq\rho
then (
3.23
) may lead to

\displaystyle\partial_{vv}\check{L}(\lambda,t,q,v)[u,u]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{vv}\tilde{L}^{\ast\ast\ast}(\lambda,t,q,v)[u,u]+2\Xi^{\prime}(|v|^{2})|u|^{2}

(3.27)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\Upsilon|u|^{2},\quad\forall u\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}.

By Proposition
3.3
(c),
\tilde{L}^{\ast}_{\lambda}(t,q,v)
is
strictly convex in
v
. (L2) may follow from (
3.27
) and (
3.26
) because

\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau]\times\overline{B^{n}_{3\iota/4}(0)}\times\overline{B^{n}_{\rho}(0)}
is either compact or sequential compact.
Using the same reason we obtain that
\check{L}
satisfies (L3) because

\check{L}(\lambda,t,q,v)=\tilde{L}^{\ast\ast\ast}(\lambda,t,q,v)+\Xi(|v|^{2})=C^{\prime\prime}+\Upsilon|v|^{2}+\Theta\quad\forall|v|>R.

Finally, since
\partial_{q}\check{L}(\lambda,t,0,0)
and
\partial_{v}\check{L}(\lambda,t,0,0)
are bounded
using the Taylor formula (L5) and (L6) easily follows from (L3).
∎

Consider the Banach subspace

{\bf X}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}:=\left\{\xi\in C^{1}([0,\tau];\mathbb{R}^{n})\,|\,(\xi(0),\xi(\tau))\in V_{0}\times V_{1}\right\}

of
C^{1}([0,\tau],\mathbb{R}^{n})
, and
the Hilbert subspace

{\bf H}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}:=\left\{\xi\in W^{1,2}([0,\tau];\mathbb{R}^{n})\,|\,(\xi(0),\xi(\tau))\in V_{0}\times V_{1}\right\}

of
W^{1,2}([0,\tau];\mathbb{R}^{n})
. The spaces
{\bf H}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}
and
{\bf X}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}
have the following open subsets

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}:

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle W^{1,2}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}\left([0,\tau];B^{n}_{\iota/2}(0)\right)=\left\{\xi\in W^{1,2}\left([0,\tau];B^{n}_{\iota/2}(0)\right)\,\big|\,(\xi(0),\xi(\tau))\in V_{0}\times V_{1}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}^{X}:

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}\cap{\bf X}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}=C^{1}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}([0,\tau];B^{n}_{\iota/2}(0))

respectively. Define a family of functionals
\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}:\mathcal{U}\to{\mathbb{R}}
given by

\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}(x)=\int^{\tau}_{0}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))dt,\quad\lambda\in\hat{\Lambda}.

(3.28)

Since
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda}
is defined on
C^{1}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}([0,\tau];B^{n}_{\iota}(0))
and

\mathcal{U}^{X}=C^{1}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}([0,\tau];B^{n}_{\iota/2}(0))
is an open neighborhood of

0\in C^{1}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}([0,\tau];B^{n}_{\iota}(0))
, by (L1) in Lemma
3.8
we obtain

\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda}=\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}|_{\mathcal{U}^{X}}\quad\hbox{in}\quad\{x\in\mathcal{U}^{X}\,|\,\|x\|_{C^{1}}<\rho_{0}\}\subset\mathcal{U}^{X},

(3.29)

and therefore the following (
3.30
).

Proposition 3.9
.

(i)

Each
\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}
is

C^{2-0}
and twice Gâteaux-differentiable, and
d\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}(0)=0
and

m^{\star}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda},0)=m^{\star}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}|_{\mathcal{U}^{X}},0)=m^{\star}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda},0),\quad\star=-,0.

(3.30)

(ii)

Each critical point of
\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}
sits in

C^{2}\left([0,\tau];B^{n}_{\iota/2}(0)\right)\cap\mathcal{U}^{X}
, and satisfies
the boundary problem:

\displaystyle\left.\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{d}{dt}\big(\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))\big)-\partial_{q}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))=0,\\
(x(0),x(\tau))\in V_{0}\times V_{1},\\
\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(0,x(0),\dot{x}(0))[v_{0}]=0\quad\forall v_{0}\in V_{0},\\
\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(\tau,x(\tau),\dot{x}(\tau))[v_{1}]=0\quad\forall v_{1}\in V_{1}.\end{array}\right\}

(iii)

The gradient of
\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}
at
x\in\mathcal{U}
, denoted by

\nabla\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}(x)
, is given by

\displaystyle\nabla\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}(x)(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{t}\int^{t}_{0}\left[e^{-2s}\int^{s}_{0}e^{r}f_{\lambda,x}(r)dr\right]ds+c_{1}(\lambda,x)e^{t}+c_{2}(\lambda,x)e^{-t}

(3.36)

\displaystyle\qquad+\int^{t}_{0}\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s))ds,

where
c_{1}(\lambda,x),c_{2}(\lambda,x)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
are suitable constant vectors and

\displaystyle f_{\lambda,x}(t)=-\partial_{q}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))+\int^{t}_{0}\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s))ds.

(3.37)

(iv)

\nabla\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}
restricts to a
C^{1}
map
A_{\lambda}

from
\mathcal{U}^{X}
to
{\bf X}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}
.

(v)

\nabla\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}

has the Gâteaux derivative
B_{\lambda}(\zeta)\in{\mathscr{L}}_{s}({\bf H}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}})
at
\zeta\in\mathcal{U}
given by

\displaystyle(B_{\lambda}(\zeta)\xi,\eta)_{1,2}=\int_{0}^{\tau}\Bigl(\!\!\!\!\!

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\partial_{vv}\check{L}_{{\lambda}}\bigl(t,\zeta(t),\dot{\zeta}(t)\bigr)\bigl[\dot{\xi}(t),\dot{\eta}(t)\bigr]+\partial_{qv}\check{L}_{{\lambda}}\bigl(t,\zeta(t),\dot{\zeta}(t)\bigr)\bigl[\xi(t),\dot{\eta}(t)\bigr]

(3.38)

\displaystyle+\partial_{vq}\check{L}_{{\lambda}}\bigl(t,\zeta(t),\dot{\zeta}(t)\bigr)\bigl[\dot{\xi}(t),\eta(t)\bigr]

\displaystyle+\partial_{qq}\check{L}_{{\lambda}}\bigl(t,\zeta(t),\dot{\zeta}(t)\bigr)\bigl[\xi(t),\eta(t)\bigr]\Bigr)\,dt

for any
\xi,\eta\in{\bf H}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}
.

B_{\lambda}(\zeta)
is a self-adjoint Fredholm operator and
has a decomposition

{B}_{\lambda}(\zeta)=\textsl{{P}}_{{\lambda}}(\zeta)+\textsl{{Q}}_{{\lambda}}(\zeta)
, where

\textsl{P}_{{\lambda}}(\zeta)\in{\mathscr{L}}_{s}({\bf H}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}})
is a positive
definitive linear operator defined by

\displaystyle(\textsl{P}_{{\lambda}}(\zeta)\xi,\eta)_{1,2}=\int_{0}^{\tau}\big(\partial_{vv}\check{L}_{{\lambda}}\bigl(t,\zeta(t),\dot{\zeta}(t)\bigr)[\dot{\xi}(t),\dot{\eta}(t)]+\bigl(\xi(t),\eta(t)\bigr)_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}\big)\,dt,

(3.39)

and
\textsl{Q}_{{\lambda}}(\zeta)\in\check{\mathscr{L}}_{s}({\bf H}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}})

is a compact self-adjoint linear operator.
Moreover, (L2) in Lemma
3.8
implies that
(\textsl{P}_{{\lambda}}(\zeta)\xi,\xi)_{1,2}\geq\min\{\check{c},1\}\|\xi\|_{1,2}^{2}

for all
\zeta\in\mathcal{U}
and
\xi\in{\bf H}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}
.

(vi)

If
(\lambda_{k})\subset\hat{\Lambda}
and
(\zeta_{k})\subset\mathcal{U}
converge to
\mu\in\hat{\Lambda}
and
0
, respectively,
then
\|\textsl{P}_{{\lambda_{k}}}(\zeta_{k})\xi-\textsl{P}_{{\mu}}(0)\xi\|_{1,2}\to 0
for each
\xi\in{\bf H}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}
.

(vii)

\mathcal{U}\ni\zeta\mapsto\textsl{Q}_{{\lambda}}(\zeta)\in\mathscr{L}_{s}({\bf H}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}})

is uniformly continuous at
0\in\mathcal{U}
with respect to
\lambda\in\hat{\Lambda}
and

\|\textsl{Q}_{\lambda_{k}}(0)-\textsl{Q}_{\mu}(0)\|\to 0
as
(\lambda_{k})\subset\Lambda
converges to
\mu\in\hat{\Lambda}
.

(viii)

\{\xi\in{\bf H}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}\mid B_{\lambda}(0)\xi=s\xi,\,s\leq 0\}\subset{\bf X}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}
and

\{\xi\in{\bf H}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}\mid B_{\lambda}(0)\xi\in{\bf X}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}\}\subset{\bf X}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}.

Proof.

(i) is obtained by
[
16
, §4]
or
[
17
, §3]
and
[
15
]
.
(ii) follows from
[
4
, Theorem 4.5]
because of conditions (L0), (L2) and (L4)-(L6) in Lemma
3.8
.
(iii) is obtained by (4.13) and (4.14) in
[
16
]
.
(iv) and (v) are proved in
[
16
, §4]
.

In order to prove (vi), by (
3.39
) we have

\displaystyle\|[\textsl{P}_{{\lambda_{k}}}(\zeta_{k})-\textsl{P}_{{\mu}}(0)]\xi\|^{2}_{1,2}\leq\int_{0}^{\tau}\big|[\partial_{vv}\check{L}_{{\lambda_{k}}}\bigl(t,\zeta_{k}(t),\dot{\zeta}_{k}(t)\bigr)-\partial_{vv}\check{L}_{\mu}\bigl(t,0,0\bigr)]\dot{\xi}(t)\big|^{2}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\,dt.

Note that
\|\zeta_{k}\|_{1,2}\to 0
implies
\|\zeta_{k}\|_{C^{0}}\to 0
.
Since
(\lambda,t,x,v)\mapsto\partial_{vv}\check{L}_{{\lambda}}(t,x,v)
is continuous, by
the third inequality in (L3) in Lemma
3.8
we may apply
[
21
, Prop. B.9]
(
[
20
, Prop. C.1]
) to

f(t,\eta;\lambda)=\partial_{vv}\check{L}(\lambda,t,\zeta_{k}(t),\dot{\zeta}_{k}(t))\eta

to get that

\int_{0}^{\tau}\big|[\partial_{vv}\check{L}_{{\lambda_{k}}}\bigl(t,\zeta_{k}(t),\dot{\zeta}_{k}(t)\bigr)-\partial_{vv}\check{L}_{{\mu}}\bigl(t,0,0\bigr)]\dot{\xi}(t)\big|^{2}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\,dt\to 0.

Moreover, the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem also leads to

\int_{0}^{\tau}\big|[\partial_{vv}\check{L}_{{\lambda_{k}}}\bigl(t,0,0\bigr)-\partial_{vv}\check{L}_{{\mu}}\bigl(t,0,0\bigr)]\dot{\xi}(t)\big|^{2}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\,dt\to 0.

Hence
\|[\textsl{P}_{{\lambda_{k}}}(\zeta_{k})-\textsl{P}_{\mu}(0)]\xi\|_{1,2}\to 0
.

Next, let us prove (vii).
Write
\textsl{Q}_{\lambda}(\zeta):=\textsl{Q}_{\lambda,1}(\zeta)+\textsl{Q}_{\lambda,2}(\zeta)+\textsl{Q}_{\lambda,3}(\zeta)
, where

\displaystyle(\textsl{Q}_{\lambda,1}(\zeta)\xi,\eta)_{1,2}=\int_{0}^{\tau}\partial_{vq}\check{L}_{\lambda}\big(t,\zeta(t),\dot{\zeta}(t)\big)[\dot{\xi}(t),\eta(t)]dt,

\displaystyle(\textsl{Q}_{\lambda,2}(\zeta)\xi,\eta)_{1,2}=\int_{0}^{\tau}\partial_{qv}\check{L}_{\lambda}\big(t,\zeta(t),\dot{\zeta}(t)\big)[\xi(t),\dot{\eta}(t)]dt,

\displaystyle(\textsl{Q}_{\lambda,3}(\zeta)\xi,\eta)_{1,2}=\int_{0}^{\tau}\Bigl(\partial_{qq}\check{L}_{\lambda}\big(t,\zeta(t),\dot{\zeta}(t)\big)[\xi(t),\eta(t)]-\bigl(\xi(t),\eta(t)\bigr)_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Bigr)\,dt.

As above the first claim follows from (L3) in Lemma
3.8
and
[
21
, Prop. B.9]
(
[
20
, Prop. C.1]
) directly.

In order to prove the second claim, as in the proof of
[
15
, page 571]
we have

\displaystyle\|\textsl{Q}_{\lambda_{k},1}(0)-\textsl{Q}_{\mu,1}(0)\|_{\mathscr{L}({\bf H})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2(e^{\tau}+1)\left(\int^{\tau}_{0}\left|\partial_{vq}\check{L}_{\lambda_{k}}(s,0,0)-\partial_{vq}\check{L}_{\mu}(s,0,0)\right|^{2}ds\right)^{1/2}.

Because of the second inequality in (
L2
), it follows from the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem
that
\|\textsl{Q}_{\lambda_{k},1}(0)-\textsl{Q}_{\mu,1}(0)\|_{\mathscr{L}({\bf H})}\to 0
.
Observe that

(\textsl{Q}_{\lambda,2}(\zeta)\xi,\eta)_{1,2}=\bigr(\xi,(\textsl{Q}_{\lambda,1}(\zeta))^{\ast}\eta\bigl)_{1,2}
.
Hence
\|\textsl{Q}_{\lambda_{k},2}(0)-\textsl{Q}_{\mu,2}(0)\|_{\mathscr{L}({\bf H})}\to 0
.
Finally, it is easy to deduce that

\displaystyle\|\textsl{Q}_{\lambda_{k},3}(0)-\textsl{Q}_{\mu,3}(0)\|^{2}_{\mathscr{L}({\bf H})}\leq\int_{0}^{\tau}\bigl|\partial_{qq}\check{L}_{\lambda_{k}}\big(t,0,0\big)-\partial_{qq}\check{L}_{\mu}\big(t,0,0\big)\bigr|^{2}\,dt.

By the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem the right side converges to zero.
Then
\|\textsl{Q}_{\lambda_{k},3}(0)-\textsl{Q}_{\mu,3}(0)\|_{\mathscr{L}({\bf H})}\to 0
and therefore

\|\textsl{Q}_{\lambda_{k}}(0)-\textsl{Q}_{\mu}(0)\|\to 0
.

Finally, we prove (viii). Below
[
16
, (4.17)]
we concluded that
these can be proved by almost repeating the arguments in
[
15
, §3]
.
The case
s<0
in the first claim was proved in
[
6
, p.178]
.
Here we present an improvement of the method from
[
15
, §3]
,
which can be effectively applied to the case considered in Section
4
.
Define

\displaystyle\mathfrak{P}(t)=\partial_{vv}\check{L}_{{\lambda}}\bigl(t,0,0\bigr),\quad\mathfrak{Q}(t)=D_{{q}{v}}\check{L}_{\lambda}\big(t,0,0\big),\quad\mathfrak{R}(t)=D_{{q}{q}}\check{L}_{\lambda}\big(t,0,0\big),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}(t,\tilde{y},\tilde{v})=\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{P}(t)\tilde{v}\cdot\tilde{v}+\mathfrak{Q}(t)\tilde{y}\cdot\tilde{v}+\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{R}(t)\tilde{y}\cdot\tilde{y},

which are
C^{2}
, and a functional
\mathfrak{F}
on
{\bf H}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}
by

\mathfrak{F}(\tilde{y})=\int\limits_{0}^{\tau}\mathfrak{L}\big(t,\tilde{y}(t),\dot{\tilde{y}}(t)\big)dt,\quad\forall\tilde{y}\in{\bf H}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}.

Then
\mathfrak{F}
is
C^{2}
-smooth on
{\bf H}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}
(and hence
on
{\bf X}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}
), and
\tilde{y}=0\in{\bf H}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}

is a critical point of
\mathfrak{F}
. It is also easily checked that

\displaystyle d\mathfrak{F}(\tilde{y})[\tilde{z}]=d^{2}\mathfrak{F}(0)(\tilde{y},\tilde{z})=(B_{\lambda}(0)\tilde{y},\tilde{z})_{1,2},\quad\forall\tilde{y},\tilde{z}\in{\bf H}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}.

(3.40)

Therefore,
\tilde{y}\in\mathbf{H}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}
belongs to
\ker B_{\lambda}(0)
if and only if
\tilde{y}
is a critical point of
\mathfrak{F}
, which means it satisfies (in the distributional sense) the differential equation given in the first line of the boundary problem:

\left.\begin{array}[]{l}\frac{d}{dt}\big(\partial_{v}\mathfrak{L}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))\big)-\partial_{q}\mathfrak{L}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))=0,\\
(x(0),x(\tau))\in V_{0}\times V_{1},\\
\partial_{v}\mathfrak{L}(0,x(0),\dot{x}(0))[v_{0}]=0\quad\forall v_{0}\in V_{0},\\
\partial_{v}\mathfrak{L}(\tau,x(\tau),\dot{x}(\tau))[v_{1}]=0\quad\forall v_{1}\in V_{1}.\end{array}\right\}

Of course,
\tilde{y}
is automatically
C^{2}
and hence satisfies the above boundary problem in the classical sense (cf.
[
6
, p. 178]
).
In particular,
\tilde{y}\in{\bf X}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}
.
(As shown in
[
6
, p. 178]
, such an argument is also applicable to the case considered in Section
4
.)

The case where
s<0
in the first claim can be reduced the case
s=0
.
Assume that
\xi\in{\bf H}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}
satisfies
B_{\lambda}(0)\xi=s\xi
for some
s<0
,
and let the functional
\mathfrak{F}
and the Lagrangian
\mathfrak{L}
be as above.
Define a new Lagrangian
\widehat{\mathfrak{L}}
and the corresponding functional

\widehat{\mathfrak{F}}
on
{\bf H}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}
by

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathfrak{L}}(t,\tilde{y},\tilde{v})

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{L}(t,\tilde{y},\tilde{v})-\frac{s}{2}(\tilde{v},\tilde{v})_{1,2},

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathfrak{F}}(\tilde{y})

\displaystyle=\int\limits_{0}^{\tau}\widehat{\mathfrak{L}}\big(t,\tilde{y}(t),\dot{\tilde{y}}(t)\big)dt,\quad\forall\tilde{y}\in{\bf H}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}.

As above, we have
d\widehat{\mathfrak{F}}(\tilde{y})[\tilde{z}]=(\widehat{B}_{\lambda}(0)\tilde{y},\tilde{z})_{1,2}
for all
\tilde{y},\tilde{z}\in{\bf H}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}
.
Let
{\rm I}_{{\bf H}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}}
be the identity operator on
{\bf H}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}
,
and let
I_{n}
be the identity matrix of order
n
.
It is easily checked that
\widehat{B}_{\lambda}(0)={B}_{\lambda}(0)-s{\rm I}_{{\bf H}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}}
and that

\widehat{\mathfrak{L}}(t,\tilde{y},\tilde{v})=\frac{1}{2}\widehat{\mathfrak{P}}(t)\tilde{v}\cdot\tilde{v}+\widehat{\mathfrak{Q}}(t)\tilde{y}\cdot\tilde{v}+\frac{1}{2}\widehat{\mathfrak{R}}(t)\tilde{y}\cdot\tilde{y},

where
\widehat{\mathfrak{P}}(t)={\mathfrak{P}}(t)-sI_{n}
,
\widehat{\mathfrak{Q}}(t)={\mathfrak{Q}}(t)
and
\widehat{\mathfrak{R}}(t)={\mathfrak{R}}(t)
.
The key is that all
\widehat{\mathfrak{P}}(t)
are positive definite because
s<0
.
Therefore, by the above conclusion for
s=0
, we obtain
\ker~\widehat{B}_{\lambda}(0)\subset{\bf X}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}
and hence
\xi\in{\bf X}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}
.

What remains is to prove the second inclusion (relation).
Let
\xi\in{\bf H}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}
be such that
\zeta:=B_{\lambda}(0)\xi\in{\bf X}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}
.
By (
3.40
),
\nabla\mathfrak{F}(\xi)=B_{\lambda}(0)\xi=\zeta
.
Applying the conclusions in (iii) to
\mathfrak{F}
, we arrive at

\displaystyle\zeta(t)=\nabla\mathfrak{F}(\xi)(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{t}\int^{t}_{0}\left[e^{-2s}\int^{s}_{0}e^{r}\hat{f}_{\lambda,\xi}(r)dr\right]ds+c_{1}(\lambda,\xi)e^{t}+c_{2}(\lambda,\xi)e^{-t}

(3.41)

\displaystyle\qquad+\int^{t}_{0}\partial_{v}\mathfrak{L}(s,\xi(s),\dot{\xi}(s))ds,

where
c_{1}(\lambda,\xi),c_{2}(\lambda,\xi)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
are suitable constant vectors and

\displaystyle\hat{f}_{\lambda,\xi}(t)=-\partial_{q}\mathfrak{L}(t,\xi(t),\dot{\xi}(t))+\int^{t}_{0}\partial_{v}\mathfrak{L}(s,\xi(s),\dot{\xi}(s))ds.

Differentiating both sides of equation (
3.41
) with respect to
t
yields

\displaystyle\dot{\zeta}(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{t}\int^{t}_{0}\left[e^{-2s}\int^{s}_{0}e^{r}\hat{f}_{\lambda,\xi}(r)dr\right]ds+e^{-t}\int^{t}_{0}e^{r}\hat{f}_{\lambda,\xi}(r)dr

\displaystyle\qquad+c_{1}(\lambda,\xi)e^{t}-c_{2}(\lambda,\xi)e^{-t}+\partial_{v}\mathfrak{L}(t,\xi(t),\dot{\xi}(t)).

Since
\dot{\zeta}(t)
is continuous, this equation implies that

\partial_{v}\mathfrak{L}(t,\xi(t),\dot{\xi}(t))
is continuous with respect to
t
.
But
\partial_{v}\mathfrak{L}(t,\xi(t),\dot{\xi}(t))=\mathfrak{P}(t)\dot{\xi}(t)+\mathfrak{Q}(t)\xi(t)
,
and so

\dot{\xi}(t)=(\mathfrak{P}(t))^{-1}\partial_{v}\mathfrak{L}(t,\xi(t),\dot{\xi}(t))-(\mathfrak{P}(t))^{-1}\mathfrak{Q}(t)\xi(t).

It follows that
\dot{\xi}(t)
is continuous in
t
and hence
\xi\in{\bf X}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}
.
The proof of (viii) is complete.
∎

In order to apply our abstract theory in
[
19
,
21
,
21
]
to the family of functionals in
(
3.28
) we also need two results.

Proposition 3.10
.

Both maps
\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathcal{U}^{X}\ni(\lambda,x)\mapsto\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}(x)\in{\mathbb{R}}
and

\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathcal{U}^{X}\ni(\lambda,x)\mapsto A_{\lambda}(x)\in{\bf X}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}
are continuous.

Proof.

Step 1
(
Prove that
\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathcal{U}\ni(\lambda,x)\mapsto\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}(x)\in{\mathbb{R}}
is continuous, and therefore obtain the first claim
).
Indeed, for any two points
(\lambda,x)
and
(\lambda_{0},x_{0})
in
\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathcal{U}
we can write

\displaystyle\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}(x)-\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda_{0}}(x_{0})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\int^{\tau}_{0}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))dt-\int^{\tau}_{0}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x_{0}(t),\dot{x}_{0}(t))dt\right]

\displaystyle+\left[\int^{\tau}_{0}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x_{0}(t),\dot{x}_{0}(t))dt-\int^{\tau}_{0}\check{L}_{\lambda_{0}}(t,x_{0}(t),\dot{x}_{0}(t))dt\right].

As
(\lambda,x)\to(\lambda_{0},x_{0})
, we derive from (L6) in Lemma
3.8
and
[
21
, Prop.B.9]

or
[
20
, Proposition C.1]
(resp. (L6) in Lemma
3.8

and the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem) that the first (resp. second) bracket on the right side converges to the zero.

Step 2
(
Prove that
\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathcal{U}^{X}\ni(\lambda,x)\mapsto A_{\lambda}(x)\in{\bf X}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}
is continuous
).
By
[
16
, (4.14)]
we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\nabla\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}(x)(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{t}\int^{t}_{0}\left[e^{-2s}\int^{s}_{0}e^{r}f_{\lambda,x}(r)dr\right]ds+e^{-t}\int^{t}_{0}e^{r}f_{\lambda,x}(r)dr

(3.42)

\displaystyle\quad+c_{1}(\lambda,x)e^{t}-c_{2}(\lambda,x)e^{-t}+\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}\left(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t)\right).

This and (
3.36
) lead to

\displaystyle 2c_{1}(\lambda,x)e^{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2e^{t}\int^{t}_{0}\left[e^{-2s}\int^{s}_{0}e^{r}f_{\lambda,x}(r)dr\right]ds-e^{-t}\int^{t}_{0}e^{r}f_{\lambda,x}(r)dr

(3.43)

\displaystyle-\int^{t}_{0}\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s))ds-\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}\left(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t)\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}(x)(t)+\frac{d}{dt}\nabla\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}(x)(t)

and

\displaystyle 2c_{2}(\lambda,x)e^{-t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{-t}\int^{t}_{0}e^{r}f_{\lambda,x}(r)dr-\int^{t}_{0}\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s))ds

(3.44)

\displaystyle+\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}\left(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t)\right)+\nabla\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}(x)(t)-\frac{d}{dt}\nabla\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}(x)(t).

Moreover, since

\displaystyle d\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda_{1}}(x)[\xi]-d\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda_{2}}(y)[\xi]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\tau}\big(\partial_{q}\check{L}(\lambda_{1},t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))-\partial_{q}\check{L}(\lambda_{2},t,y(t),\dot{y}(t))\big)\cdot\xi(t)dt

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{\tau}\big(\partial_{v}\check{L}\big(\lambda_{1},t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))-\partial_{v}\check{L}(\lambda_{2},t,y(t),\dot{y}(t))\big)\cdot\dot{\xi}(t)\big)\,dt,

we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda_{1}}(x)-\nabla\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda_{2}}(y)\|_{1,2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\int_{0}^{\tau}\left|\partial_{q}\check{L}(\lambda_{1},t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))-\partial_{q}\check{L}(\lambda_{2},t,y(t),\dot{y}(t))\right|^{2}dt\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle+\left(\int_{0}^{\tau}\left|\partial_{v}\check{L}(\lambda_{1},t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))-\partial_{v}\check{L}(\lambda_{2},t,y(t),\dot{y}(t))\right|^{2}dt\right)^{1/2}.

Fix a point
(\lambda_{1},x)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathcal{U}^{X}
. Then
\{(\lambda_{1},t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))\,|\,t\in[0,\tau]\}

is a compact subset of
\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau]\times B^{n}_{\iota/2}(0)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
. Since

\partial_{q}\check{L}
and
\partial_{v}\check{L}
are uniformly continuous in any compact neighborhood of this compact subset we deduce:
If
(\lambda_{2},y)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathcal{U}^{X}
converges to
(\lambda_{1},x)
in
\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathcal{U}^{X}
, then

\|\nabla\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda_{1}}(x)-\nabla\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda_{2}}(y)\|_{1,2}\to 0\quad\hbox{and so}\quad\|\nabla\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda_{1}}(x)-\nabla\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda_{2}}(y)\|_{C^{0}}\to 0.

This fact, (
3.37
) and (
3.43
)–(
3.44
) imply that

c_{1}(\lambda,x)
and
c_{2}(\lambda,x)
are continuous in
\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathcal{U}^{X}
.
From the latter claim, (
3.36
)–(
3.37
) and (
3.42
), it easily follows that as

(\lambda_{2},y)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathcal{U}^{X}
converges to
(\lambda_{1},x)
in
\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathcal{U}^{X}
,

\left\|\frac{d}{dt}\nabla\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda_{1}}(x)-\frac{d}{dt}\nabla\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda_{2}}(y)\right\|_{C^{0}}\to 0

and hence
\|\nabla\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda_{1}}(x)-\nabla\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda_{2}}(y)\|_{C^{1}}\to 0
.
∎

Proposition 3.11
.

For any given
\epsilon>0
there exists
\varepsilon>0
such that
if a critical point
x
of
\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}
satisfies

\|x\|_{1,2}<\varepsilon
then
\|x\|_{C^{2}}<\epsilon
. (
Note
:
\varepsilon
is independent of
\lambda\in\hat{\Lambda}
.)
Consequently, if
0\in\mathcal{U}^{X}
is an isolated critical point of
\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}|_{\mathcal{U}^{X}}

then
0\in\mathcal{U}
is also an isolated critical point of
\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}
.

By Proposition
3.9
(ii),

{\rm Crit}(\check{\mathcal{E}}):=\{(\lambda,x)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathcal{U}\,|\,d\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}(x)=0\}\subset\hat{\Lambda}\times C^{2}\big([0,\tau];B^{n}_{\iota/2}(0)\big)\cap\mathcal{U}^{X}.

Proposition
3.11
claims that
\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathcal{U}
and

\hat{\Lambda}\times C^{2}\big([0,\tau];B^{n}_{\iota/2}(0)\big)\cap\mathcal{U}^{X}
induce the equivalence topologies on

{\rm Crit}(\check{\mathcal{E}})
.

Proof of Proposition
3.11
.

The second claim may follow from the first one by contradiction.
Let us prove the first one. By Proposition
3.9
(ii),
x
is
C^{2}
.
Let
c_{1}(\lambda,x)
and
c_{2}(\lambda,x)
be given by (
3.43
) and (
3.44
), respectively.

Step 1
(
Prove that both
|c_{1}(\lambda,x)-c_{1}(\lambda,0)|
and

|c_{2}(\lambda,x)-c_{2}(\lambda,0)|
uniformly converge to zero
in
\lambda\in\hat{\Lambda}
as
\|x\|_{1,2}\to 0
).

Since
\nabla\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}(x)(t)\equiv 0
and
\frac{d}{dt}\nabla\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}(x)(t)\equiv 0
,
by (
3.43
) for any
t\in[0,\tau]
we have

\displaystyle 2|c_{1}(\lambda,x)-c_{1}(\lambda,0)|\leq 2|c_{1}(\lambda,x)e^{t}-c_{1}(\lambda,0)e^{t}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2e^{t}\int^{t}_{0}\left[e^{-2s}\int^{s}_{0}e^{r}|f_{\lambda,x}(r)-f_{\lambda,0}(r)|dr\right]ds+e^{-t}\int^{t}_{0}e^{r}|f_{\lambda,x}(r)-f_{\lambda,0}(r)|dr

\displaystyle+\int^{t}_{0}|\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s))-\check{L}_{\lambda}(s,0,0)|ds+|\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}\left(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t)\right)-\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}\left(t,0,0\right)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2e^{2\tau}\tau\int^{\tau}_{0}|f_{\lambda,x}(r)-f_{\lambda,0}(r)|dr+\int^{\tau}_{0}|f_{\lambda,x}(r)-f_{\lambda,0}(r)|dr

\displaystyle+\int^{\tau}_{0}|\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s))-\check{L}_{\lambda}(s,0,0)|ds+|\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}\left(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t)\right)-\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}\left(t,0,0\right)|.

Integrating this inequality over
[0,\tau]
yields

\displaystyle 2\tau|c_{1}(\lambda,x)-c_{1}(\lambda,0)|\leq 2\int^{\tau}_{0}|c_{1}(\lambda,x)e^{t}-c_{1}(\lambda,0)e^{t}|dt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2e^{2\tau}\tau^{2}\int^{\tau}_{0}|f_{\lambda,x}(r)-f_{\lambda,0}(r)|dr+\tau\int^{\tau}_{0}|f_{\lambda,x}(r)-f_{\lambda,0}(r)|dr

\displaystyle+(\tau+1)\int^{\tau}_{0}|\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s))-\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(s,0,0)|ds,

that is,

\displaystyle|c_{1}(\lambda,x)-c_{1}(\lambda,0)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(e^{2\tau}\tau+1)\int^{\tau}_{0}|f_{\lambda,x}(r)-f_{\lambda,0}(r)|dr

(3.45)

\displaystyle+\frac{(\tau+1)}{2\tau}\int^{\tau}_{0}|\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s))-\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(s,0,0)|ds.

Moreover, (
3.37
) leads to

\displaystyle\int^{\tau}_{0}|f_{\lambda,x}(t)-f_{\lambda,0}(t)|dt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int^{\tau}_{0}|\partial_{q}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))-\partial_{q}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,0,0)|dt

(3.46)

\displaystyle+\tau\int^{\tau}_{0}|\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))-\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,0,0)|dt.

From this and (
3.45
) we derive

\displaystyle|c_{1}(\lambda,x)-c_{1}(\lambda,0)|\leq(e^{2\tau}\tau+1)\int^{\tau}_{0}|\partial_{q}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))-\partial_{q}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,0,0)|dt

\displaystyle\qquad+\left((e^{2\tau}\tau+1)\tau+\frac{\tau+1}{2\tau}\right)\int^{\tau}_{0}|\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s))-\check{L}_{\lambda}(s,0,0)|ds.

(3.47)

Similarly, by (
3.44
) we obtain

\displaystyle 2|c_{2}(\lambda,x)-c_{2}(\lambda,0)|\leq\int^{t}_{0}e^{r}|f_{\lambda,x}(r)-f_{\lambda,0}(r)|dr

\displaystyle\qquad+e^{t}\int^{t}_{0}|\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s))-\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(s,0,0)|ds+e^{t}|\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}\left(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t)\right)-\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,0,0)|.

Integrating this inequality over
[0,\tau]
and using (
3.46
) lead to

\displaystyle 2\tau|c_{2}(\lambda,x)-c_{2}(\lambda,0)|\leq e^{\tau}\int^{\tau}_{0}|\partial_{q}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))-\partial_{q}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,0,0)|dt

\displaystyle+e^{\tau}(2\tau+1)\int^{\tau}_{0}|\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}\left(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t)\right)-\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,0,0)|dt.

(3.48)

Note that (L5) of Lemma
3.8
and

[
21
, Proposition B.9]
(
[
20
, Proposition C.1]
) imply that

\displaystyle\int^{\tau}_{0}\big|\big[\partial_{q}\check{L}_{\lambda}\bigl(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s)\bigr)-\partial_{q}\check{L}_{\lambda}\bigl(s,0,0\bigr)\big]\big|ds\to 0\quad\hbox{and}

\displaystyle\int^{\tau}_{0}\big|\big[\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}\bigl(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s)\bigr)-\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}\bigl(s,0,0\bigr)\big]\big|ds

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\tau}\Big(\int^{\tau}_{0}\Big|\big[\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}\bigl(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s)\bigr)-\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}\bigl(s,0,0\bigr)\big]\big|^{2}ds\Big)^{1/2}\to 0

uniformly in
\lambda\in\hat{\Lambda}
as
\|x\|_{1,2}\to 0
.
The required claim follows from this and (
3.1.3
)-(
3.1.3
).

Step 2
(
Prove that for any given
\nu^{\prime}>0
there exists
\varepsilon^{\prime}>0

such that
\nabla\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}(x)=0
and
\|x\|_{1,2}\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}
imply
\|x\|_{C^{1}}<\nu^{\prime}
).

Since
\nabla\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}(x)=0
and
\nabla\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}(0)=0
, by (
3.42
) we have

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{t}\int^{t}_{0}\left[e^{-2s}\int^{s}_{0}e^{r}f_{\lambda,x}(r)dr\right]ds+e^{-t}\int^{t}_{0}e^{r}f_{\lambda,x}(r)dr

\displaystyle\quad+c_{1}(\lambda,x)e^{t}-c_{2}(\lambda,x)e^{-t}+\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}\left(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t)\right).

and

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{t}\int^{t}_{0}\left[e^{-2s}\int^{s}_{0}e^{r}f_{\lambda,0}(r)dr\right]ds+e^{-t}\int^{t}_{0}e^{r}f_{\lambda,0}(r)dr

\displaystyle\quad+c_{1}(\lambda,0)e^{t}-c_{2}(\lambda,0)e^{-t}+\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}\left(t,0,0\right).

For each
0\leq t\leq\tau
, the former minus the latter gives rise to

\displaystyle|\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}\left(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t)\right)-\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}\left(t,0,0\right)|\leq e^{t}|c_{1}(\lambda,x)-c_{1}(\lambda,0)|+e^{-t}|c_{2}(\lambda,x)-c_{2}(\lambda,0)|

\displaystyle\qquad+e^{t}\int^{t}_{0}\left[e^{-2s}\int^{s}_{0}e^{r}|f_{\lambda,x}(r)-f_{\lambda,0}(r)|dr\right]ds+e^{-t}\int^{t}_{0}e^{r}|f_{\lambda,x}(r)-f_{\lambda,0}(r)|dr

\displaystyle\leq e^{\tau}|c_{1}(\lambda,x)-c_{1}(\lambda,0)|+|c_{2}(\lambda,x)-c_{2}(\lambda,0)|+e^{\tau}(\tau+1)\int^{\tau}_{0}|f_{\lambda,x}(r)-f_{\lambda,0}(r)|dr

and (by (
3.46
)) hence

\displaystyle|\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}\left(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t)\right)-\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}\left(t,0,0\right)|\leq e^{\tau}|c_{1}(\lambda,x)-c_{1}(\lambda,0)|+|c_{2}(\lambda,x)-c_{2}(\lambda,0)|

\displaystyle\qquad+e^{\tau}(\tau+1)\int^{\tau}_{0}|\partial_{q}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))-\partial_{q}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,0,0)|dt

\displaystyle\qquad+e^{\tau}(\tau+1)\tau\int^{\tau}_{0}|\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))-\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,0,0)|dt.

(3.49)

Note that (L3) in Lemma
3.8
and the mean value theorem of integrals may lead to

\displaystyle\check{c}|v|^{2}\leq\int^{1}_{0}\left(\partial_{vv}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,q,sv)[v],v\right)_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}ds=\left(\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,q,v)-\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,q,0),v\right)_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}

and so

\check{c}|v|\leq\left|\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,q,v)-\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,q,0)\right|

for any
(\lambda,t,q,v)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau]\times B^{n}_{3\iota/4}(0)\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
. In particular, for all
t\in[0,\tau]
we have

\displaystyle\check{c}|\dot{x}(t)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))-\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),0)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))-\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,0,0)\right|+\left|\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),0)-\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,0,0)\right|.

By this and (
3.1.3
) we arrive at

\displaystyle\check{c}|\dot{x}(t)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle e^{\tau}|c_{1}(\lambda,x)-c_{1}(\lambda,0)|+|c_{2}(\lambda,x)-c_{2}(\lambda,0)|+\left|\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),0)-\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,0,0)\right|

(3.50)

\displaystyle+e^{\tau}(\tau+1)\int^{\tau}_{0}|\partial_{q}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))-\partial_{q}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,0,0)|dt

\displaystyle+e^{\tau}(\tau+1)\tau\int^{\tau}_{0}|\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))-\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,0,0)|dt.

Since
\|x\|_{C^{0}}\leq(\sqrt{\tau}+1/\sqrt{\tau})\|x\|_{1,2}
,
as in the final proof of Step 1, the required claim may follow from
(
3.50
) and the conclusion in Step 1.

Step 3
(
Complete the proof for the first claim
).
Note that
x
satisfies

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\big(\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))\big)-\partial_{q}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))

(3.51)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{vv}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))\ddot{x}(t)+\partial_{vq}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))\dot{x}(t)+\partial_{vt}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))

\displaystyle-\partial_{q}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t)).

In particular, taking
x=0
we get

\displaystyle 0=\partial_{vt}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,0,0)-\partial_{q}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,0,0).

(3.52)

(
3.51
) minus (
3.52
) gives rise to

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{vv}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))\ddot{x}(t)+\partial_{vq}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))\dot{x}(t)

(3.53)

\displaystyle+\partial_{vt}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))-\partial_{vt}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,0,0)

\displaystyle-\partial_{q}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))+\partial_{q}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,0,0).

Since (L2) of Lemma
3.8
implies

|[\partial_{vv}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))]^{-1}\xi|\leq\frac{1}{\check{c}}|\xi|\;\forall\xi\in{\mathbb{R}^{n}}
, (
3.53
) and (L3) in Lemma
3.8
lead to

\displaystyle|\ddot{x}(t)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\check{c}}|\partial_{vq}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))|\cdot|\dot{x}(t)|+\frac{1}{\check{c}}|\partial_{vt}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))-\partial_{vt}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,0,0)|

(3.54)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\check{c}}|\partial_{q}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))-\partial_{q}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,0,0)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\check{C}}{\check{c}}(1+|\dot{x}(t)|)|\cdot|\dot{x}(t)|+\frac{1}{\check{c}}|\partial_{vt}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))-\partial_{vt}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,0,0)|

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\check{c}}|\partial_{q}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))-\partial_{q}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,0,0)|.

Recall that
\hat{\Lambda}
is either compact or sequential compact and that

\partial_{q}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,q,v)
and
\partial_{vt}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,q,v)

is continuous in
(\lambda,t,q,v)
by (L0) in Lemma
3.8
.
The desired claim easily follows from (
3.54
) and the result in Step 2.
∎

Remark 3.12
.

(i)

The existence and continuity of the partial derivative
\partial_{t}\check{L}(\cdot)
in (L0) of Lemma
3.8

are not used in the proofs of Propositions
3.9
,
3.10
,
3.11
.

(ii)

The existence and continuity of the partial derivatives
\partial_{tv}\check{L}(\cdot)
and
\partial_{vt}\check{L}(\cdot)
in (L0)
of Lemma
3.8
are not used in the proofs of Propositions
3.9
,
3.10
;
but they are necessary for the proof of Proposition
3.11
.

Proof of Theorem
3.5
(I).

Since there exist a sequence
\{(\lambda_{k},x_{k})\}_{k\geq 1}\subset\Lambda\times C^{1}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}([0,\tau];B^{n}_{\iota}(0))

converging to
(\mu,0)
such that each
x_{k}
is a nonzero solution of
the problem (
3.13
)–(
3.1.1
) with
\lambda=\lambda_{k}
, i.e.,

\nabla\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{k}}(x_{k})=0
,
k=1,2,\cdots
,
by (L1) in Lemma
3.8
with
\hat{\Lambda}=\{\mu,\lambda_{k}\,|\,k\in\mathbb{N}\}
we deduce that
\nabla\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda_{k}}(x_{k})=0
for
k
large enough.
Therefore
(\mu,0)
is a bifurcation point along sequence of

\nabla\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}(x)=0
in
\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathcal{U}
.
By (i) and (v)-(vii) of Proposition
3.9

we see that the conditions of
[
19
, Theorem 3.1]
(
[
22
, Theorem C.6]
) are satisfied with
