\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}={\check{\mathcal{E}}}_{\lambda}
and
H=X={\bf H}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}
,
U=\mathcal{U}
and
\lambda^{\ast}=\mu
.
Then
m^{0}_{\tau}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\mu},0)>0
and so

m^{0}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda},0)>0
by (
3.30
).
∎

Proof of Theorem
3.7
.

Since
\Lambda
is a real interval and
\mu\in{\rm Int}(\Lambda)
we can take a small
\varepsilon>0

so that
\hat{\Lambda}:=[\mu-\varepsilon,\mu+\varepsilon]\subset\Lambda
.
Propositions
3.9
,
3.10

shows that
(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{U}^{X},\{\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}\,|\,\lambda\in\hat{\Lambda}\})
satisfies the conditions
in
[
20
, Theorem 3.6]
with
\lambda^{\ast}=\mu
except for the condition (f).
The latter may follow from (
3.30
) and the assumption, that is,

m^{0}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\mu},0)\neq 0
and

m^{0}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda},0)=0
for all
\lambda\in\hat{\Lambda}\setminus\{\mu\}
near
\mu
,
and
m^{-}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda},0)
take, respectively, values
m^{-}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\mu},0)
and

m^{-}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\mu},0)+m^{0}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\mu},0)

as
\lambda\in\hat{\Lambda}
varies in two deleted half neighborhoods of
\mu
.
Therefore from
[
22
, Theorem C.7]
(
[
21
, Theorem 3.6]
) we deduce that one of the following occurs:

(i)

\nabla\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\mu}
has a sequence of nontrivial zero points converging to
0
in
\mathcal{U}
.

(ii)

For every
\lambda\in\hat{\Lambda}\setminus\{\mu\}
near
\mu
,

A_{\lambda}
has a zero point
y_{\lambda}\neq 0
, which converge to zero in
\mathcal{U}^{X}
as
\lambda\to\mu
.

(iii)

For a given neighborhood
\mathfrak{M}
of
0\in\mathcal{U}^{X}
, there is
a one-sided neighborhood
\Lambda^{0}
of
\mu
in

\hat{\Lambda}
(therefore in
\Lambda
) such that
for any
\lambda\in\Lambda^{0}\setminus\{\mu\}
,
A_{\lambda}
has
at least two distinct nontrivial zero points in
\mathfrak{M}
,
y_{\lambda}^{1}
and
y_{\lambda}^{2}
,
which can also be required to satisfy
\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}(y^{1}_{\lambda})\neq\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}(y^{2}_{\lambda})

provided that
m^{0}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\mu},0)>1
and
A_{\lambda}
has only
finitely many nontrivial zero points in
\mathfrak{M}
.

As above the required results may follow from Proposition
3.11
and (L0) in Lemma
3.8
.
∎

To prove Theorem
3.5
(II) and Theorem
3.6
,
noting that because of Propositions
3.9
,
3.10
,
(specially Proposition
3.9
(iv) implies that

\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}|_{\mathcal{U}^{X}}\in C^{2}(\mathcal{U}^{X},\mathbb{R})

and
B_{\lambda}
is continuous as a map from
\mathcal{U}^{X}
to
\mathscr{L}_{s}({\bf H}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}})

because
A_{\lambda}^{\prime}=B_{\lambda}
),
we may, respectively, apply
[
22
, Theorem C.4]
and
[
22
, Theorem C.5]
to

(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{U}^{X},\{\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}\,|\,\lambda\in\hat{\Lambda}\})
to obtain (I) and (II) of the following theorem.

Theorem 3.13
.

(I)

(
Sufficient condition
):

Suppose that
\hat{\Lambda}
is first countable and that there exist two sequences in
\Lambda
converging to
\mu
,
(\lambda_{k}^{-})
and

(\lambda_{k}^{+})
, such that one of the following conditions is satisfied:

(I.1)

For each
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
either
0
is not an isolated critical point of
\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}_{k}}
,
or
0
is not an isolated critical point of
\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}_{k}}
,
or
0
is an isolated critical point of
\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}_{k}}
and
\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}_{k}}
and

C_{m}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}_{k}},0;{\bf K})
and
C_{m}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}_{k}},0;{\bf K})
are not isomorphic for some Abel group
{\bf K}
and some
m\in\mathbb{Z}
. Moreover, in the third case, “
C_{m}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}_{k}},0;{\bf K})
and

C_{m}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}_{k}},0;{\bf K})
” may be replaced by
“
C_{\ast}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}_{k}}|_{\mathcal{U}^{X}},0;{\bf K})
and

C_{\ast}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}_{k}}|_{\mathcal{U}^{X}},0;{\bf K})
”.

(I.2)

For each
k\in\mathbb{N}
,

[m^{-}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}_{k}},0),m^{-}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}_{k}},0)+m^{0}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}_{k}},0)]\cap[m^{-}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}_{k}},0),m^{-}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}_{k}},0)+m^{0}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}_{k}},0)]=\emptyset
,
and there exists
\lambda\in\{\lambda^{+}_{k},\lambda^{-}_{k}\}
such that
0
is an either non-isolated or homological visible critical point of

\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}
. Moreover,
\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}
can be replaced by
\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}|_{\mathcal{U}^{X}}

in the second condition.

(I.3)

For each
k\in\mathbb{N}
,

[m^{-}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}_{k}},0),m^{-}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}_{k}},0)+m^{0}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}_{k}},0)]\cap[m^{-}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}_{k}},0),m^{-}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}_{k}},0)+m^{0}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}_{k}},0)]=\emptyset
,
and either
m^{0}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}_{k}},0)=0
or
m^{0}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}_{k}},0)=0
.

Then there exists a sequence
\{(\lambda_{k},x_{k})\}_{k\geq 1}
in
\check{\Lambda}\times(\mathcal{U}\cap C^{2}([0,\tau],{\mathbb{R}}^{n}))

such that
\lambda_{k}\to\mu
,
0<\|x_{k}\|_{C^{2}}\to 0
and
\nabla\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}(x_{k})=0
for
k=1,2,\cdots
,
where
\check{\Lambda}=\{\mu,\lambda^{+}_{k},\lambda^{-}_{k}\,|\,k\in\mathbb{N}\}
.
In particular,
(\mu,0)
is a bifurcation point of the problem
\nabla\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}(x)=0

in
\check{\Lambda}\times C^{2}([0,\tau],{\mathbb{R}}^{n})
with respect to the branch
\{(\lambda,0)\,|\,\lambda\in\check{\Lambda}\}

(and so
\{(\lambda,0)\,|\,\lambda\in\hat{\Lambda}\}
).

(II)

(
Existence for bifurcations
):

For
\lambda^{-},\lambda^{+}
in a path-connected component of
\hat{\Lambda}

suppose that one of the following conditions is satisfied:

(II.1)

Either
0
is not an isolated critical point of
\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}}
,
or
0
is not an isolated critical point of
\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}}
,
or
0
is an isolated critical point of
\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}}
and
\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}}
and

C_{m}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}},0;{\bf K})
and
C_{m}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}},0;{\bf K})
are not isomorphic for some Abel group
{\bf K}
and some
m\in\mathbb{Z}
. Moreover, in the final case, “
C_{m}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}},0;{\bf K})
and

C_{m}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}},0;{\bf K})
” may be replaced by
“
C_{\ast}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}}|_{\mathcal{U}^{X}},0;{\bf K})
and

C_{\ast}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}}|_{\mathcal{U}^{X}},0;{\bf K})
”.

(II.2)

[m^{-}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}},0),m^{-}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}},0)+m^{0}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}},0)]\cap[m^{-}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}},0),m^{-}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}},0)+m^{0}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}},0)]=\emptyset
,
and there exists
\lambda\in\{\lambda^{+},\lambda^{-}\}
such that
0
is an either non-isolated or homological visible critical point of

\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}
. Moreover, in the second condition,
\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}
can be replaced by
\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}|_{\mathcal{U}^{X}}
.

(II.3)

[m^{-}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}},0),m^{-}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}},0)+m^{0}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}},0)]\cap[m^{-}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}},0),m^{-}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}},0)+m^{0}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}},0)]=\emptyset
,
and either
m^{0}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}},0)=0
or
m^{0}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}},0)=0
.

Then for any path
\alpha:[0,1]\to\hat{\Lambda}
connecting
\lambda^{+}
to
\lambda^{-}

there exists a sequence
\{(t_{k},x_{k})\}_{k\geq 1}
in
[0,1]\times\mathcal{U}

converging to
(\bar{t},0)
for some
\bar{t}\in[0,1]
, such that each
x_{k}

is a nonzero solution of
\nabla\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha(t_{k})}(x)=0
,
k=1,2,\cdots
.
(In fact,
\|x_{k}\|_{C^{2}}\to 0
by Proposition
3.11
.)
Moreover,
\alpha(\bar{t})
is not equal to
\lambda^{+}
(resp.
\lambda^{-}
) if
m^{0}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}},0)=0
(resp.
m^{0}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}},0)=0
).

Proof.

Step 1
(
Prove
(I)).
Because of Propositions
3.9
,
3.10
,
(specially Proposition
3.9
(iv) implies that

\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}|_{\mathcal{U}^{X}}\in C^{2}(\mathcal{U}^{X},\mathbb{R})

and
B_{\lambda}
is continuous as a map from
\mathcal{U}^{X}
to
\mathscr{L}_{s}({\bf H}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}})

since
A_{\lambda}^{\prime}=B_{\lambda}
),
for the case (I.2) [resp. (I.3)] we apply
[
22
, Theorem C.4(B.1),(B.2)]

(resp.
[
22
, Theorem C.4(B.3)]
) to obtain:

(
\ast
)

There exists a sequence
\{(\lambda_{k},x_{k})\}_{k\geq 1}\subset\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathcal{U}\setminus\{(\mu,0)\}

converging to
(\mu,0)
such that
x_{k}\neq 0
and
\nabla\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}(x_{k})=0
for
k=1,2,\cdots
.

For the case (I.1), if
\{\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}_{k}}\}_{k\geq 1}
or
\{\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}_{k}}\}_{k\geq 1}

has a subsequence such that each term of it has
0
as a non-isolated critical point,
then we have (
\ast
) naturally.
Otherwise, for each sufficiently large integer
l
,
0
is an isolated critical point of
\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}_{l}}
and
\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}_{l}}
and
C_{m_{l}}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}_{l}},0;{\bf K}_{l})
and

C_{m_{l}}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}_{l}},0;{\bf K}_{l})
are not isomorphic
for some Abel group
{\bf K}_{l}
and some
m_{l}\in\mathbb{Z}
, which implies
that for each such an integer
l
,

0
is an isolated critical point of
\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}_{l}}|_{\mathcal{U}^{X}}
and

\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}_{l}}|_{\mathcal{U}^{X}}
and
C_{m_{l}}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}_{l}}|_{\mathcal{U}^{X}},0;{\bf K}_{l})
and
C_{m_{l}}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}_{l}}|_{\mathcal{U}^{X}},0;{\bf K}_{l})
are not isomorphic
because by Proposition
3.9
we may use
[
12
, Corollary 2.8]
to deduce that

C_{m_{l}}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}_{l}}|_{\mathcal{U}^{X}},0;{\bf K}_{l})\cong C_{m_{l}}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}_{l}},0;{\bf K}_{l})
and
C_{m_{l}}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}_{l}}|_{\mathcal{U}^{X}},0;{\bf K}_{l})\cong C_{m_{l}}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}_{l}},0;{\bf K}_{l})
.
Then we may use
[
22
, Theorem C.4(A)]
to get a contradiction provided that (
\ast
) is not true.

In summary, we get (
\ast
) in this case, and therefore the desired statements by Proposition
3.11
.

Step 2
(
Prove
(II)). Applying
[
22
, Theorem C.5]
to
(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{U}^{X},\{\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}\,|\,\lambda\in\hat{\Lambda}\})
a similar proof to that of Step 1 yields the required results.
∎

Proof of Theorem
3.5
(II).

Suppose that (II.1) is satisfied. If
\{\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}}\}_{k\geq 1}
or
\{\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{k}^{-}}\}_{k\geq 1}

has a subsequence such that each term of it has
0
as a non-isolated critical point,
then the required result may follow from (
3.29
) and Proposition
3.11
.
Otherwise, for each sufficiently large integer
l
,
0
is an isolated critical point of
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{l}^{+}}
and

\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{l}^{-}}
and
C_{m_{l}}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{l}^{+}},0;{\bf K}_{l})
and
C_{m_{l}}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{l}^{-}},0;{\bf K}_{l})
are not isomorphic for some Abel group
{\bf K}_{l}
and some
m_{l}\in\mathbb{Z}
, which implies by (
3.29
) that

0
is an isolated critical point of
\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}_{l}}|_{\mathcal{U}^{X}}
and

\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}_{l}}|_{\mathcal{U}^{X}}
and
C_{m_{l}}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}_{l}}|_{\mathcal{U}^{X}},0;{\bf K}_{l})
and
C_{m_{l}}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}_{l}}|_{\mathcal{U}^{X}},0;{\bf K}_{l})
are not isomorphic.
That is, the condition (I.1) in Theorem
3.13
is satisfied.
Hence (
3.29
) and the conclusion in Theorem
3.13
(I) lead to the required result.

Next, let (II.2) be satisfied. If the assumptions in the second sentence in last paragraph are true we are done.
Otherwise, for each sufficiently large integer
l
,
0
is an isolated critical point of
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{l}^{+}}
and

\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{l}^{-}}
and either
C_{m_{l}}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{+}},0;{\bf K}_{l})\neq 0

for some Abel group
{\bf K}_{l}
and some
m_{l}\in\mathbb{Z}
or
and
C_{n_{l}}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{l}^{-}},0;{\bf K}^{\prime}_{l})\neq 0
for some Abel group
{\bf K}^{\prime}_{l}
and some
n_{l}\in\mathbb{Z}
.
Therefore by (
3.29
)
0
is an isolated critical point of
\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}_{l}}|_{\mathcal{U}^{X}}
and

\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}_{l}}|_{\mathcal{U}^{X}}
and either
C_{m_{l}}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}_{l}}|_{\mathcal{U}^{X}},0;{\bf K}_{l})\neq 0
or
C_{n_{l}}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}_{l}}|_{\mathcal{U}^{X}},0;{\bf K}^{\prime}_{l})\neq 0
. These mean that
the condition (I.2) in Theorem
3.13
is satisfied. As above, Theorem
3.13
(I) and (
3.29
) yield the desired conclusions.

For the case (I.3), by (
3.30
) we see that the condition (I.3) in Theorem
3.13
is satisfied.
The required statements are derived as above.
∎

Proof of Theorem
3.6
.

For the case (i) in Theorem
3.6
. The first two cases easily follow from
Proposition
3.11
and (
3.29
).
For the third case,
0
is also an isolated critical point of
\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}}

and
\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}}
by Proposition
3.11
and (
3.29
),
and
C_{m}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}}|_{\mathcal{U}^{X}},0;{\bf K})
and
C_{m}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{-}}|_{\mathcal{U}^{X}},0;{\bf K})
are not isomorphic. Theorem
3.13
(II) leads to the required results.

For the case (ii) in Theorem
3.6
, by (
3.29
)
“
0
is an either non-isolated or homological visible critical point of

\mathcal{E}^{\ast}_{\lambda}
” is equivalent to
“
0
is an either non-isolated or homological visible critical point of

\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{+}}|_{\mathcal{U}^{X}}
”.
Because of these and (
3.30
), Theorem
3.13
(II) yields the required results.

The case (iii) in Theorem
3.6
follows from (
3.30
) and Theorem
3.13
(II).
∎

3.1.4
Proof of Theorem
1.5

By contradiction suppose that there exists a path
\alpha:[0,1]\to\Lambda
connecting
\lambda^{+}
to
\lambda^{-}
such that
each point
(\alpha(s),\gamma_{\alpha(s)})
,
s\in[0,1]
, is not a bifurcation point of the problem (
1.12
)–(
1.13
) in

\alpha([0,1])\times C^{2}_{S_{0}\times S_{1}}([0,\tau];M)
with respect to the branch
\{(\lambda,\gamma_{\lambda})\,|\,\lambda\in\alpha([0,1])\}
.
Then for some small
\epsilon>0
we have:

\left.\begin{array}[]{ll}&\gamma\in C^{2}_{S_{0}\times S_{1}}([0,\tau];M)\;\hbox{satisfies
$\|\gamma-\gamma_{\alpha(s)}\|_{C^{2}([0,\tau];\mathbb{R}^{N})}\leq\epsilon$}\\
&\hbox{and (\ref{e:LagrGener})--(\ref{e:LagrGenerB}) with $\lambda=\alpha(s)$ for some $s\in[0,1]$}\\
&\Longrightarrow\;\gamma=\gamma_{\lambda}\;\hbox{for some $\lambda\in\alpha([0,1])$.}\end{array}\right\}

(3.55)

Fix a point
\mu\in\alpha([0,1])
. Let
\overline{\gamma}
be as in (
3.2
).
We have a compact neighborhood
\hat{\Lambda}
of
\mu
in
\alpha([0,1])
such that
(
3.3
) is satisfied for all
(\lambda,t)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau]
, i.e.,

\displaystyle{\rm dist}_{g}(\gamma_{\lambda}(t),\overline{\gamma}(t))<\iota,\quad\forall(\lambda,t)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau].

(3.56)

Then the reduction in Section
3.1.1
is valid after we use
\hat{\Lambda}
to replace
\Lambda
therein.
Therefore for the functionals in (
3.10
), (
3.55
) implies that for some
\bar{\epsilon}>0
we have

\left.\begin{array}[]{ll}&x\in C^{1}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}([0,\tau];B^{n}_{\iota}(0))\;\hbox{satisfies $\|x\|_{C^{2}([0,\tau];\mathbb{R}^{N})}\leq\bar{\epsilon}$}\\
&\hbox{and $d\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda}(x)=0$ for some $\lambda\in\hat{\Lambda}$}\quad\Longrightarrow\;x=0.\end{array}\right\}

(3.57)

It follows from this fact, (
3.29
) and Proposition
3.11
that
there exists
\hat{\epsilon}>0
such that

\left.\begin{array}[]{ll}&x\in\mathcal{U}\;\hbox{satisfies $\|x\|_{1,2}\leq\hat{\epsilon}$ and}\\
&\hbox{$d\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}(x)=0$ for some $\lambda\in\hat{\Lambda}$}\quad\Longrightarrow\;x=0.\end{array}\right\}

(3.58)

Since we can shrink
\hat{\epsilon}>0
so small that the ball

\bar{B}_{\hat{\epsilon}}({\bf H}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}):=\{\xi\in{\bf H}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}\,|\,\|\xi\|_{1,2}\leq\hat{\epsilon}\}

is contained in
\mathcal{U}
, (
3.58
) means that
0\in\mathcal{U}

is a unique critical point of
\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}
in
\bar{B}_{\hat{\epsilon}}({\bf H}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}})

for each
\lambda\in\hat{\Lambda}
.

Take
\bar{s}\in[0,1]
such that
\alpha(\bar{s})=\mu
. We have a connected compact neighborhood
N(\bar{s})
of
\bar{s}
in
[0,1]

such that
\alpha(N(\bar{s}))\subset\hat{\Lambda}
.
Because of (
3.58
), as in the proof of
[
22
, Theorem C.5]
we have a correspondent result of
[
22
, (C.17)]

and therefore obtain that for any Abel group
{\bf K}
and any
s,s^{\prime}\in N(\bar{s})
,

\displaystyle C_{q}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha(s)},0;{\bf K})\cong C_{q}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha(s^{\prime})},0;{\bf K}),\;\;\forall q\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}.

(3.59)

As in the previous proofs we may use
[
12
, Corollary 2.8]
to deduce that

\displaystyle C_{q}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}|_{\mathcal{U}^{X}},0;{\bf K})\cong C_{q}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda},0;{\bf K}),\quad\forall\lambda\in\hat{\Lambda}.

This, (
3.29
) and (
3.11
) lead to

\displaystyle C_{q}(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}|_{\mathcal{U}^{X}},0;{\bf K})\cong C_{q}(\tilde{\cal E}^{\ast}_{\lambda},0;{\bf K})\cong C_{q}({\cal E}_{\lambda},\gamma_{\lambda};{\bf K}),\quad\forall\lambda\in\hat{\Lambda}.

Combining this with equation (
3.59
), we obtain

\displaystyle C_{q}({\cal E}_{\alpha(s)},\gamma_{\alpha(s)};{\bf K})\cong C_{q}({\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha(s^{\prime})},\gamma_{\alpha(s^{\prime})};{\bf K}),\;\;\forall s,s^{\prime}\in N(\bar{s}),\;\forall q\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}.

(3.60)

Because the point
\mu\in\alpha([0,1])
is arbitrary, (
3.60
) implies

\displaystyle C_{q}({\cal E}_{\lambda},\gamma_{\lambda};{\bf K})\cong C_{q}({\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda^{\prime}},\gamma_{\lambda^{\prime}};{\bf K}),\;\;\forall\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}\in\alpha([0,1]),\;\forall q\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}.

(3.61)

Almost repeating the arguments below (C.18) in the proof of
[
22
, Theorem C.5]
we may see that
(
3.61
) contradicts to each of the conditions (i)-(iii) in Theorem
1.5
.
Hence the assumption above (
3.55
) is not true. Theorem
1.5
is proved.

\Box

3.2
Proof of Theorem
1.9

We first admit the following version of the Morse index theorem due to Duistermaat
[
6
]
,
which can directly lead to the Morse index theorem in Finsler geometry
(
[
23
, Corollary 9.7]
).

Theorem 3.14
.

Let the functionals
\mathcal{L}_{S_{0},s}
be as in (
1.16
). Then

m^{-}(\mathcal{L}_{S_{0},\lambda},\gamma_{\lambda})=\sum_{0<s<\lambda}m^{0}(\mathcal{L}_{S_{0},s},\gamma_{s}),\quad\forall\lambda\in(0,\tau].

Theorem
3.14
implies that there exist only finitely many
\mu\in(0,\tau)

such that
m^{0}(\mathcal{L}_{S_{0},\mu},\gamma_{\mu})\neq 0
, and hence the conclusion (i) follows.

Proof of Theorem
1.9
.

In the arguments above (
3.9
) taking
\gamma_{\mu}=\gamma
and
S_{1}=\{\gamma(\tau)\}

we have a unique map

{\bf u}\in C^{1}_{V_{0}\times\{0\}}([0,\tau];B^{n}_{\iota}(0))\cap C^{3}([0,\tau];B^{n}_{\iota}(0))

such that
{\bf u}(0)={\bf u}(\tau)=0
and
\gamma(t)=\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(t,{\bf u}(t))
for all
t\in[0,\tau]
.
For
\lambda\in(0,\tau]
let

W^{1,2}_{V_{0}\times\{{\bf u}(\lambda)\}}([0,\lambda];B^{n}_{2\iota}(0))=\big\{u\in W^{1,2}_{V_{0}\times\{{\bf u}(\lambda)\}}([0,\lambda];\mathbb{R}^{n})\,\big|\,u([0,\lambda])\subset B^{n}_{2\iota}(0))\big\},

which contains
C^{1}_{V_{0}\times\{{\bf u}(\lambda)\}}([0,\lambda];B^{n}_{2\iota}(0))
as a dense subset.
Using
[
28
, Theorems 4.2, 4.3]
, as before we deduce that

W^{1,2}_{S_{0}\times\{\gamma(\lambda)\}}([0,\lambda];M):=\big\{\alpha\in W^{1,2}([0,\lambda];M)\,\big|\,\alpha(0)\in S_{0},\alpha(\lambda)=\gamma(\lambda)\big\}

is a
C^{4}
Hilbert manifold and obtain a
C^{2}
chart

\displaystyle\Phi_{\lambda}:W^{1,2}_{V_{0}\times\{0\}}([0,\lambda];B^{n}_{\iota}(0))\to W^{1,2}_{S_{0}\times\{\gamma(\lambda)\}}([0,\lambda];M)

given by
\Phi_{\lambda}(\xi)(t)=\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(t,{\bf u}_{\lambda}(t)+\xi(t))\;\forall t\in[0,\lambda]
for each
\lambda\in(0,\tau]
,
where
{\bf u}_{\lambda}:={\bf u}|_{[0,\lambda]}
. Then

\Phi_{\lambda}(0)=\gamma_{\lambda}:=\gamma|_{[0,\lambda]}
.
Note that
\Phi_{\lambda}:C^{1}_{V_{0}\times\{0\}}([0,\lambda];B^{n}_{\iota}(0))\to C^{1}_{S_{0}\times\{\gamma(\lambda)\}}([0,\lambda];M)

is also a
C^{2}
chart by the
\omega
-Lemma (cf.
[
26
]
).
Clearly, the Banach space isomorphism

\Gamma_{\lambda}:W^{1,2}_{V_{0}\times\{0\}}([0,1];\mathbb{R}^{n})\to W^{1,2}_{V_{0}\times\{0\}}([0,\lambda];\mathbb{R}^{n}),\;\xi\mapsto\xi(\lambda^{-1}\cdot)

maps
W^{1,2}_{V_{0}\times\{0\}}([0,1];B^{n}_{\iota}(0))
onto
W^{1,2}_{V_{0}\times\{0\}}([0,\lambda];B^{n}_{\iota}(0))
.
Put

{\bf L}_{\lambda}:W^{1,2}_{V_{0}\times\{0\}}([0,1];B^{n}_{\iota}(0))\to\mathbb{R},\;\xi\mapsto\mathcal{L}_{S_{0},\lambda}\circ\Phi_{\lambda}\circ\Gamma_{\lambda}(\xi).

It is easy to check that

\displaystyle{\bf L}_{\lambda}(\xi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int^{\lambda}_{0}L\big(t,\Phi_{\lambda}(\Gamma_{\lambda}(\xi))(t),\frac{d}{dt}\Phi_{\lambda}(\Gamma_{\lambda}(\xi))(t)\big)dt

(3.62)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int^{1}_{0}\lambda L\big(\lambda s,\Phi_{\lambda}(\Gamma_{\lambda}(\xi))(\lambda s),\frac{d}{dt}\Phi_{\lambda}(\Gamma_{\lambda}(\xi))(t)\Big|_{t=\lambda s}\big)ds.

Note that
\Phi_{\lambda}(\Gamma_{\lambda}(\xi))(t)=\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(t,{\bf u}_{\lambda}(t)+\Gamma_{\lambda}(\xi)(t))=\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(t,{\bf u}_{\lambda}(t)+\xi(t/\lambda))

for all
t\in[0,\lambda]
. We have

\frac{d}{dt}\Phi_{\lambda}(\Gamma_{\lambda}(\xi))(t)=D_{1}\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(t,{\bf u}_{\lambda}(t)+\xi(t/\lambda))+D_{2}\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(t,{\bf u}_{\lambda}(t)+\xi(t/\lambda))[\dot{\bf u}_{\lambda}(t)+\frac{1}{\lambda}\dot{\xi}(t/\lambda)].

It follows that for any
s\in[0,1]
,

\displaystyle\Phi_{\lambda}(\Gamma_{\lambda}(\xi))(\lambda s)=\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(\lambda s,{\bf u}(\lambda s)+\xi(s)),

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\Phi_{\lambda}(\Gamma_{\lambda}(\xi))(t)\Big|_{t=\lambda s}=D_{1}\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(\lambda s,{\bf u}(\lambda s)+\xi(s))+D_{2}\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(\lambda s,{\bf u}(\lambda s)+\xi(s))[\dot{\bf u}(\lambda s)+\frac{1}{\lambda}\dot{\xi}(s)].

Define
\hat{L}:(0,\tau)\times[0,1]\times B^{n}_{\iota}(0)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
by

\displaystyle\hat{L}(\lambda,s,q,v)=\hat{L}_{\lambda}(s,q,v)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda L\big(\lambda s,\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(\lambda s,{\bf u}(\lambda s)+q),D_{1}\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(\lambda s,{\bf u}(\lambda s)+q)+\frac{1}{\lambda}D_{2}\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(\lambda s,{\bf u}(\lambda s)+q)[v+\lambda\dot{\bf u}(\lambda s)]\big).

Since both
{\bf u}
and
L
are
C^{3}
,
\hat{L}
is
C^{2}
and fiberwise strictly convex. Clearly, (
3.62
) becomes

\displaystyle{\bf L}_{\lambda}(\xi)=\int^{1}_{0}\hat{L}_{\lambda}\left(t,\xi(t),\dot{\xi}(t)\right)dt.

(3.64)

Let
\gamma_{\lambda}:=\gamma|_{[0,\lambda]}
. Then
\Phi_{\lambda}\circ\Gamma_{\lambda}(0)=\gamma_{\lambda}
and

\displaystyle d{\bf L}_{\lambda}(0)=d\mathcal{L}_{S_{0},\lambda}(\gamma_{\lambda})\circ d(\Phi_{\lambda}\circ\Gamma_{\lambda})(0)=0,

\displaystyle d^{2}{\bf L}_{\lambda}(0)[\xi,\eta]=d^{2}\mathcal{L}_{S_{0},\lambda}(\gamma_{\lambda})\big[d\Phi_{\lambda}(0)[\Gamma_{\lambda}(\xi)],d\Phi_{\lambda}(0)[\Gamma_{\lambda}(\eta)]\big],\quad\forall\xi,\eta\in W^{1,2}_{V_{0}\times\{0\}}([0,1];\mathbb{R}^{n}).

Let

m^{-}({\bf L}_{\lambda},0)
and
m^{0}({\bf L}_{\lambda},0)
be the Morse index and nullity of
{\bf L}_{\lambda}
at
0
,
respectively. Then

m^{-}({\bf L}_{\lambda},0)=m^{-}(\mathcal{L}_{S_{0},\lambda},\gamma_{\lambda})\quad\hbox{and}\quad m^{0}({\bf L}_{\lambda},0)=m^{0}(\mathcal{L}_{S_{0},\lambda},\gamma_{\lambda})

(3.65)

because

d\Phi_{\lambda}(0)\circ\Gamma_{\lambda}\big(C^{1}_{V_{0}\times\{0\}}([0,1];\mathbb{R}^{n})\big)
is equal to

C^{1}_{S_{0}\times\{\gamma_{\lambda}(\lambda)\}}(\gamma_{\lambda}^{\ast}TM):=\left\{X\in C^{1}(\gamma_{\lambda}^{\ast}TM)\,\big|\,X(0)\in T_{\gamma_{\lambda}(0)}S_{0}=T_{\gamma(0)}S_{0},\,X(\lambda)=0\right\}.

Claim 3.15
.

For
\mu\in(0,\tau]
,
\mu
is a bifurcation instant for
(S_{0},\gamma)

if and only if
(\mu,0)\in(0,\tau]\times C^{1}_{V_{0}\times\{0\}}([0,1];B^{n}_{\iota}(0))

is a bifurcation point of the problem

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\big(\partial_{v}\hat{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))\big)-\partial_{x}\hat{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))=0,

(3.66)

\displaystyle\left.\begin{array}[]{ll}x\in C^{2}([0,1];B^{n}_{\iota}(0)),\;(x(0),x(1))\in V_{0}\times\{0\}\quad\hbox{and}\\
\partial_{v}\hat{L}_{\lambda}(0,x(0),\dot{x}(0))[v_{0}]=0\;\forall v_{0}\in V_{0}.\end{array}\right\}

with respect to the trivial branch
\{(\lambda,0)\,|\,\lambda\in(0,\tau]\}

in
(0,\tau]\times C^{1}_{V_{0}\times\{0\}}([0,1];B^{n}_{\iota}(0))
.

Proof.

By Definition
1.8
a real
\mu\in(0,\tau]
is a bifurcation instant for
(S_{0},\gamma)

if and only if there exists a sequence
(\lambda_{k})\subset(0,\tau]
converging to
\mu

and a sequence of Euler-Lagrange curves of
L
emanating perpendicularly from
S_{0}
,
\gamma^{k}:[0,\lambda_{k}]\to M
, such that
(
1.19
) and (
1.20
) are satisfied, i.e.,

\gamma^{k}(\lambda_{k})=\gamma(\lambda_{k})\;\hbox{for all $k\in{\mathbb{N}}$},\quad 0<\|\gamma^{k}-\gamma|_{[0,\lambda_{k}]}\|_{C^{1}([0,\lambda_{k}],\mathbb{R}^{N})}\to 0\;\hbox{ as $k\to\infty$}.

Since
\lambda_{k}\to\mu
, from the latter we deduce that for each
k
large enough
each
\gamma^{k}
sits in the image of the map

\Phi_{\lambda_{k}}:C^{1}_{V_{0}\times\{0\}}([0,\lambda_{k}];B^{n}_{\iota}(0))\to C^{1}_{S_{0}\times\{\gamma(\lambda_{k})\}}([0,\lambda_{k}];M)

and therefore there exists a unique

{\bf u}^{k}\in C^{1}_{V_{0}\times\{0\}}([0,\lambda_{k}];B^{n}_{\iota}(0))

such that
\Phi_{\lambda_{k}}({\bf u}^{k})=\gamma^{k}\in C^{1}_{S_{0}\times\{\gamma(\lambda_{k})\}}([0,\lambda_{k}];M)
.
Let
{\bf v}^{k}:=(\Gamma_{\lambda_{k}})^{-1}({\bf u}^{k})\in C^{1}_{V_{0}\times\{0\}}([0,1];B^{n}_{\iota}(0))
.
It follows from
\Phi_{\lambda_{k}}(0)=\gamma|_{[0,\lambda_{k}]}
that

0<\|{\bf u}^{k}\|_{C^{1}([0,\lambda_{k}],\mathbb{R}^{n})}\to 0
and so

0<\|{\bf v}^{k}\|_{C^{1}([0,1],\mathbb{R}^{n})}\to 0

as
k\to\infty
. Moreover,
\Phi_{\lambda_{k}}\circ\Gamma_{\lambda_{k}}({\bf v}^{k})=\gamma|_{[0,\lambda_{k}]}
implies

d{\bf L}_{\lambda_{k}}({\bf v}^{k})=d\mathcal{L}_{S_{0},\lambda_{k}}\big(\Phi_{\lambda_{k}}\circ\Gamma_{\lambda_{k}}({\bf v}^{k})\big)\circ d(\Phi_{\lambda_{k}}\circ\Gamma_{\lambda_{k}})({\bf v}^{k})=0.

These affirm the necessary.

Carefully checking the above arguments it is easily seen that the sufficiency also holds.
∎

Suppose that
\mu\in(0,\tau]
is a bifurcation instant for
(S_{0},\gamma)
.
Then it follows from Claim
3.15
and Theorem
1.4
(or Theorem
3.5
)
that
m^{0}({\bf L}_{\mu},0)>0
and therefore

m^{0}(\mathcal{L}_{S_{0},\mu},\gamma_{\mu})>0
by (
3.65
). That is,

\mu
is a
S_{0}
-focal point along
\gamma
. (ii) is proved.

Finally, let us prove (iii). Suppose that
\mu\in(0,\tau)
is a
S_{0}
-focal point along
\gamma
.
By (i) or Theorem
3.14
there only exist finitely many numbers in
(0,\tau)
,

0<\mu_{1}<\cdots<\mu_{m}<\tau
, such that
m^{0}(\mathcal{L}_{S_{0},\mu_{i}},\gamma_{\mu_{i}})>0
,

i=1,\cdots,m
. Therefore

\mu\in\{\mu_{1},\cdots,\mu_{m}\}
.
Then by Theorem
3.14
and (
3.65
) we obtain that

\displaystyle m^{0}({\bf L}_{\mu_{i}},0)\neq 0,\;i=1,\cdots,m\quad\hbox{and}\quad m^{0}({\bf L}_{\lambda},0)=0\quad\hbox{for}\quad\lambda\in(0,\tau)\setminus\{\mu_{1},\cdots,\mu_{m}\},

\displaystyle m^{-}({\bf L}_{\lambda},0)=\sum_{0<s<\lambda}m^{0}({\bf L}_{s},0),\quad\forall\lambda\in(0,\tau].

(3.70)

Let
\rho
be the distance from
\mu
to the set
\{0,\mu_{1},\cdots,\mu_{m},\tau\}\setminus\{\mu\}
.
Then for any
0<\epsilon<\rho
it holds that
m^{-}({\bf L}_{\mu-\epsilon},0)\neq m^{-}({\bf L}_{\mu+\epsilon},0)

and that
m^{0}({\bf L}_{\mu-\epsilon},0)=m^{0}({\bf L}_{\mu+\epsilon},0)=0
.
By Theorem
3.5
(II) and Claim
3.15

we deduce that
\mu
is a bifurcation instant for
(S_{0},\gamma)
.
This completes the proof of the first claim in (iii).

It remains to prove others in (iii).
Note that (
3.2
) gives rise to

m^{-}({\bf L}_{\lambda},0)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}m^{-}({\bf L}_{\mu},0)&\,{\rm for}\;\lambda<\mu\;\hbox{near $\mu$},\\
m^{-}({\bf L}_{\mu},0)+m^{0}({\bf L}_{\mu},0)&\,{\rm for}\;\lambda>\mu\;\hbox{near $\mu$}.\end{array}\right.

Then Theorem
3.7
may be applicable to
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda}={\bf L}_{\lambda}
and
V_{1}=\{0\}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
, and therefore
one of the following alternatives occurs:

(A)

The problem (
3.66
)–(
3.15
)
with
\lambda=\mu
has a sequence of distinct solutions,
{\bf v}^{k}\neq 0
,
k=1,2,\cdots
,
which converges to
0
in
C^{2}_{V_{0}\times\{0\}}([0,1];B^{n}_{\iota}(0))
.

(B)

There exists a real
0<\sigma<\min\{\mu,\tau-\mu\}
such that for every
\lambda\in[\mu-\sigma,\mu+\sigma]\setminus\{\mu\}

the problem (
3.66
)–(
3.15
) with parameter value
\lambda

has a solution
{\bf v}^{\lambda}\neq 0
to satisfy
\|{\bf v}^{\lambda}\|_{C^{2}}\to 0
as
\lambda\to\mu
.

(C)

For a given neighborhood
\mathfrak{W}
of
0\in C^{1}_{V_{0}\times\{0\}}([0,1];B^{n}_{\iota}(0))
,
there exists a real
0<\sigma<\min\{\mu,\tau-\mu\}
such that for any
\lambda\in\Lambda^{0}\setminus\{\mu\}
, where
\Lambda^{0}=[\mu-\sigma,\mu]

or
[\mu,\mu+\sigma]
, the problem
(
3.66
)–(
3.15
) with parameter value
\lambda

has at least two distinct solutions in
\mathfrak{W}
,
{\bf v}_{1}^{\lambda}\neq 0
and
{\bf v}_{2}^{\lambda}\neq 0
,
which can also be required to satisfy
{\bf L}_{\lambda}({\bf v}_{1}^{\lambda})\neq{\bf L}_{\lambda}({\bf v}_{2}^{\lambda})

provided that
m^{0}({\bf L}_{\mu},0)>1
and the problem
(
3.13
)–(
3.1.1
) with parameter value
\lambda

has only finitely many solutions in
\mathfrak{W}
.

Let us prove that the cases (A) and (B) lead to (iii-1) and (iii-2) in Theorem
1.9
, respectively.

In the case of (A) above
,
C^{2}
paths

\alpha_{k}:[0,\mu]\to M,\;t\mapsto(\Phi_{\mu}\circ\Gamma_{\mu})({\bf v}^{k})(t)=\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}\big(t,{\bf u}(t)+{\bf v}^{k}(t/\mu)\big),\quad k=1,2,\cdots,

are a sequence distinct
C^{2}
Euler-Lagrange curves of
L
emanating perpendicularly from
S_{0}
and ending at
\gamma(\mu)
,
and each of them is not equal to
\gamma|_{[0,\mu]}
.
Moreover, since
\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}:[0,\tau]\times B^{n}_{2\iota}(0)\to M

and
{\bf u}:[0,\tau]\to B^{n}_{\iota}(0)
are
C^{5}
and
C^{3}
, respectively,
we have a continuous map

C^{2}([0,\mu];[0,\tau]\times B^{n}_{2\iota}(0))\to C^{2}([0,\mu];M),\;w\mapsto\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}\circ w

by Exercise 10 on the page 64 of
[
10
]
, and a
C^{\infty}
map

\Theta:C^{2}([0,1];B^{n}_{\iota}(0))\to C^{2}([0,\mu];[0,\tau]\times B^{n}_{2\iota}(0))

given by
\Theta(v)(t)=(t,{\bf u}(t)+v(t/\mu))
. Hence the composition map

C^{2}([0,1];B^{n}_{\iota}(0))\to C^{2}([0,\mu];M),\;v\mapsto\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}\circ\Theta(v)=(\Phi_{\mu}\circ\Gamma_{\mu})(v)

is continuous, and therefore
\alpha_{k}=(\Phi_{\mu}\circ\Gamma_{\mu})({\bf v}^{k})\to\Phi_{\mu}(0)=\gamma|_{[0,\mu]}
in
C^{2}([0,\mu],\mathbb{R}^{N})
as
k\to\infty
.

In the case of (B) above
, for each
\lambda\in[\mu-\sigma,\mu+\sigma]\setminus\{\mu\}
,

\alpha^{\lambda}:[0,\lambda]\to M,\;t\mapsto(\Phi_{\lambda}\circ\Gamma_{\lambda})({\bf v}^{\lambda})(t)=\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}\big(t,{\bf u}(t)+{\bf v}^{\lambda}(t/\lambda)\big)

is a
C^{2}
Euler-Lagrange curve of
L
emanating perpendicularly from
S_{0}
and ending at
\gamma(\lambda)
,
and not equal to
\gamma|_{[0,\lambda]}
.
We cannot prove the desired claim as above. But noting that we have assumed
M\subset\mathbb{R}^{N}
,

\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}
can be viewed as a
C^{5}
map from
[0,\tau]\times B^{n}_{2\iota}(0)
to
\mathbb{R}^{N}
.
A straight computation leads to

\displaystyle(\alpha^{\lambda})^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D_{1}\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}\big(t,{\bf u}(t)+{\bf v}^{\lambda}(t/\lambda)\big)+D_{2}\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}\big(t,{\bf u}(t)+{\bf v}^{\lambda}(t/\lambda)\big)[{\bf u}^{\prime}(t)+\frac{1}{\lambda}({\bf v}^{\lambda})^{\prime}(t/\lambda)],

\displaystyle(\alpha^{\lambda})^{\prime\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D_{1}D_{1}\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}\big(t,{\bf u}(t)+{\bf v}^{\lambda}(t/\lambda)\big)+D_{2}D_{1}\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}\big(t,{\bf u}(t)+{\bf v}^{\lambda}(t/\lambda)\big)[{\bf u}^{\prime}(t)+\frac{1}{\lambda}({\bf v}^{\lambda})^{\prime}(t/\lambda)]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle D_{2}D_{1}\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}\big(t,{\bf u}(t)+{\bf v}^{\lambda}(t/\lambda)\big)[{\bf u}^{\prime}(t)+\frac{1}{\lambda}({\bf v}^{\lambda})^{\prime}(t/\lambda)]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle D_{2}\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}\big(t,{\bf u}(t)+{\bf v}^{\lambda}(t/\lambda)\big)[{\bf u}^{\prime\prime}(t)+\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}({\bf v}^{\lambda})^{\prime\prime}(t/\lambda)]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle D_{2}D_{2}\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}\big(t,{\bf u}(t)+{\bf v}^{\lambda}(t/\lambda)\big)[{\bf u}^{\prime}(t)+\frac{1}{\lambda}({\bf v}^{\lambda})^{\prime}(t/\lambda),{\bf u}^{\prime}(t)+\frac{1}{\lambda}({\bf v}^{\lambda})^{\prime}(t/\lambda)],

where we denote by
D_{1}
and
D_{2}
the partial derivatives of
\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(t,x)

with respect to the arguments
t
and
x
, respectively, and in particular, the final term is equal to

\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial s_{1}\partial s_{2}}\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}\Big(t,{\bf u}(t)+{\bf v}^{\lambda}(t/\lambda)+(s_{1}+s_{2})({\bf u}^{\prime}(t)+\frac{1}{\lambda}({\bf v}^{\lambda})^{\prime}(t/\lambda))\Big)\Big|_{s_{1}=s_{2}=0}.

Since
(\mu+\sigma)^{i}\leq\lambda^{i}\leq(\mu-\sigma)^{i}
,
i=1,2
, and
\|v^{\lambda}\|_{C^{2}}\to 0
as
\lambda\to\mu
,
it follows from the above expressions that
\|\alpha_{\lambda}-\gamma|_{[0,\lambda]}\|_{C^{2}([0,\lambda],\mathbb{R}^{N})}\to 0
as
\lambda\to\mu
.

(As pointed out below (
1.15
) the above Euler-Lagrange curves of
L
,
\alpha_{k}
,
\alpha^{\lambda}
and the following

\beta^{i}_{\lambda}
are actually
C^{3}
.)

Suppose that (iii-1) and (iii-2) in Theorem
1.9
do not hold
.
Then the above proofs show that the cases (A) and (B) do not occur.
That is, the case (C) must hold. Let us prove that it implies (iii-3) in Theorem
1.9
.
By the proof of the case (B) above we have positive numbers
\delta

and
\sigma^{\prime}<\min\{\mu,\tau-\mu\}
such that

\mathfrak{W}_{0}:=\big\{{\bf v}\in C^{1}_{V_{0}\times\{0\}}([0,1];B^{n}_{\iota}(0))\,\big|\,\|{\bf v}\|_{C^{1}}\leq\delta\big\}\subset\mathfrak{W}

and that
C^{1}
-paths

\alpha_{\lambda,\bf v}:[0,\lambda]\to M,\;t\mapsto\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}\left(t,{\bf u}(t)+{\bf v}(t/\lambda)\right)

associated with
(\lambda,{\bf v})\in[\mu-\sigma^{\prime},\mu+\sigma]\times\{{\bf v}\in C^{1}_{V_{0}\times\{0\}}([0,1];B^{n}_{\iota}(0))\,|\,\|{\bf v}\|_{C^{1}}\leq\delta\}
satisfy

\|\alpha_{\lambda,\bf v}-\gamma|_{[0,\lambda]}\|_{C^{1}}<\epsilon.

By (C) there exists a positive number
\sigma_{0}\leq\min\{\sigma^{\prime},\sigma\}
such that
the corresponding conclusions in (C) also hold true after

\mathfrak{W}
and
\sigma
are replaced by
\mathfrak{W}_{0}
and
\sigma_{0}
, respectively.
For
\Lambda^{*}:=\Lambda^{0}\cap[\mu-\sigma_{0},\mu+\sigma_{0}]
and
\lambda\in\Lambda^{\ast}\setminus\{\mu\}
,
let
{\bf v}_{1}^{\lambda}\in\mathfrak{W}_{0}\setminus\{0\}

and
{\bf v}_{2}^{\lambda}\in\mathfrak{W}_{0}\setminus\{0\}
be two distinct solutions of
the problem (
3.66
)–(
3.15
) with parameter value
\lambda
.
Then

\beta^{i}_{\lambda}:[0,\lambda]\to M,\;t\mapsto(\Phi_{\lambda}\circ\Gamma_{\lambda})({\bf v}_{i}^{\lambda})(t)=\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}\big(t,{\bf u}(t)+{\bf v}_{i}^{\lambda}(t/\lambda)\big),\quad i=1,2,

are two distinct
C^{2}
Euler-Lagrange curves of
L
emanating perpendicularly from
S_{0}
and ending at
\gamma(\lambda)
,
and not equal to
\gamma|_{[0,\lambda]}
. Moreover both satisfy

\|\beta_{i}^{\lambda}-\gamma|_{[0,\lambda]}\|_{C^{1}}<\epsilon
,
i=1,2
.

Suppose further that
m^{0}(\mathcal{L}_{S_{0},\mu},\gamma_{\mu})=m^{0}({\bf L}_{\mu},0)>1
.
For
\lambda\in\Lambda^{\ast}\setminus\{\mu\}
, if there only exist
finitely many distinct
C^{2}
Euler-Lagrange curves of
L
emanating perpendicularly from
S_{0}
and ending at
\gamma(\lambda)
,

\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{m}
, such that
\|\alpha_{i}-\gamma|_{[0,\lambda]}\|_{C^{1}}<\epsilon
,
i=1,\cdots,m
.
then the above arguments imply that
the problem (
3.66
)–(
3.15
) with parameter value
\lambda

has only finitely many solutions in
\mathfrak{W}_{0}
. In this case the above

{\bf v}_{1}^{\lambda}
and
{\bf v}_{2}^{\lambda}
can be chosen to satisfy

{\bf L}_{\lambda}({\bf v}_{1}^{\lambda})\neq{\bf L}_{\lambda}({\bf v}_{2}^{\lambda})
, which implies (
1.21
).
∎

Proof of Theorem
3.14
.

Follow the first paragraph in the proof of Theorem
1.9
.
We have a
C^{2}
chart

\displaystyle\Psi_{\lambda}:C^{1}_{V_{0}\times\{{\bf u}(\lambda)\}}([0,\lambda];B^{n}_{2\iota}(0))\to C^{1}_{S_{0}\times\{\gamma(\lambda)\}}([0,\lambda];M)

given by
\Psi_{\lambda}(\xi)(t)=\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(t,\xi(t))\;\forall t\in[0,\lambda]
for each
\lambda\in(0,\tau]
.
Then
\Psi_{\lambda}({\bf u}_{\lambda}(t))=\gamma_{\lambda}
, where
{\bf u}_{\lambda}:={\bf u}|_{[0,\lambda]}

and
\gamma_{\lambda}=\gamma|_{[0,\lambda]}
.
Define
\tilde{L}:[0,\tau]\times B^{n}_{2\iota}(0)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
by

\tilde{L}(t,q,v)=\tilde{L}(t,q,v)=L\left(t,\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(t,q),D_{t}\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(t,q)+D_{q}\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(t,q)[v]\right).

By Assumption
1.7
,
\tilde{L}
is
C^{3}

and
\tilde{L}(t,q,v)
is strictly convex in
v
for each
(t,q)\in[0,\tau]\times B^{n}_{2\iota}(0)
.
Therefore for each
\lambda\in(0,\tau]
the functional

C^{1}_{V_{0}\times\{{\bf u}(\lambda)\}}([0,\lambda];B^{n}_{2\iota}(0))\ni x\mapsto\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{V_{0},\lambda}(x)=\int^{\lambda}_{0}\tilde{L}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))dt\in{\mathbb{R}}

is
C^{2}
, and satisfies
d\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{V_{0},\lambda}({\bf u}_{\lambda})=0
and

\tilde{\cal E}_{V_{0},\lambda}(\xi)={\cal L}_{S_{0},\lambda}\left(\Psi_{\overline{\gamma}}(\xi)\right)\quad\hbox{for all}\;\xi\in C^{1}_{V_{0}\times\{{\bf u}(\lambda)\}}([0,\lambda];B^{n}_{2\iota}(0)).

With the same reasoning as for (
3.65
) these yield

m^{-}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{V_{0},\lambda},{\bf u}_{\lambda})=m^{-}(\mathcal{L}_{S_{0},\lambda},\gamma_{\lambda})\quad\hbox{and}\quad m^{0}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{V_{0},\lambda},{\bf u}_{\lambda})=m^{0}(\mathcal{L}_{S_{0},\lambda},\gamma_{\lambda}).

(3.71)

Therefore
from now on we may assume that
M={\mathbb{R}}^{n}
and
S_{0}
is a linear subspace in
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.

Then for
0<\lambda\leq\tau
and
y,z\in C^{1}_{S_{0}\times\{0\}}([0,\lambda];{\mathbb{R}}^{n})
,

D^{2}\mathcal{L}_{S_{0},\lambda}(\gamma_{\lambda})[y,z]=\int^{\lambda}_{0}\left[(\textsf{P}\dot{y}+\textsf{Q}y)\cdot\dot{z}+\textsf{ Q}^{\top}\dot{y}\cdot z+\textsf{R}y\cdot z\right]dt,

(3.72)

where
\textsf{P}(t)=\partial_{vv}L\left(t,\gamma(t),\dot{\gamma}(t)\right)
,

\textsf{Q}(t)=\partial_{xv}L\left(t,\gamma(t),\dot{\gamma}(t)\right)
and

\textsf{R}(t)=\partial_{xx}L\left(t,\gamma(t),\dot{\gamma}(t)\right)
.

Let
v=v(t,x,\xi)
be the solution of
\xi=\partial_{v}L(t,x,v)
. Define

H(t,x,\xi)=\langle v(t,x,\xi),\xi\rangle_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}-L(t,x,v(t,x,\xi)).

Writing
x(t)=\gamma(t)
and
\xi(t)=\partial_{v}L(t,x(t),v(t))
, we get that
(
1.15
) is equivalent to

\left.\begin{array}[]{ll}&\frac{d}{dt}x(t)=\partial_{\xi}H(t,x(t),\xi(t)),\\
&\frac{d}{dt}\xi(t)=-\partial_{x}H(t,x(t),\xi(t))\end{array}\right\}

(3.73)

with boundary condition

\left.\begin{array}[]{ll}&(x(0),x(\tau))\in S_{0}\times\{q\}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\\
&(\xi(0),-\xi(\tau))\in S_{0}^{\bot}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\end{array}\right\}

(3.74)

since
(T_{(x(0),x(\tau))}(S_{0}\times\{q\})^{\bot}=(S_{0}\times\{0\})^{\bot}=S_{0}^{\bot}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.
Note that
T^{\ast}{\mathbb{R}}^{n}={\mathbb{R}}^{n}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
. The natural projection
\pi:T^{\ast}M\to M

becomes the projection from
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
onto the first factor
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.
The co-normal bundle of
S_{0}
,
i.e.,
N^{\ast}S_{0}=\{(x,\xi)\in T^{\ast}M\,|\,x\in S_{0},\langle\xi,v\rangle=0\;\forall v\in T_{x}S_{0}\}
,
becomes
S_{0}\times S_{0}^{\bot}
and therefore its tangent space
U=S_{0}\times S_{0}^{\bot}
. Moreover
the vertical space
V
is equal to
\{0\}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.
By
[
6
, Proposition 4.5]
with
\rho=U\times V
we have

m^{-}(D^{2}\mathcal{L}_{S_{0},\lambda}(\gamma_{\lambda}))=\sum_{0<s<\lambda}\dim(U\cap\Phi(0,s)^{-1}(V)),

(3.75)

where
\Phi(0,t)
, according to (1.19) and (1.20) in
[
6
]
, is the fundamental matrix solution of

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\dot{x}(t)\\
\dot{y}(t)\\
\end{array}\right)=A(0,t)\left(\begin{array}[]{c}{x}(t)\\
{y}(t)\\
\end{array}\right)

(3.76)

with

\displaystyle A(0,t)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}D^{2}_{x\xi}H(t,x(t),\xi(t))&D^{2}_{\xi}H(t,x(t),\xi(t))\\
-D^{2}_{xx}H(t,x(t),\xi(t))&-D^{2}_{\xi x}H(t,x(t),\xi(t))\\
\end{array}\right)

with
\xi(t)=\partial_{v}L(t,\gamma(t),\dot{\gamma}(t))
. Note that
[
6
, (1.13)-(1.14)]
with
\mu=0
corresponds to
the Jacobi equation of the functional
\mathcal{L}_{S_{0},\tau}
, namely, the following linearized problem of (
1.15
)

\left.\begin{array}[]{ll}&\frac{d}{dt}\Big(\textsf{Q}(t)\cdot{x}(t)+\textsf{P}(t)\cdot\dot{x}(t)\Big)=\textsf{R}(t)\cdot x(t)+\textsf{Q}^{\top}(t)\cdot\dot{x}(t),\\
&x(0)\in S_{0},\;x(\tau)=0\quad\hbox{and}\quad\textsf{Q}(0)\cdot{x}(0)+\textsf{P}(0)\cdot\dot{x}(0)\in S_{0}^{\bot}\end{array}\right\}

(3.78)

whose solution space is equal to the kernel of
D^{2}\mathcal{L}_{S_{0},\tau}(\gamma)
.
It was claimed in
[
6
, page 179]
that (
3.78
) is equivalent to
(
3.76
) plus
with boundary condition

\left.\begin{array}[]{ll}&(x(0),x(\tau))\in S_{0}\times\{0\}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\\
&y(0)\in S_{0}^{\bot}.\end{array}\right\}

(3.79)

According to the deduction from
[
6
, (1.13)-(1.14)]
with
\mu=0
to
[
6
, (1.19)-(1.21)]
with
\mu=0
,
(
3.76
) was obtained by putting

y(t):=\textsf{Q}(t)\cdot{x}(t)+\textsf{P}(t)\cdot\dot{x}(t)
in (
3.78
).
Hence (
3.76
) is exactly

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\dot{x}(t)\\
\dot{y}(t)\\
\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}-[\textsf{P}(t)]^{-1}\textsf{Q}(t)&[\textsf{P}(t)]^{-1}\\
\textsf{R}(t)-[\textsf{Q}(t)]^{\top}[\textsf{P}(t)]^{-1}\textsf{Q}(t)&[\textsf{Q}(t)]^{\top}[\textsf{P}(t)]^{-1}\\
\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{c}{x}(t)\\
{y}(t)\\
\end{array}\right),

(3.80)

that is,

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}-[\textsf{P}(t)]^{-1}\textsf{Q}(t)&[\textsf{P}(t)]^{-1}\\
\textsf{R}(t)-[\textsf{Q}(t)]^{\top}[\textsf{P}(t)]^{-1}\textsf{Q}(t)&[\textsf{Q}(t)]^{\top}[\textsf{P}(t)]^{-1}\\
\end{array}\right)=A(0,t)

with
\xi(t)=\partial_{v}L(t,\gamma(t),\dot{\gamma}(t))
.
(Hence
\Phi(0,t)=d\phi^{t}(\gamma(0),\partial_{v}L(t,\gamma(0),\dot{\gamma}(0))):{\mathbb{R}}^{2n}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2n}
,
where
\phi^{t}
is the flow of the Hamiltonian system (
3.73
); see

[
6
, page 192]
.)

Note that the map sending
(\bar{x},\bar{y})\in U\cap\Phi(0,\tau)^{-1}(V)
to
x
, where

(x(t),y(t))=\Phi(0,t)(\bar{x},\bar{y})
with
0\leq t\leq\tau
, is a linear isomorphism
between
U\cap\Phi(0,\tau)^{-1}(V)
and the space of solutions of (
3.78
). We obtain

\dim(U\cap\Phi(0,\tau)^{-1}(V))=m^{0}(D^{2}\mathcal{L}_{S_{0},\lambda}(\gamma_{\tau}))

and so
\dim(U\cap\Phi(0,s)^{-1}(V))=m^{0}(D^{2}\mathcal{L}_{S_{0},s}(\gamma_{s}))
since

\tau
may be replaced by any
0<\lambda\leq\tau
. The desired conclusion
follows from these and (
3.75
).
∎

4
Proofs of Theorems
1.13
,
1.14
,
1.15

The proofs are completely similar to those of Section
3
. We only outline main procedures.

4.1
Reduction to Euclidean spaces

As in Section
3.1.1
, we have a positive number
\iota
such that
3\iota
is less than the injectivity
radius of
g
at each point on
\gamma_{\mu}([0,\tau])
, and a path

\overline{\gamma}\in C^{1}_{\mathbb{I}_{g}}([0,\tau];M)\cap C^{7}([0,\tau];M)
such that (
3.2
) is satisfied.
Then the injectivity radius of
g
at each point on

\overline{\gamma}([0,\tau])
is at least
2\iota
.
Then we assume that (
3.3
) is satisfied.
(For cases of Theorems
1.13
,
1.15
,
it is naturally satisfied after shrinking
\Lambda
toward
\mu
.)

As in
[
17
, §3]
, starting with a unit orthogonal
frame at
T_{\gamma_{\mu}(0)}M
and using the parallel
transport along
\overline{\gamma}
with respect to the Levi-Civita
connection of the Riemannian metric
g
we get a unit orthogonal
parallel
C^{5}
frame field
[0,\tau]\to\overline{\gamma}^{\ast}TM,\;t\mapsto(e_{1}(t),\cdots,e_{n}(t))
.
Note that there exists a unique orthogonal matrix

E_{\overline{\gamma}}
such that

(e_{1}(\tau),\cdots,e_{n}(\tau))=(\mathbb{I}_{g\ast}e_{1}(0),\cdots,\mathbb{I}_{g\ast}e_{n}(0))E_{\overline{\gamma}}
.
Let
B^{n}_{2\iota}(0):=\{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\,|\,|x|<2\iota\}
and
\exp
denote
the exponential map of
g
. Then

\displaystyle\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}:[0,\tau]\times B^{n}_{2\iota}(0)\to M,\;(t,x)\mapsto\exp_{\overline{\gamma}(t)}\Big(\sum^{n}_{i=1}x_{i}e_{i}(t)\Big)

(4.1)

is a
C^{5}
map and satisfies

\displaystyle\phi_{{\overline{\gamma}}}(\tau,x)=\mathbb{I}_{g}\left(\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(0,(E_{\overline{\gamma}}x^{\top})^{\top})\right)\quad\hbox{and}\quad d\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(\tau,x)[(1,v)]=d\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(0,(E_{\overline{\gamma}}x^{\top})^{\top})[(1,(E_{\overline{\gamma}}v^{\top})^{\top})]

for any
(t,x,v)\in[0,\tau]\times B^{n}_{2\iota}(0)\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
.
(Note that the tangent map
d\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}:T([0,\tau]\times B^{n}_{2\iota}(0))\to TM
is
C^{4}
.)
Consider the Hilbert subspace

W^{1,2}_{E_{\overline{\gamma}}}([0,\tau];{\mathbb{R}}^{n}):=\{u\in W^{1,2}([0,\tau];\mathbb{R}^{n})\,|\,u(\tau)=E_{\overline{\gamma}}u(0)\}

(4.2)

of
W^{1,2}([0,\tau];\mathbb{R}^{2n})
equipped with
W^{1,2}
-inner product (
2.2
),
and Banach spaces

\displaystyle C^{i}_{E_{\overline{\gamma}}}([0,\tau];{\mathbb{R}}^{n})=\{u\in C^{i}([0,\tau];{\mathbb{R}}^{n})\,|\,u(\tau)=E_{\overline{\gamma}}u(0)\}

(4.3)

with the induced norm
\|\cdot\|_{C^{i}}
from
C^{i}([0,\tau],\mathbb{R}^{n})
for
i\in{\mathbb{N}}\cup\{0\}
.
By
[
28
, Theorem 4.3]
, we get a
C^{2}
coordinate chart around
\overline{\gamma}
on
the
C^{4}
Banach manifold
C^{1}_{\mathbb{I}_{g}}([0,\tau];M)
,

\displaystyle\Phi_{\overline{\gamma}}:C^{1}_{{E}_{\overline{\gamma}}}([0,\tau],B^{n}_{2\iota}(0))=\{\xi\in C^{1}_{{E}_{\overline{\gamma}}}([0,\tau],\mathbb{R}^{n})\,|\,\|\xi\|_{C^{0}}<2\iota\}\to C^{1}_{\mathbb{I}_{g}}([0,\tau];M)

(4.4)

given by
\Phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(\xi)(t)=\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(t,\xi(t))
, and

d\Phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(0):C^{1}_{{E}_{\overline{\gamma}}}([0,\tau],\mathbb{R}^{n})\to C^{1}_{\mathbb{I}_{g}}(\overline{\gamma}^{\ast}TM),\;\xi\mapsto\sum^{n}_{j=1}\xi_{j}e_{j}.

By (
3.2
) and (
3.3
), for each
\lambda\in\Lambda
there exists a unique map

{\bf u}_{\lambda}:[0,\tau]\to B^{n}_{\iota}(0)
such that

\gamma_{\lambda}(t)=\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(t,{\bf u}_{\lambda}(t))=\exp_{\overline{\gamma}(t)}\Big(\sum^{n}_{i=1}{\bf u}_{\lambda}^{i}e_{i}(t)\Big),\quad t\in[0,\tau].

As in the proofs of Lemma
3.1
,
3.2
we have:

Lemma 4.1
.

{\bf u}_{\lambda}\in C^{2}([0,\tau];B^{n}_{\iota}(0))
,

{\bf u}_{\mu}(0)=0={\bf u}_{\mu}(\tau)
(and so
{\bf u}_{\lambda}\in C^{1}_{{E}_{\overline{\gamma}}}([0,\tau],B^{n}_{\iota}(0))
) and

(\lambda,t)\mapsto{\bf u}_{\lambda}(t),\quad(\lambda,t)\mapsto\dot{\bf u}_{\lambda}(t)\quad\hbox{and}\quad(\lambda,t)\mapsto\ddot{\bf u}_{\lambda}(t)

are continuous as maps from
\Lambda\times[0,\tau]
to
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.

Let
\tilde{L}^{\ast}:\Lambda\times[0,\tau]\times B^{n}_{\iota}(0)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be given by
(
3.9
). It satisfies Proposition
3.3
.
Each functional

\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda}:C^{1}_{{E}_{\overline{\gamma}}}([0,\tau],B^{n}_{\iota}(0))\to{\mathbb{R}},\;x\mapsto\int^{1}_{0}\tilde{L}^{\ast}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))dt

(4.5)

is
C^{2}
, and satisfies

\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda}(x)=\mathscr{E}_{\lambda}\left(\Phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(x+{\bf u}_{\lambda})\right)\;\forall x\in C^{1}_{{E}_{\overline{\gamma}}}([0,\tau],B^{n}_{\iota}(0))\quad\hbox{and}\quad d\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda}(0)=0.

(4.6)

Hence for each
\lambda\in\Lambda
,

x\in C^{1}_{{E}_{\overline{\gamma}}}([0,\tau],B^{n}_{\iota}(0))
satisfies

d\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda}(x)=0
if and only if
\gamma:=\Phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(x+{\bf u}_{\lambda})
satisfies

d{\mathscr{E}}_{\lambda}(\gamma)=0
; and in this case
\gamma
and
x

have the same Morse indexes and nullities.
In particular, for each
\lambda\in\Lambda
, it holds that

m^{-}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda},0)=m^{-}(\mathscr{E}_{\lambda},\gamma_{\lambda})\quad\hbox{and}\quad m^{0}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda},0)=m^{0}(\mathscr{E}_{\lambda},\gamma_{\lambda}).

(4.7)

The critical points of
\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda}

correspond to the solutions of the following boundary problem:

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\big(\partial_{v}\tilde{L}^{\ast}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))\big)-\partial_{q}\tilde{L}^{\ast}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))=0,

(4.8)

\displaystyle\left.\begin{array}[]{ll}x\in C^{2}_{{E}_{\overline{\gamma}}}([0,\tau],B^{n}_{\iota}(0))\quad\hbox{and}\\
({E}_{\overline{\gamma}}^{\top})^{-1}\partial_{v}\tilde{L}^{\ast}_{\lambda}(0,x(0),\dot{x}(0))=\partial_{v}\tilde{L}^{\ast}_{\lambda}(\tau,x(\tau),\dot{x}(\tau))\end{array}\right\}

(
[
4
, Proposition 4.2]
). Corresponding to Theorems
3.5
,
3.6
,
3.7
,
we have the following three theorems, which also hold true provided that
\tilde{L}^{\ast}
satisfies (a) in Proposition
3.3
and the weaker
(b’) in Remark
3.4
as noted in Remark
3.4
.

Theorem 4.2
.

(I)

(
Necessary condition
):

Suppose that
(\mu,0)\in\Lambda\times C^{1}_{{E}_{\overline{\gamma}}}([0,\tau],B^{n}_{\iota}(0))
is a
bifurcation point along sequences of the problem (
4.8
)–(
4.1
)
with respect to the trivial branch
\{(\lambda,0)\,|\,\lambda\in\Lambda\}
in
\Lambda\times C^{1}_{{E}_{\overline{\gamma}}}([0,\tau],B^{n}_{\iota}(0))
.
Then
m^{0}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\mu},0)>0
.

(II)

(
Sufficient condition
):

Suppose that
\Lambda
is first countable and that there exist two sequences in
\Lambda
converging to
\mu
,
(\lambda_{k}^{-})
and

(\lambda_{k}^{+})
, such that one of the following conditions is satisfied:

(II.1)

For each
k\in\mathbb{N}
, either
0
is not an isolated critical point of
\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{+}_{k}}
,
or
0
is not an isolated critical point of
\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{-}_{k}}
,
or
0
is an isolated critical point of
\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{+}_{k}}
and
\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{-}_{k}}
and

C_{m}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{+}_{k}},0;{\bf K})
and
C_{m}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{-}_{k}},0;{\bf K})

are not isomorphic for some Abel group
{\bf K}
and some
m\in\mathbb{Z}
.

(II.2)

For each
k\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists
\lambda\in\{\lambda^{+}_{k},\lambda^{-}_{k}\}
such that

0
is an either non-isolated or homological visible critical point of

\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda}
, and

[m^{-}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{k}^{-}},0),m^{-}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{k}^{-}},0)+m^{0}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{k}^{-}},0)]\cap[m^{-}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},0),m^{-}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},0)+m^{0}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},0)]=\emptyset.

(II.3)

[m^{-}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{k}^{-}},0),m^{-}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{k}^{-}},0)+m^{0}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{k}^{-}},0)]\cap[m^{-}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},0),m^{-}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},0)+m^{0}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},0)]=\emptyset
,
and either
m^{0}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{k}^{-}},0)=0
or
m^{0}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},0)=0

for each
k\in\mathbb{N}
.

Then
(\mu,0)
is a bifurcation point of the problem (
4.8
)–(
4.1
)
in
\hat{\Lambda}\times C^{2}_{{E}_{\overline{\gamma}}}([0,\tau],B^{n}_{\iota}(0))
with respect to the branch
\{(\lambda,0)\,|\,\lambda\in\hat{\Lambda}\}

(and so
\{(\lambda,0)\,|\,\lambda\in\Lambda\}
), where

\hat{\Lambda}=\{\mu,\lambda^{+}_{k},\lambda^{-}_{k}\,|\,k\in\mathbb{N}\}
.

Theorem 4.3

(
Existence for bifurcations
)
.

Let
\Lambda
be connected. For
\lambda^{-},\lambda^{+}\in\Lambda

suppose that
one of the following conditions is satisfied:

(i)

Either
0
is not an isolated critical point of
\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{+}}
,
or
0
is not an isolated critical point of
\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{-}}
,
or
0
is an isolated critical point of
\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{+}}
and
\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{-}}
and

C_{m}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{+}},0;{\bf K})
and
C_{m}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{-}},0;{\bf K})
are not isomorphic for some Abel group
{\bf K}
and some
m\in\mathbb{Z}
.

(ii)

[m^{-}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{-}},0),m^{-}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{-}},0)+m^{0}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{-}},0)]\cap[m^{-}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{+}},0),m^{-}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{+}},0)+m^{0}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{+}},0)]=\emptyset
,
and there exists
\lambda\in\{\lambda^{+},\lambda^{-}\}
such that
0
is an either non-isolated or homological visible critical point of

\mathscr{E}^{\ast}_{\lambda}
.

(iii)

[m^{-}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{-}},0),m^{-}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{-}},0)+m^{0}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{-}},0)]\cap[m^{-}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{+}},0),m^{-}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{+}},0)+m^{0}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{+}},0)]=\emptyset
,
and either
m^{0}(\mathscr{E}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{+}},0)=0
or
m^{0}(\mathscr{E}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{-}},0)=0
.

Then for any path
\alpha:[0,1]\to\Lambda
connecting
\lambda^{+}
to
\lambda^{-}
there exists
a sequence
(t_{k})\subset[0,1]
converging to some
\bar{t}\in[0,1]
, and
a nonzero solution
x_{k}
of the problem (
4.8
)–(
4.1
)
with
\lambda=\alpha(t_{k})
for each
k\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\|x_{k}\|_{C^{2}([0,\tau];\mathbb{R}^{n})}\to 0

as
k\to\infty
.
Moreover,
\alpha(\bar{t})
is not equal to
\lambda^{+}
(resp.
\lambda^{-}
) if
m^{0}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{+}},0)=0
(resp.
m^{0}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{-}},0)=0
).

Theorem 4.4

(
Alternative bifurcations of Rabinowitz’s type
)
.

Let
\Lambda
be a real interval and
\mu\in{\rm Int}(\Lambda)
. Suppose that
m^{0}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\mu},0)>0
,
and that
m^{0}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda},0)=0
for each
\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\{\mu\}
near
\mu
, and

m^{-}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda},0)
take, respectively, values
m^{-}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\mu},0)
and

m^{-}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\mu},0)+m^{0}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\mu},0)

as
\lambda\in\Lambda
varies in two deleted half neighborhoods of
\mu
.
Then one of the following alternatives occurs:

(i)

The problem (
4.8
)–(
4.1
)
with
\lambda=\mu
has a sequence of solutions,
x_{k}\neq 0
,
k=1,2,\cdots
,
which converges to
0
in
C^{2}([0,\tau],{\mathbb{R}}^{n})
.

(ii)

For every
\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\{\mu\}
near
\mu
there is a solution
y_{\lambda}\neq 0
of
(
4.8
)–(
4.1
) with parameter value
\lambda
,
such that
y_{\lambda}
converges to zero in
C^{2}([0,\tau],{\mathbb{R}}^{n})
as
\lambda\to\mu
.

(iii)

For a given neighborhood
\mathfrak{W}
of
0\in C^{1}_{{E}_{\overline{\gamma}}}([0,\tau],B^{n}_{\iota}(0))
,
there is a one-sided neighborhood
\Lambda^{0}
of
\mu
such that
for any
\lambda\in\Lambda^{0}\setminus\{\mu\}
, the problem (
4.8
)–(
4.1
) with parameter value
\lambda

has at least two distinct solutions in
\mathfrak{W}
,
y_{\lambda}^{1}\neq 0
and
y_{\lambda}^{2}\neq 0
,
which can also be required to satisfy
\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda}(y^{1}_{\lambda})\neq\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda}(y^{2}_{\lambda})

provided that
m^{0}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\mu},0)>1
and the problem
(
4.8
)–(
4.1
) with parameter value
\lambda

has only finitely many solutions in
\mathfrak{W}
.

As in the proofs of Theorems
1.4
,
1.6
,
Theorems
1.13
,
1.15

are derived from Theorems
4.2
,
4.4
, respectively.

4.2
Proofs of Theorems
1.13
,
1.14
,
1.15

Let us write

\displaystyle{\bf H}_{E_{\overline{\gamma}}}:=W^{1,2}_{E_{\overline{\gamma}}}([0,\tau];{\mathbb{R}}^{n})\quad\hbox{and}\quad{\bf X}_{E_{\overline{\gamma}}}:=C^{1}_{E_{\overline{\gamma}}}([0,\tau];{\mathbb{R}}^{n}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}:=\big\{u\in W^{1,2}\big([0,\tau];B^{n}_{\iota/2}(0)\big)\,\big|\,u(\tau)=E_{\overline{\gamma}}u(0)\big\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}^{X}:=\mathcal{U}\cap{\bf X}_{E_{\overline{\gamma}}}=\big\{u\in C^{1}\big([0,\tau];B^{n}_{\iota/2}(0)\big)\,\big|\,u(\tau)=E_{\overline{\gamma}}u(0)\big\}

The latter two sets are open subsets in the spaces
{\bf H}_{E_{\overline{\gamma}}}
and
{\bf X}_{E_{\overline{\gamma}}}
, respectively.
Let the continuous function
\check{L}:\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau]\times B^{n}_{3\iota/4}(0)\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}}

be given by Lemma
3.8
.
Define a family of functionals
\check{\mathscr{E}}_{\lambda}:\mathcal{U}\to{\mathbb{R}}
given by

\check{\mathscr{E}}_{\lambda}(x)=\int^{\tau}_{0}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))dt,\quad\lambda\in\hat{\Lambda}.

(4.12)

Then (L1) in Lemma
3.8
implies

\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda}=\check{\mathscr{E}}_{\lambda}|_{\mathcal{U}^{X}}\quad\hbox{in}\quad\{x\in\mathcal{U}^{X}\,|\,\|x\|_{C^{1}}<\rho_{0}\}\subset\mathcal{U}^{X},

(4.13)

and hence

m^{\star}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda},0)=m^{\star}(\check{\mathscr{E}}_{\lambda}|_{\mathcal{U}^{X}},0)=m^{\star}(\check{\mathscr{E}}_{\lambda},0),\quad\star=-,0.

(4.14)

Since
{\bf H}_{E_{\overline{\gamma}}}
contains the subspace
\{u\in W^{1,2}([0,\tau];\mathbb{R}^{n})\,|\,u(\tau)=0=u(0)\}
, carefully checking the computation of
[
16
, (4.14)]
it is easily seen that
replacing
H_{V}
by
{\bf H}_{E_{\overline{\gamma}}}
we also obtain that
the gradient
\nabla\check{\mathscr{E}}_{\lambda}(x)
of
\check{\mathscr{E}}_{\lambda}
at
x\in\mathcal{U}

is still given by

\displaystyle\nabla\check{\mathscr{E}}_{\lambda}(x)(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{t}\int^{t}_{0}\left[e^{-2s}\int^{s}_{0}e^{r}f_{\lambda,x}(r)dr\right]ds+c_{1}(\lambda,x)e^{t}+c_{2}(\lambda,x)e^{-t}

(4.15)

\displaystyle\qquad+\int^{t}_{0}\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s))ds,

where
c_{1}(\lambda,x),c_{2}(\lambda,x)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
are suitable constant vectors and
f_{\lambda}(t)
is given by (
3.37
).

Proposition 4.5
.

Proposition
3.9
is still effective after making the following substitutions:

\bullet

The functionals
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda}
and

\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}
are changed into

\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\ast}_{\lambda}
and
\check{\mathscr{E}}_{\lambda}
, respectively.

\bullet

The spaces
{\bf H}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}
and
{\bf X}_{V_{0}\times V_{1}}
are changed into

{\bf H}_{E_{\overline{\gamma}}}
and
{\bf X}_{E_{\overline{\gamma}}}
, respectively.

\bullet

The boundary problem (
(ii)
) is changed into

\displaystyle\left.\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{d}{dt}\big(\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))\big)-\partial_{q}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))=0,\\
x(\tau)=E_{\overline{\gamma}}x(0).\end{array}\right\}

Then repeating proofs of Propositions
3.10
,
3.11
we can obtain:

Proposition 4.6
.

Both maps
\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathcal{U}^{X}\ni(\lambda,x)\mapsto\check{\mathscr{E}}_{\lambda}(x)\in{\mathbb{R}}
and

\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathcal{U}^{X}\ni(\lambda,x)\mapsto A_{\lambda}(x)\in{\bf X}_{E_{\overline{\gamma}}}
are continuous.

Proposition 4.7
.

For any given
\epsilon>0
there exists
\varepsilon>0
such that
if a critical point
x
of
\check{\mathscr{E}}_{\lambda}
satisfies

\|x\|_{1,2}<\varepsilon
then
\|x\|_{C^{2}}<\epsilon
. (
Note
:
\varepsilon
is independent of
\lambda\in\hat{\Lambda}
.)
Consequently, if
0\in\mathcal{U}^{X}
is an isolated critical point of
\check{\mathscr{E}}_{\lambda}|_{\mathscr{U}^{X}}

then
0\in\mathcal{U}
is also an isolated critical point of
\check{\mathscr{E}}_{\lambda}
.

Having these we can prove

\bullet

Theorem
4.2
(I) with
[
19
, Theorem 3.1]
(
[
22
, Theorem C.6]
)
as in the proof of Theorem
3.5
(I),

\bullet

Theorem
4.4
with

[
22
, Theorem C.7]
(
[
21
, Theorem 3.6]
)
as in the proof of Theorem
3.7
,

\bullet

a corresponding result of Theorem
3.13
, from which
Theorem
4.2
(II) and Theorem
4.3
may be derived,

\bullet

Theorem
1.14
as in the proof of Theorem
1.5
in Section
3.1.4
.

5
Proofs of Theorems
1.22
,
1.23
,
1.24

Following the paragraph above (
3.2
)
we take a path
\overline{\gamma}\in EC^{7}(S_{\tau};M)
such that

\displaystyle{\rm dist}_{g}(\gamma_{\mu}(t),\overline{\gamma}(t))<\iota\;\forall t\in\mathbb{R}.

(5.1)

We first assume:

\displaystyle d_{g}(\gamma_{\lambda}(t),\overline{\gamma}(t))<\iota,\quad\forall(\lambda,t)\in\Lambda\times\mathbb{R}.

(5.2)

(For cases of Theorems
1.22
,
1.24
,
by contradiction we may use nets to prove that (
5.2
) is satisfied after shrinking
\Lambda
toward
\mu
.)
Then (
5.1
) and (
5.2
) imply

\displaystyle d_{g}(\gamma_{\lambda}(t),\gamma_{\mu}([0,\tau]))\leq d_{g}(\gamma_{\lambda}(t),\gamma_{\mu}(t))<2\iota,\quad\forall(\lambda,t)\in\Lambda\times\mathbb{R}.

(5.3)

By Claim
A.1
we have
a unit orthogonal parallel
C^{5}
frame field

{\mathbb{R}}\to\overline{\gamma}^{\ast}TM,\;t\mapsto(e_{1}(t),\cdots,e_{n}(t))

to satisfy

\displaystyle(e_{1}(\tau\pm t),\cdots,e_{n}(\tau\pm t))=(e_{1}(t),\cdots,e_{n}(t))\quad\forall t\in{\mathbb{R}}.

(5.4)

Let
B^{n}_{2\iota}(0):=\{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\,|\,|x|<2\iota\}
and
\exp
denote the exponential map of
g
. Then

\displaystyle\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}:{\mathbb{R}}\times B^{n}_{2\iota}(0)\to M,\;(t,x)\mapsto\exp_{\overline{\gamma}(t)}\Big(\sum^{n}_{i=1}x_{i}e_{i}(t)\Big)

is a
C^{5}
map and satisfies

\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(\tau\pm t,x)=\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(t,x)\quad\hbox{and}\quad\frac{d}{dt}\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(\tau\pm t,x)=\pm(\phi_{\overline{\gamma}})^{\prime}_{1}(\tau\pm t,x)

(5.5)

for any
(t,x)\in{\mathbb{R}}\times B^{n}_{2\iota}(0)
. By
[
28
, Theorem 4.3]
, there exists
a
C^{2}
coordinate chart around
\overline{\gamma}
on the
C^{4}
Banach manifold

EC^{1}(S_{\tau};M)
,

\displaystyle\Phi_{\overline{\gamma}}:EC^{1}(S_{\tau};B^{n}_{2\iota}(0))=\{\xi\in C^{1}(S_{\tau};\mathbb{R}^{n})\,|\,\|\xi\|_{C^{0}}<2\iota\}\to EC^{1}(S_{\tau};M)

(5.6)

given by
\Phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(\xi)(t)=\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(t,\xi(t))
, and

d\Phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(0):EC^{1}(S_{\tau};\mathbb{R}^{n})\to T_{\overline{\gamma}}EC^{1}(S_{\tau};M),\;\xi\mapsto\sum^{n}_{j=1}\xi_{j}e_{j}.

Define

d_{\infty}(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})=\max_{t\in S_{\tau}}{\rm dist}_{g}(\gamma_{1}(t),\gamma_{2}(t))

for
\gamma_{i}\in EC^{1}(S_{\tau},M)
,
i=1,2
. Then

\displaystyle\Phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(C^{1}(S_{\tau};B^{n}_{2\iota}(0)))=\mathcal{U}(\overline{\gamma},2\iota):=\{\gamma\in C^{1}(S_{\tau},M)\,|\,d_{\infty}(\gamma,\overline{\gamma})<2\iota\},

(5.7)

Let
EC^{1}(S_{\tau};\mathbb{R}^{n})=\{\xi\in C^{1}({\mathbb{R}};\mathbb{R}^{n})\,|\,\xi(t+\tau)=\xi(t)\,\&\,\xi(-t)=\xi(t)\;\forall t\in{\mathbb{R}}\}
and

\displaystyle EC^{1}(S_{\tau};B^{n}_{2\iota}(0))=\{\xi\in EC^{1}(S_{\tau};\mathbb{R}^{n})\,|\,\|\xi\|_{C^{0}}<2\iota\}.

(5.8)

Since
\overline{\gamma}\in C^{7}(S_{\tau};M)\cap EC^{1}(S_{\tau};M)
, it follows from (
5.7
) and (
5.8
) that

\displaystyle\Phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(EC^{1}(S_{\tau};B^{n}_{2\iota}(0)))=EC^{1}(S_{\tau};M)\cap\mathcal{U}(\overline{\gamma},2\iota)=\{\gamma\in EC^{1}(S_{\tau},M)\,|\,d_{\infty}(\gamma,\overline{\gamma})<2\iota\}.

Hence we obtain the following:

Claim 5.1
.

The restriction of
\Phi_{\overline{\gamma}}
to
EC^{1}(S_{\tau};B^{n}_{2\iota}(0)

gives rise to a
C^{2}
coordinate chart around
\overline{\gamma}
on
EC^{1}(S_{\tau};M)
.
And
\Phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(EC^{1}(S_{\tau};B^{n}_{\iota}(0)))
contains all

\gamma_{\lambda}
by (
5.2
).

Since the injectivity radius of
g
at each point on
\overline{\gamma}(S_{\tau})
is at least
2\iota
,
by (
5.2
) there exists a unique map
{\bf u}_{\lambda}:{\mathbb{R}}\to B^{n}_{\iota}(0)
such that

\gamma_{\lambda}(t)=\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(t,{\bf u}_{\lambda}(t))=\exp_{\overline{\gamma}(t)}\Big(\sum^{n}_{i=1}{\bf u}_{\lambda}^{i}(t)e_{i}(t)\Big)\quad\forall t\in{\mathbb{R}}.

Note that
\overline{\gamma}
is even and
\tau
-periodic. From (
5.4
) we derive

\gamma_{\lambda}(\tau\pm t)=\exp_{\overline{\gamma}(\tau\pm t)}\Big(\sum^{n}_{i=1}{\bf u}_{\lambda}^{i}(\tau\pm t)e_{i}(\tau\pm t)\Big)=\exp_{\overline{\gamma}(t)}\Big(\sum^{n}_{i=1}{\bf u}_{\lambda}^{i}(\tau\pm t)e_{i}(t)\Big).

Hence
{\bf u}_{\lambda}\in EC^{1}(S_{\tau};B^{n}_{\iota}(0))\cap C^{2}(S_{\tau};B^{n}_{\iota}(0))
.
By Lemmas
3.1
,
3.2
we also see that

(\lambda,t)\mapsto{\bf u}_{\lambda}(t),\quad(\lambda,t)\mapsto\dot{\bf u}_{\lambda}(t)\quad\hbox{and}\quad(\lambda,t)\mapsto\ddot{\bf u}_{\lambda}(t)

are continuous maps from
\Lambda\times{\mathbb{R}}
to
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.

Define
L^{\ast}_{\lambda}:{\mathbb{R}}\times B^{n}_{2\iota}(0)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
by

L^{\ast}_{\lambda}(t,x,v)=L_{\lambda}\big(t,\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(t,x),(\phi_{\overline{\gamma}})^{\prime}_{1}(t,x)+(\phi_{\overline{\gamma}})^{\prime}_{2}(t,x)[v]\big).

(5.9)

Then it follows from (
5.5
) that

L^{\ast}_{\lambda}(\tau+t,x,v)=L^{\ast}_{\lambda}(t,x,v)

and

\displaystyle L^{\ast}_{\lambda}(\tau-t,x,-v)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle L_{\lambda}\left(\tau-t,\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(\tau-t,x),(\phi_{\overline{\gamma}})^{\prime}_{1}(\tau-t,x)+(\phi_{\overline{\gamma}})^{\prime}_{2}(\tau-t,x)[-v]\right)

(5.10)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle L_{\lambda}\left(\tau-t,\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(t,x),-(\phi_{\overline{\gamma}})^{\prime}_{1}(t,x)-(\phi_{\overline{\gamma}})^{\prime}_{2}(t,x)[v]\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle L_{\lambda}\left(t,\phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(t,x),(\phi_{\overline{\gamma}})^{\prime}_{1}(t,x)+(\phi_{\overline{\gamma}})^{\prime}_{2}(t,x)[v]\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle L^{\ast}_{\lambda}(t,x,v),\quad\forall(t,x,v)\in{\mathbb{R}}\times B^{n}_{2\iota}(0)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}.

As before we also define
\tilde{L}^{\ast}:\Lambda\times\mathbb{R}\times B^{n}_{\iota}(0)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
by

\tilde{L}^{\ast}(\lambda,t,q,v)=\tilde{L}^{\ast}_{\lambda}(t,q,v)={L}^{\ast}(\lambda,t,q+{\bf u}_{\lambda}(t),v+\dot{\bf u}_{\lambda}(t)).

(5.11)

It satisfies Proposition
3.3
after the interval
[0,\tau]
is replaced by
\mathbb{R}
.
Moreover, (
5.10
) leads to

\displaystyle\tilde{L}^{\ast}(\lambda,\tau-t,q,-v)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{L}^{\ast}(\lambda,\tau-t,q+{\bf u}_{\lambda}(\tau-t),-v+\dot{\bf u}_{\lambda}(\tau-t))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{L}^{\ast}(\lambda,\tau-t,q+{\bf u}_{\lambda}(t),-v-\dot{\bf u}_{\lambda}(t))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{L}^{\ast}(\lambda,t,q+{\bf u}_{\lambda}(t),v+\dot{\bf u}_{\lambda}(t))=\tilde{L}^{\ast}(\lambda,t,q,v)

because
{\bf u}_{\lambda}(\tau-t)={\bf u}_{\lambda}(t)
implies
-\dot{\bf u}_{\lambda}(\tau-t)=\dot{\bf u}_{\lambda}(t)
.

Now we have a family of
C^{2}
functionals

\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{E}_{\lambda}:EC^{1}(S_{\tau};B^{n}_{\iota}(0))\to{\mathbb{R}},\;x\mapsto\int^{\tau}_{0}\tilde{L}^{\ast}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))dt,\quad\lambda\in\Lambda.

(See the proof of the first claim in
[
20
, Proposition 4.2]
).
For
\xi\in EC^{1}(S_{\tau};B^{n}_{\iota}(0))
, since

\frac{d}{dt}\Phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(\xi+{\bf u}_{\lambda})(t)=(\phi_{\overline{\gamma}})^{\prime}_{1}(t,\xi(t)+{\bf u}_{\lambda}(t))+(\phi_{\overline{\gamma}})^{\prime}_{2}(t,\xi(t)+{\bf u}_{\lambda}(t))[\dot{\xi}(t)+\dot{\bf u}_{\lambda}(t)]\quad\forall t\in{\mathbb{R}},

we deduce

\tilde{\cal L}^{E}_{\lambda}(\xi)={\cal L}^{E}_{\lambda}\left(\Phi_{\overline{\gamma}}(\xi+{\bf u}_{\lambda})\right)\quad\forall\xi\in EC^{1}(S_{\tau};B^{n}_{\iota}(0))

(5.12)

and therefore that

m^{-}_{\tau}(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{E}_{\lambda},0)=m^{-}_{\tau}(\mathcal{L}^{E}_{\lambda},\gamma_{\lambda})\quad\hbox{and}\quad m^{0}_{\tau}(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{E}_{\lambda},0)=m^{0}_{\tau}(\mathcal{L}^{E}_{\lambda},\gamma_{\lambda}).

For the function
\tilde{L}^{\ast}
in (
5.11
), a positive number
\rho_{0}>0
and a subset
\hat{\Lambda}\subset\Lambda
which is either compact or sequential compact, as in Lemma
3.8
using Lemmas
2.4
,
2.7

we can construct a continuous function
\check{L}:\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathbb{R}\times B^{n}_{3\iota/4}(0)\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}}

satisfying the properties (L0)-(L6) in Lemma
3.8
on

\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathbb{R}\times B^{n}_{3\iota/4}(0)\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
and the following equality

\displaystyle\check{L}(\lambda,-t,q,-v)=\check{L}(\lambda,t,q,v)=\check{L}(\lambda,\tau+t,q,v)

for all
(\lambda,t,q,v)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathbb{R}\times B^{n}_{3\iota/4}(0)\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
.
Let us write

\displaystyle{\bf H}:=W^{1,2}(S_{\tau};{\mathbb{R}}^{n}),\quad{\bf X}:=C^{1}(S_{\tau};{\mathbb{R}}^{n}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}:=W^{1,2}(S_{\tau};B^{n}_{\iota/2}(0),\quad\mathcal{U}^{X}:=\mathcal{U}\cap{\bf X}=EC^{1}\big(S_{\tau};B^{n}_{\iota/2}(0)\big),

\displaystyle{\bf H}_{e}:=EW^{1,2}(S_{\tau};{\mathbb{R}}^{n})=\{x\in W^{1,2}(S_{\tau};{\mathbb{R}}^{n})\,|\,x(-t)=x(t)\;\forall t\},\quad{\bf X}_{e}:=EC^{1}(S_{\tau};{\mathbb{R}}^{n}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{e}:=\big\{u\in W^{1,2}\big(S_{\tau};B^{n}_{\iota/2}(0)\big)\,\big|\,u(-t)=u(t)\;\forall t\big\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}^{X}_{e}:=\mathcal{U}\cap{\bf X}=\big\{u\in C^{1}\big(S_{\tau};B^{n}_{\iota/2}(0)\big)\,\big|\,u(-t)=u(t)\;\forall t\big\}.

For each
\lambda\in\hat{\Lambda}
let us define functionals

\displaystyle\check{\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda}:\mathcal{U}\to{\mathbb{R}},\;x\mapsto\int^{\tau}_{0}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))dt\quad\hbox{and}\quad\check{\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda}^{E}:\mathcal{U}_{e}\to{\mathbb{R}},\;x\mapsto\int^{\tau}_{0}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))dt.

Clearly,
\check{\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda}|_{\mathcal{U}_{e}}=\check{\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda}^{E}
. As a special case,

\{(\check{\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda},\mathcal{U},\mathcal{U}^{X})\,|\,\lambda\in\hat{\Lambda}\}

has the same properties as
\{(\check{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda},\mathcal{U},\mathcal{U}^{X})\,|\,\lambda\in\hat{\Lambda}\}

in Section
4.2
.
By the arguments in
[
14
]
,
\{(\check{\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda}^{E},\mathcal{U}_{e},\mathcal{U}^{X}_{e})\,|\,\lambda\in\hat{\Lambda}\}

has also the same properties. (In fact, from the expression of
\nabla\check{\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda}
it is not hard to prove that

\nabla\check{\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda}(x)
is even for each
x\in\mathcal{U}_{e}
. Therefore

\nabla\check{\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda}^{E}(x)=\nabla\check{\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda}(x)
for each
x\in\mathcal{U}_{e}
.)
This implies that for each
x\in\mathcal{U}_{e}
the operator
B_{\lambda}(x)\in\mathscr{L}_{s}({\bf H})
defined by (
3.38
)
maps
{\bf H}_{e}
into
{\bf H}_{e}
.
Almost repeating the proofs in Section
3
we can obtain the required results.
Of course,
[
23
, §8]
also provides a way.

6
Corollaries and examples

In this section, we first derive some corollaries of the main results using the abstract framework in Appendix
B
, as these corollaries are more readily applicable. We then apply these results to physical Lagrangians—such as those involving potential and electromagnetic forces—and finally present bifurcation results for the planar forced simple pendulum.

6.1
Corollaries

Assumption 6.1
.

Let
(M,g,\mathbb{I}_{g})
be as in Assumption 1.0 in Introduction.
For a real
\tau>0
, consider a function
W\in C^{2}([0,\tau]\times M,\mathbb{R})
, and a
C^{2}
Lagrangian
\mathbf{L}(t,q,v)
on
[0,\tau]\times TM
that is
strictly convex with respect to
v
.
Let
{\bf N}
be either
{\rm Graph}(\mathbb{I}_{g})
or the product
S_{0}\times S_{1}
of the submanifolds

S_{0}
and
S_{1}
in Assumption
1.1
.
Assume that
\bar{\gamma}\in C^{2}([0,\tau];M)
satisfies
Lagrangian boundary value problem:

\left.\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{d}{dt}\big(\partial_{v}\mathbf{L}(t,\gamma(t),\dot{\gamma}(t))\big)-\partial_{q}\mathbf{L}(t,\gamma(t),\dot{\gamma}(t))=0\;\;\forall t\in[0,\tau],\\
(\gamma(0),\gamma(\tau))\in{\bf N}\quad\hbox{and}\\
\partial_{v}\mathbf{L}(0,\gamma(0),\dot{\gamma}(0))[v_{0}]=\partial_{v}\mathbf{L}(\tau,\gamma(\tau),\dot{\gamma}(\tau))[v_{1}]\;\;\forall(v_{0},v_{1})\in T_{(\gamma(0),\gamma(\tau))}{\bf N}\end{array}\right\}

Assume that for all
t
,
\partial_{q}W(t,\bar{\gamma}(t))=0
,
and that the Hessian of each
W(t,\cdot)
at the critical point
\bar{\gamma}(t)

is either positive definite or negative definite.

Under Assumption
6.1
, for
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
and
(t,q,v)\in[0,\tau]\times TM
, we define

L(\lambda,t,q,v)=L_{\lambda}(t,q,v)=\mathbf{L}(t,q,v)+\lambda W(t,q).

(6.1)

It is easy to check that
L
satisfies Assumptions
1.1
,
1.11
.
With this
L
, let
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}
and
\mathscr{E}_{\lambda}
be given by
(
1.11
) and (
1.26
), respectively. Then

\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}=\mathcal{E}_{0}+\lambda\mathcal{W}
and

\mathscr{E}_{\lambda}=\mathscr{E}_{0}+\lambda\mathscr{W}
, where

\mathcal{E}_{0}(\gamma)=\int^{\tau}_{0}L_{\lambda}(t,\gamma(t),\dot{\gamma}(t))dt\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{W}=\int^{\tau}_{0}W(t,\gamma(t))dt

for
\gamma\in C^{1}_{S_{0}\times S_{1}}([0,\tau];M)
, and

\mathscr{E}_{0}(\gamma)=\int^{\tau}_{0}L_{\lambda}(t,\gamma(t),\dot{\gamma}(t))dt\quad\text{and}\quad\mathscr{W}=\int^{\tau}_{0}W(t,\gamma(t))dt

for
\gamma\in C^{1}_{\mathbb{I}_{g}}([0,\tau];M)
.
When
\mathbf{N}
is equal to
S_{0}\times S_{1}
(resp.
\mathrm{Graph}(\mathbb{I}_{g})
),
\bar{\gamma}
is a critical point of
\mathcal{E}_{0}
and
\mathcal{W}
(resp.
\mathscr{E}_{0}
and
\mathscr{W}
), and hence of
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}
(resp.
\mathscr{E}_{\lambda}
), for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
, because of Assumption
6.1
.
A standard computation shows that the second differentials of

\mathcal{W}
and
\mathscr{W}
at
\bar{\gamma}
are, respectively,

\displaystyle D^{2}\mathcal{W}(\bar{\gamma})[\xi,\xi]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\tau}\langle\mathrm{Hess}W(t,\bar{\gamma})\xi(t),\xi(t)\rangle_{g}dt,\quad\xi\in W^{1,2}_{S_{0}\times S_{1}}(\bar{\gamma}^{\ast}TM),

\displaystyle D^{2}\mathscr{W}(\bar{\gamma})[\xi,\xi]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\tau}\langle\mathrm{Hess}W(t,\bar{\gamma})\xi(t),\xi(t)\rangle_{g}dt,\quad\xi\in W^{1,2}_{\mathbb{I}_{g}}(\bar{\gamma}^{\ast}TM).

Therefore, by the standard methods in functional analysis it is easily proved that

D^{2}\mathcal{W}(\bar{\gamma})
and
D^{2}\mathscr{W}(\bar{\gamma})

are given by self-adjoint and compact operators

W^{1,2}_{S_{0}\times S_{1}}(\bar{\gamma}^{\ast}TM)
and

W^{1,2}_{\mathbb{I}_{g}}(\bar{\gamma}^{\ast}TM)
, respectively.
Moreover, since
\bar{\gamma}([0,\tau])\subset M
is compact,
by modifying
L
outside some compact neighborhood of
\bar{\gamma}([0,\tau])\subset M
as done in this paper (if necessary), a localized argument shows
that both
D^{2}\mathcal{E}_{0}(\bar{\gamma})
and
D^{2}\mathscr{E}_{0}(\bar{\gamma})

are self-adjoint Fredholm and have finite Morse indexes (cf.
[
2
, Proposition 3.1(iii)]

and
[
6
]
).
(Recall that a continuous symmetric bilinear form on a Hilbert space
H
is said to Fredholm if
the associated self-adjoint operator is Fredholm.)
Hence, for any
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
,
D^{2}\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}(\bar{\gamma})
and
D^{2}\mathscr{E}_{\lambda}(\bar{\gamma})

are also self-adjoint Fredholm and have also finite Morse indexes.
By Proposition
B.1
we immediately obtain:

Proposition 6.2
.

Under Assumption
6.1
, suppose that
\mathbf{N}=S_{0}\times S_{1}

and
m^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{0},\bar{\gamma})>0
. Then
there exists
0<\delta_{0}<1
such that the following hold:

(i)

If the Hessian of each
W(t,\cdot)
at the critical point
\bar{\gamma}(t)

is positive definite, then

m^{-}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda},\bar{\gamma})=\begin{cases}m^{-}(\mathcal{E}_{0},\bar{\gamma}),&0<\lambda<\delta_{0},\\[4.0pt]
m^{-}(\mathcal{E}_{0},\bar{\gamma})+m^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{0},\bar{\gamma}),&-\delta_{0}<\lambda<0.\end{cases}

(ii)

If the Hessian of each
W(t,\cdot)
at the critical point
\bar{\gamma}(t)

is negative definite, then

m^{-}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda},\bar{\gamma})=\begin{cases}m^{-}(\mathcal{E}_{0},\bar{\gamma})+m^{0}(\mathfrak{E}_{0},\bar{\gamma}),&0<\lambda<\delta_{0},\\[4.0pt]
m^{-}(\mathcal{E}_{0},\bar{\gamma}),&-\delta_{0}<\lambda<0.\end{cases}

Similarly, if
\mathbf{N}=\mathrm{Graph}(\mathbb{I}_{g})
and
m^{0}(\mathscr{E}_{0},\bar{\gamma})>0
, then
(i) and (ii) hold with
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}
and
\mathcal{E}_{0}
replaced by
\mathscr{E}_{\lambda}
and
\mathscr{E}_{0}
, respectively.

Assumption 6.3
.

Let
(M,g,\mathbb{I}_{g})
be as in Assumption 1.0 in Introduction.
Consider a function
W\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R}\times M,\mathbb{R})
, and a
C^{2}
Lagrangian
\mathbf{L}(t,q,v)
on
\mathbb{R}\times TM
that is
strictly convex with respect to
v
. For some
\tau>0
, we assume that both
W
and

\mathbf{L}
are also
\mathbb{I}_{g}
-invariant in the
sense of (
1.28
), i.e., for all
(t,q,v)\in\mathbb{R}\times TM
,

W(t,q)=W(t+\tau,\mathbb{I}_{g}(q)),\quad\mathbf{L}(t+\tau,\mathbb{I}_{g}(q),d\mathbb{I}_{g}(q)[v])=\mathbf{L}(t,q,v).

Let
\bar{\gamma}\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R},M)
satisfy the problem

\left.\begin{array}[]{ll}&\frac{d}{dt}\big(\partial_{v}\mathbf{L}(t,\gamma(t),\dot{\gamma}(t))\big)-\partial_{q}\mathbf{L}(t,\gamma(t),\dot{\gamma}(t))=0\;\;\forall t\in\mathbb{R},\\
&\mathbb{I}_{g}(\gamma(t))=\gamma(t+\tau)\quad\forall t\in\mathbb{R}.\end{array}\right\}

Assume that for all
t
,
\partial_{q}W(t,\bar{\gamma}(t))=0
,
and that the Hessian of each
W(t,\cdot)
at the critical point
\bar{\gamma}(t)

is either positive definite or negative definite.

Let
L(\lambda,t,q,v)=L_{\lambda}(t,q,v)=\mathbf{L}(t,q,v)+\lambda W(t,q)

for
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
and
(t,q,v)\in\mathbb{R}\times TM
.
Then
L
satisfies Assumption
1.16
, and

\bar{\gamma}
solves (
1.29
) with this
L
, i.e.,
\bar{\gamma}
is a critical
point of each
C^{2}
functional

\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda}:=\mathfrak{E}_{0}+\lambda\mathfrak{W}

on the
C^{4}
Banach manifold
\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g})
, where

\mathfrak{E}_{0}(\gamma)=\int_{0}^{\tau}\mathbf{L}(t,\gamma(t),\dot{\gamma}(t))\,dt\quad\text{and}\quad\mathfrak{W}(\gamma)=\int_{0}^{\tau}W(t,\gamma(t))\,dt.

Note that
the tangent space
T_{\gamma}\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g})=\{\xi\in C^{1}(\gamma^{\ast}TM\,|\,d\mathbb{I}_{g}(\gamma(t))[\xi(t)]=\xi(t+\tau)\}

for
\gamma\in\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g})
.
Let
W^{1,2}\left(T_{\gamma}\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g})\right)

denote the completion of
T_{\gamma}\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g})
with respect to
the inner product given by (
1.10
).
Since
\partial_{vv}\mathbf{L}(t,\bar{\gamma}(t),\dot{\gamma}(t))
is positive definite,
we have a new Riemannian metric on the vector bundle
\bar{\gamma}^{\ast}TM\to\mathbb{R}
given by

\langle u,v\rangle^{\ast}=\langle\partial_{vv}\mathbf{L}(t,\bar{\gamma}(t),\dot{\gamma}(t))u,v\rangle_{g},\quad\forall u,v\in(\bar{\gamma}^{\ast}TM)_{t}=T_{\bar{\gamma}(t)}M,

By
[
5
, Assertion 4.6]
(with the same proof as
[
3
, Proposition 5.1]
),
there exists is a covariant derivative
D
compatible with the metric such that

\langle\!\langle\xi,\eta\rangle\!\rangle=\int^{\tau}_{0}[\langle D\xi(t),D\eta(t)\rangle^{\ast}+\langle\xi(t),\eta(t)\rangle^{\ast}]dt

defines a new inner product on

W^{1,2}\left(T_{\gamma}\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g})\right)

that induces a norm equivalent to the one induced by the original inner product
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{1,2}
. As pointed out in
[
5
, Remark 4.14]
,
under this new inner product,
D^{2}\mathfrak{E}_{0}(\bar{\gamma})

is self-adjoint Fredholm and has finite Morse index. (See
[
3
]
and
[
17
]
